The split guard's own summary comment states that it "validates evidence already computed by a caller; it does not calculate hashes, near-duplicate scores, accuracy, training state, or promotion decisions" (DynamicGraphHeldOutSplitGuard.cs:11-13). The novelty meter's own summary comment states that it "does not select rows, calculate pattern or token rates, run training" (DynamicGraphNoveltyMeter.cs:11-13). The novelty observation's own doc-comment says it "carries already-produced held-out selection evidence" (DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:64-67). The one place that does the arithmetic (DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityNoveltyEvaluation.cs:492-497) computes its rates from caller-attested pattern and token fingerprints and from supplied learned/baseline selection accuracies, not from a model's own inference. So the contracts are a real, exercised measurement-admission layer, but the "held-out score" they carry is a re-validated caller-supplied number, not a metric emitted by a persisted trained model.

Is any measurement bound to a persisted trained artifact? No (code fact). The only binding the code performs is measurement to target identity: the coordinator pairs a measured value with a DynamicGraphMeasurementTarget (a record that carries only an id, a level, a mechanism kind, and a boolean; its own comment says it "carries identity and classification only; it is not a lifecycle verdict", DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:34-37) and returns it transiently inside an OperationResult, with no persistence. Independently of Task 3, I searched the whole repository for the two carriers the plan names — GraphCheckpointManifest and GraphLearningExport — and found zero occurrences in any .cs file; those carriers do not exist in the code. The only real consumer that persists anything is the live-local HTTP tool: DynamicGraphSelectedTargetEvidenceInvoker.Invoke calls the coordinator at DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1739, and the measurement it returns is held in an in-memory, per-session evidence buffer of capacity 16 and handed back over HTTP by an opaque handle (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:722-791). That tool does write a file it internally calls a "checkpoint", but the persisted record PersistedCompositionSession (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionPersistence.cs:1100-1109, schema "dynamic-graph-composition-session.v1") stores only the session id, tenant, timestamps, the bootstrap profile, and the composition topology evidence — it does not store any measurement record. So a document (the plan) claims the measurements must be bound to GraphCheckpointManifest and GraphLearningExport, whereas the code binds a measurement only to a transient logical target identity and never to any persisted trained artifact.

What the plan requires (document claim). The plan file active-codex-execution-plan-11-measurement.md:22 states that measurement "must bind held-out scorer results, deterministic-baseline ablation, source-contamination rebuild status, and promotion/rollback evidence to GraphCheckpointManifest and GraphLearningExport, not just to training logs", and :24 requires every metric row to carry a SourceHandleId (an exact source-map handle so a future reviewer can trace repo root, relative path, role, owner, status, and verification date). The block title at :32 reads "Блок плана 11 — Измерение: как доказать ЧИСЛОМ, что узел/ребро графа реально обучился" (Russian for "Plan block 11 — Measurement: how to prove with a NUMBER that a graph node/edge really learned"). That same plan file marks these lines as historical, evidence-only propagation, not current execution authority. The code delivers the measurement record types plus the admission-and-validation meters, but it does not deliver the plan's binding-to-carrier clause.

**Verdict:** R-11 measurement = PARTIAL — the measurement contracts are LIVE, not declaration-only (26 of 26 records are constructed in production code plus both enums are used there, each with a real producer meter and a real consumer; the only exception is the run-speed observation input, which is produced solely by tests), BUT no measurement is bound to any persisted trained artifact — the plan-named carriers GraphCheckpointManifest and GraphLearningExport are absent from the entire codebase, measurements bind only to a transient in-memory logical target identity and are handed back by an HTTP handle, and the numbers they carry are re-validated caller-attested evidence rather than metrics emitted from a persisted trained model.

### Finding 10 — Test coverage against the 14x9 matrix (Cycle 1, Task 10)

**What the plan requires (document claim).** Requirement R-13 is the consolidated test strategy. The plan file active-codex-execution-plan-13-test-strategy.md:37 lays out a "14x9 matrix": fourteen plan-block rows (named P-01 through P-14) crossed with nine proof kinds (unit, integration, e2e/host, QA-automation, UX-automation, negative, untrained-behavior, trained-acceptance, regression). The plan demands zero empty required cells and, for the three trainable rows it names (P-01 node, P-02 edge, P-04 machine), TWO separate proof columns: an untrained-honesty column the plan calls PASS_AS_HONEST_UNTRAINED_SCAFFOLD and a trained-acceptance column it calls PASS_AS_TRAINED_FEATURE. In plain words, "untrained state" means the code honestly reports it has learned nothing yet, and "trained state" means a real learned checkpoint produces a measurable gain. The plan's own numbering note at :423 says block 05 (hierarchy) has no separate product row, so the fourteen matrix rows cover thirteen code blocks like this: P-01 = block 01 node, P-02 = block 02 edge, P-03 = block 03 orchestrator, P-04 = block 04 machine, P-05 and P-06 = block 06 async-combinations, P-07 = block 07 dpo-builder, P-08 = block 08 legal-gate, P-09 = block 09 auto-dpo-loop, P-10 = block 10 lifecycle, P-11 = block 11 measurement, P-12 = block 12 hidden-determinism, and P-13 = block 13 test-strategy (a meta row that is supposed to check the matrix against itself).

**What the code actually contains (code fact).** I ran no test. I inventoried the Tests\ folder by reading file names and grepping file contents on branch DpoImplementation at HEAD 0f221ac89. The folder holds 316 test .cs files; 66 of them carry graph names (DynamicGraph / Logical / EdgeAuthority / Combination), and all 66 sit directly in the Tests\ root. Mapping those files to the plan blocks gives the table below. In the table "Yes" means at least one real test class targets that block's own contracts, and "None" means a content grep for that block's own contract names returned zero test files.

| Plan block (matrix row) | Tests exist? | Representative test files / grep evidence (code fact) |
|---|---|---|
| 01 node (P-01) | Yes | InMemoryDynamicGraphNodeRegistryTests.cs, LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNodeTests.cs, DynamicGraphUntrainedHonestyL2ProbeTests.cs, DynamicGraphUntrainedHonestyMeterTests.cs |
| 02 edge (P-02) | Yes (11 files) | DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityControlMeterTests.cs, DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityAblationEvaluationTests.cs, DynamicGraphEdgeCandidateOrderLeakageGuardUnitTests.cs, RegistryDynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityResolverTests.cs |
| 03 orchestrator (P-03) | Yes (9 files) | DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorRuntimeTests.cs, DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestratorTests.cs, LogicalOrchestratorTests.cs |
| 04 machine (P-04) | Yes (untrained only) | DynamicGraphMachineHonestUntrainedTests.cs, DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactoryTests.cs |
| 05 hierarchy (no P-row) | Yes (4 files) | PromptToTaskLogicalHierarchyTests.cs, SkillSelectionLogicalHierarchyTests.cs, ResultGapLogicalHierarchyTests.cs, OrchestrationReasoningLogicalHierarchyTests.cs |
| 06 async-combinations (P-05/P-06) | Yes (4 files) | BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs, SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs, AxisSweepCombinationStrategyTests.cs, DynamicGraphFanInMergerTests.cs |
| 07 dpo-builder (P-07) | None | grep of Tests\ for GraphDpoPair and GraphDpoBuilder = 0 files (generic DpoDatasetTests.cs / DpoTrainerTests.cs exist, but they are not the graph unit-pair builder the plan names) |
| 08 legal-gate (P-08) | None | grep of Tests\ for TeacherProvenance, provenance_field_presence and DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED = 0 files each |
| 09 auto-dpo-loop (P-09) | None | no graph auto-loop test exists; the loop cannot even run because graph training is deliberately not activated (see below) |
| 10 lifecycle (P-10) | Partial (untrained only) | only DynamicGraphMachineHonestUntrainedTests.cs, which asserts Export/Import return *_NOT_ACTIVATED; no real LoRA/checkpoint export-import round-trip on a trained state |
| 11 measurement (P-11) | Yes (8+ files) | DynamicGraphAccuracyMeterTests.cs, DynamicGraphAblationMeterTests.cs, DynamicGraphSpeedMeterTests.cs, DynamicGraphMeasurementCoordinatorTests.cs, DynamicGraphHeldOutSplitGuardTests.cs |
| 12 hidden-determinism (P-12) | Partial | DynamicGraphEdgeCandidateOrderLeakageGuardUnitTests.cs tests permutation/order-invariance; but grep of Tests\ for SemanticEdgeRouterUnit and for a 9-mechanism classification count (classified_mechanism / MechanismCount) = 0 files each |
| 13 test-strategy (P-13, meta) | None | grep of the WHOLE repo (source plus tests) for TestStrategyMatrix = 0 files; the matrix self-check lives only in the plan document, not in code |

**Are all graph tests untrained-state? Yes (code fact).** The top-level graph learning class cannot train. DynamicGraphMachine.cs:21 carries the comment "L5 ownership scaffold. Training lifecycle methods deliberately fail until a real trained path exists.", it sets RuntimeMode = "honest_untrained" (:25) and SemanticQualityClaimed = false (:26), and its four lifecycle methods each return a hard failure: TrainBatchAsync returns Fail(..., "DYNAMIC_GRAPH_TRAINING_NOT_ACTIVATED") (:45-51), and EvaluateAsync / ExportAsync / ImportAsync return the matching *_NOT_ACTIVATED codes (:55-79). Because no code path can produce a trained checkpoint, no test can assert against one. Even the measurement tests that superficially look like trained-state proofs (accuracy deltas, reward margins) feed the meters hand-written numbers rather than outputs of a real trained model — for example DynamicGraphAccuracyMeterTests.cs:47-58 passes the literals 0.60 and 0.96 into the meter and checks that it computes the 0.36 delta. So every existing graph test is necessarily an untrained-state test, and the trained-acceptance column of the 14x9 matrix has zero real behavioral proof anywhere in this repository.

**Do the sampled tests assert real behavior or only a success flag? Real behavior (code fact).** I read three representative graph test classes in assertion detail:
1. DynamicGraphMachineHonestUntrainedTests.cs asserts specific error codes and exact side-effect counts, not a bare success flag. Its helper at :369-375 checks IsSuccess == false plus the exact ErrorCode and the exact ErrorMessage; the tests assert the precise strings "DYNAMIC_GRAPH_TRAINING_NOT_ACTIVATED", "..._EVALUATION_NOT_ACTIVATED" and so on (:49-52), that the orchestrator factory was called zero times on every failing path (:53, :73, :97), and by reference-equality that GetOrchestratorAsync forwards the exact factory result exactly once (:115-117).
2. BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs asserts real concurrency and ordering, not just success. It checks that observed maximum concurrency equals the budget of 2 (:95), that execute count equals 4 (:94), that the four candidates return in plan order combination-0 through combination-3 even though they finished out of order (:80-93), and that each per-plan budget field equals 1 (:100-101).
3. DynamicGraphAccuracyMeterTests.cs asserts computed numbers, not success. It checks the locked Q30 thresholds 0.95 / 0.02 / 0.025 (:39-41), the after-minus-before accuracy delta of 0.36 (:58) and of +0.02 / -0.02 in both directions (:105-107), the held-out reward margin of 0.026 (:59), and that live accuracy cannot be substituted for held-out accuracy (:110-115).

None of the three is a stub or a success-only test; all three satisfy test-integrity Rule 6 because they assert domain values (specific error codes, concurrency counts, computed rates), not result.success alone. The one honest caveat is that these are untrained-scaffold proofs — real assertions on not-activated behavior and on measurement arithmetic — and not trained-feature proofs.

**Verdict:** R-13 test strategy = PARTIAL — real behavioral untrained-state tests exist for 7 of the 13 plan blocks (01 node, 02 edge, 03 orchestrator, 04 machine, 05 hierarchy, 06 async-combinations, 11 measurement), and the three sampled graph tests assert real values (specific *_NOT_ACTIVATED error codes, concurrency counts of 2 and 4, computed accuracy deltas and a 0.026 reward margin) rather than a success flag; but because DynamicGraphMachine deliberately never trains, every graph test is necessarily an untrained-state test and the entire trained-acceptance column of the 14x9 matrix is unproven, blocks 07 dpo-builder, 08 legal-gate, 09 auto-dpo-loop and 13 test-strategy-meta have no test coverage at all (and no Teacher-named test exists anywhere, confirming the block 16 teacher route is untested too), blocks 10 lifecycle and 12 hidden-determinism are only partially covered, and the 14x9 matrix mechanism itself (the plan's TestStrategyMatrix) exists only in the plan document and not in code.


### Finding 11 — Branch convention and dual-path proof (Cycle 1, Task 11)

**Scope in plain words.** This task checks two housekeeping requirements against the one worktree being audited, the on-disk folder C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation, which is a working copy of the "core" repository named llm_mvp_core that this effort builds its DPO work inside. "DPO" means Direct Preference Optimization, the training method the project wants to feed with a user's "this answer is better than that one" choices. R-14 asks whether the folder and branch follow the plan's naming rule. R-15 asks whether any proof exists that a scenario passed BOTH on an automated server call (called Path A) AND on a real screen a person uses (called Path B) at the same time, recorded in a "join" file.

**R-14 — the code facts (what the git commands actually reported).** The command `git -C C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation branch --show-current` printed `DpoImplementation`, so the active branch is named exactly `DpoImplementation`. The command `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` printed `C:/Projects/llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation`, so the folder path is exactly the one the plan wants. The three newest commits (`git log --oneline -3`) are `0f221ac89 Report selected-target handles as ephemeral`, then `0483fde5d Expose one-shot selected-target execution over loopback API`, then `3a20b653a Execute admitted selected targets from evidence handles`; the tip commit (HEAD) is `0f221ac89`, which matches the value this task expected, and `git status --porcelain` returned nothing, so the working tree is clean and I left it clean. The branch `DesignToCode-clean` exists in this folder (its tip commit is `3d08d2a6d1a3f13cb4bfabff5768dc9c43cf3527`). The lineage command `git merge-base --is-ancestor DesignToCode-clean HEAD` succeeded, meaning DesignToCode-clean's tip is an ancestor of the current HEAD; `git merge-base DesignToCode-clean HEAD` returned that same commit `3d08d2a6d...`, meaning DesignToCode-clean's tip is exactly the common starting point of the branch; and `git rev-list --count DesignToCode-clean..HEAD` returned `2681`, meaning the current branch is 2681 commits of work built on top of that starting point. That is precisely the shape produced by "create a new branch at DesignToCode-clean and keep committing forward."

**R-14 — the document claim (what the plan requires).** The plan file active-codex-execution-plan-14-stage-references.md states at line 6 that each repository's implementation branch must be named `DpoImplementation` (and it explicitly says NOT a branch named `DPOLegalLearning`), at line 7 that the working folder must be a git worktree at `C:\Projects\<repo-name>-DpoImplementation` with `DpoImplementation` as that folder's HEAD, and at line 9 that for the llm_mvp_core repository the source starting branch is `DesignToCode-clean`. Each of those three written requirements matches the git facts above one-for-one.

**R-15 — the code facts (what the repository actually contains).** The plan requires a "join" file whose field `sameScenarioEquality.matched` is set to `true` before a scenario counts as proven on both paths. Searching for that exact field name found it in ZERO files: `git grep -l sameScenarioEquality` returned 0 tracked files, a ripgrep over every non-ignored file returned "No files found", and a raw `grep -r` over the two large gitignored output folders `artifacts\` and `experiments-v2\` also returned nothing (both of those bounded greps finished normally, not by timeout). So the plan's required join-file field does not exist anywhere in this repository.

There ARE dual-path artifacts in this repository, but they belong to a DIFFERENT scenario. Under docs\plans\evidence\extension-full-readiness-dual-path\ there are Path-A / Path-B join reviews for the Cursor IDE extension's "Smart Skills" feature, whose scenario id is `frdp-p01-cursor-token-savings-baseline` and whose marker string is `FRDP-P01-ROW12-CURSOR-TOKEN-SAVINGS-BASELINE-V1` (this is about a code editor plugin saving tokens, not about the graph DPO learning path). The strongest of these, the file cursor-row12-full-dual-path-join-review-20260628T190856Z\review.ru.md, reaches verdict `PASS_NARROW_CURSOR_ROW12_DUAL_PATH` at its line 12 and joins Path A to Path B by matching the fields `marker`, `scenario_id`, and `pair_id` (its match table at lines 26 to 30) together with `submitted_to_core=true` (line 83). That is a different join schema from the plan's `sameScenarioEquality` tuple, and the document downgrades itself in plain words at line 22: "Это не product readiness" (this is not product readiness). The structured companion file cursor-path-a-b-join-review-20260628T185039Z\join-review.json carries only `scenario_id: frdp-p01-cursor-token-savings-baseline` and a `pair_id`; it contains no `sameScenarioEquality` field and no `matched` boolean at all. The only other `matched=true` text in the docs tree is docs\skills\phase-completion-packager-SKILL.md line 849, which reads `work_order_scope_matched=true`, an unrelated field inside a skill template rather than a piece of evidence.

For the dynamic-graph DPO scenario specifically (the plan names it SCN-R17-BETTER-OUTPUT-DPO and ties it to FLOW-38, "the choice of a better answer turns into a DPO event"), there is NO evidence artifact here at all: grepping the evidence folders for `SCN-R17`, for `BETTER-OUTPUT-DPO`, and for `GraphDpoSourceRow` returned nothing. Those names appear only inside a plan/design document, docs\plans\claude-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-DPO-PLAN-v1.md, which is a claim written on paper, not a record of a scenario that passed.

**R-15 — the document claim and the explicit out-of-scope caveat.** The plan file active-codex-execution-plan-15-same-scenario-dual-path-proof-design.md states at line 64 that scenario coverage is "accepted only when Path A and Path B both pass and the join file reports `sameScenarioEquality.matched=true` for the same tuple", and at lines 403 to 422 it gives the required join-file shape carrying that exact `sameScenarioEquality.matched` field. Importantly, the same plan places Path B (the real host user-interface path) OUTSIDE this repository: at line 229 the participating repositories for the graph DPO scenario are listed as `xiigen-platform-dot-net-angular`, `xiigen-flows-dot-net-angular`, llm_mvp_core, and thin Smart* client callers, and at lines 311 to 312 the Path B target is described as "the Xiigen platform/client host feedback UI", possibly a named Smart* host window — none of which is this llm_mvp_core repository. Because of that, the absence of a graph-DPO `sameScenarioEquality` join HERE does not prove such a proof is absent everywhere; it proves only that this single repository does not contain one. A full "does it exist anywhere" answer would require auditing those separate host repositories, which is outside the scope of this single-repo task, and I state that limitation explicitly rather than overclaim.

**Verdict:** R-14 branch convention = IMPLEMENTED — the audited worktree matches the plan exactly: the branch is named `DpoImplementation`, the folder is `C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation`, HEAD is `0f221ac89` with a clean tree, and the lineage from `DesignToCode-clean` (tip `3d08d2a6d`) is proven as the merge-base with 2681 commits built on top; R-15 dual-path proof = MISSING in this repository for the dynamic-graph DPO scenario — the plan's required `sameScenarioEquality` join field exists in zero files, the only real dual-path join present belongs to a different flow (the Cursor extension token-savings scenario, which uses a different match schema and downgrades itself to "not product readiness"), and no SCN-R17 / FLOW-38 graph-DPO evidence artifact exists here, with the explicit caveat that Path B for this scenario is defined in host repositories outside this single-repo audit, so its absence here is not proof of its absence everywhere.

_Cycle 1 (ground truth) complete: Findings 1–11, requirements R-01…R-17 mapped._

### Finding 12 - The trained-passport loophole: honesty by string, not by artifact (Cycle 2, Task 1)

Plain setup. A "passport" here means the ten-field record DynamicGraphNodeDescriptor, defined at 00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:31-41, that every graph node carries. Two of its fields are honesty flags: IsTrained (the node claims it is backed by a real validated trained checkpoint) and RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed (the node claims "my outputs are genuinely learned, not a placeholder"). Two more fields are text pointers: CheckpointRef (a pointer to a saved trained result) and ManifestRef (a pointer to that result's description).

The code fact about the central validator. The validator method DynamicGraphValidation.Validate at 00.Core\DynamicGraphValidation.cs:104-125 enforces four string-level rules and nothing more: an untrained node cannot claim quality (lines 104-105); a quality claim requires the exact authority-role string "trained_node" (lines 107-112); IsTrained must equal the fact "the role is trained_node" (lines 114-117); and if IsTrained is true then CheckpointRef and ManifestRef must each be a non-blank single-line string (lines 120-125). The helper that judges those two pointers, IsCanonicalSingleLineValue at 00.Core\DynamicGraphValidation.cs:408-413, only checks that the string is non-empty, already trimmed, and free of control characters. It never opens a file, never checks that a path exists, never reads the referenced checkpoint. So the validator accepts a fabricated trained passport whose CheckpointRef and ManifestRef are any arbitrary non-blank one-line text (for example the single letter "x").

Question (a): does any production code dereference CheckpointRef or ManifestRef to verify a real artifact exists on disk? Code fact: no. I grepped the whole repository for both field names. Every production consumer of the graph passport's CheckpointRef/ManifestRef only inspects the string, never the disk. The validator string-checks it (00.Core\DynamicGraphValidation.cs:120-125). The deterministic topology builder compares two descriptors for ordinal string equality (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DeterministicDynamicGraphTopologyBuilder.cs:308-309). The in-library machine composition factory's honesty guard checks the pointer is null (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactory.cs:265-266). The loopback host policy checks the pointer is not-null purely in order to reject the profile (tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:137-138). The only code in the repository that actually opens or hashes a checkpoint file - File.Exists(...), HashFile(...), File.ReadAllText(...) - lives either under Tests\ (for example Tests\SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:86-92 and Tests\OrchestrationReasoningMachineAssetLifecycleTests.cs:33-34) or in the separate model-server and offline-runner training subsystem (25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\* and tools\OrchestrationReasoningPhase05Runner\Program.cs:431). Those operate on a different CheckpointRef/ManifestRef - the model-export / LearningMachine reference described at 00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:115-127 that points into 25.ModelServer - and not on the graph-node passport at all. So for the graph passport specifically, zero production code existence-checks the checkpoint.

Question (b): is there any production path that could introduce a fabricated trained passport into a running topology and let candidates carry SemanticQualityClaimed=true? Code fact: no reachable one. First, the per-candidate honesty flag SemanticQualityClaimed (the last field of GraphNodeCandidate, defined at 00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:67) is fenced by the runtime guard at 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNode.cs:405-410: a candidate may set that flag true only when its own node descriptor already has RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed=true. So the whole question reduces to whether a descriptor with IsTrained/RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed=true can ever enter production. Code fact: a fabricated trained descriptor is never constructed in production. I enumerated every literal construction "new DynamicGraphNodeDescriptor(...)" in the repository - there are fourteen files, and every one of them is under Tests\ (for example Tests\DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactoryTests.cs:452 and Tests\LogicalRegistryDynamicGraphProjectorTests.cs:667). No shipping (non-test) source file constructs one. Every production reference to the record either declares it (00.Core, 19.DynamicGraphComposition.Contracts), copies an existing instance unchanged with "with { }" (for example the topology snapshot at 00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:493, the registry projector at 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\LogicalRegistryDynamicGraphProjector.cs:190, and the node adapter at 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNode.cs:74), or only reads its boolean fields. Because production never hand-builds a trained descriptor, the only way one could appear is by deserializing it from outside - and both such entry points are fenced. The loopback local host (tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer) rejects any request carrying a trained input outright, returning the error HOST_TRAINED_NODE_INPUT_FORBIDDEN at Program.cs:198-199, and its profile-admission policy method ContainsForbiddenAuthority at DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:131-142 rejects the whole profile if any node descriptor has IsTrained=true, RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed=true, a non-null CheckpointRef, a non-null ManifestRef, or an authority-role string that merely CONTAINS the substring "trained" case-insensitively - a stricter net than the validator's exact "trained_node" match. Independently, the in-library machine composition path applies its own guard IsHonestUntrained at 15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactory.cs:257-266, and if any node is not honestly untrained (not trained, not deterministic-final-authority, not quality-claiming, role exactly "baseline_candidate_generator", both pointers null) it returns TrainedStateForbidden and refuses to compose the topology (DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactory.cs:236-254). So every production entry point that could receive a descriptor either rejects the trained claim or is never reached by a constructor that makes one.

The document claim, kept separate. The plan file states at active-codex-execution-plan-01-node.md:722 that the descriptor field RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed must be false while there is no trained-evidence, and at active-codex-execution-plan-01-node.md:753 that the per-candidate SemanticQualityClaimed is an honest status carried on each candidate. The companion machine plan states at active-codex-execution-plan-04-dynamicgraph-machine.md:518 that when no checkpoint is present the orchestrator must run on a deterministic baseline with SemanticQualityClaimed=false, and at active-codex-execution-plan-04-dynamicgraph-machine.md:529 that the no-checkpoint branch must yield an honest-untrained mode with no claim to semantic quality. These are documents describing the intended honesty rule; the code facts above are what the shipped validator and guards actually enforce.

The honest risk statement. The one true weakness is real but narrow. The central validator's trained-passport check is satisfied by string shape alone: a passport with IsTrained=true and any non-blank CheckpointRef/ManifestRef passes DynamicGraphValidation without the referenced checkpoint existing anywhere (00.Core\DynamicGraphValidation.cs:120-125 together with IsCanonicalSingleLineValue at 408-413). That means the validator, taken in isolation, does not prove trained honesty - it proves only that the trained-claim fields are internally consistent and non-blank. However, that isolated weakness is not reachable as a production overclaim, for two independent reasons: (1) no production code constructs a trained descriptor at all (every "new DynamicGraphNodeDescriptor" is under Tests\), and (2) both production entry points that could accept an externally supplied descriptor - the loopback host and the machine composition factory - reject any trained, quality-claiming, or checkpoint-bearing node before it can run, and the per-candidate flag is additionally gated on the descriptor. The exposure is therefore latent, not live: if a future change ever adds a production constructor of a trained descriptor, or a new host that deserializes profiles without the ContainsForbiddenAuthority / IsHonestUntrained fence, the string-only validator would let the overclaim through undetected.

**Verdict:** trained-passport honesty = FENCED / NOT A REACHABLE PRODUCTION OVERCLAIM - (1) no production code existence-checks the checkpoint artifact: the validator only string-checks CheckpointRef/ManifestRef (DynamicGraphValidation.cs:120-125 with IsCanonicalSingleLineValue at 408-413), so in isolation it does not prove trained honesty; but (2) no production path can introduce a trained or quality-claiming node, because every "new DynamicGraphNodeDescriptor" is under Tests\, the loopback host rejects trained input (HOST_TRAINED_NODE_INPUT_FORBIDDEN at Program.cs:198-199 and ContainsForbiddenAuthority at DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:131-142), the machine composition factory rejects any non-untrained node (IsHonestUntrained / TrainedStateForbidden at DynamicGraphMachineCompositionFactory.cs:257-266), and the per-candidate SemanticQualityClaimed flag is itself gated on the descriptor (LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNode.cs:405-410); (3) the string-only validator is a latent weakness that would become exploitable only if a future production constructor of a trained descriptor, or an unfenced deserializing host, were added.


### Finding 13 — The selected-target loopback server: what it is and in/out of plan (Cycle 2, Task 2)

Severity of this finding: INFORMATIONAL (governance / classification). It identifies an out-of-plan feature but no live breach of the three safety invariants.

**What was audited, and read-only proof.** The last 13 commits on branch DpoImplementation (from d38a127ad exclusive, up to HEAD 0f221ac89) added a small local web server under tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\. Verified read-only before and after: `git rev-parse HEAD` returned 0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45 and `git status --porcelain` was empty; no file in the audited repo was edited or committed. The range is 13 commits (`git rev-list --count d38a127ad..0f221ac89` = 13), newest first: "Report selected-target handles as ephemeral" (0f221ac89), "Expose one-shot selected-target execution over loopback API" (0483fde5d), "Execute admitted selected targets from evidence handles" (3a20b653a). The feature is about 7,700 lines: roughly 6,940 lines of server source across six .cs files in the tools folder plus about 1,010 lines in three production classes (the L3 selected-target executor, the factory composition, and the profile catalog).

**Plain terms: what "loopback" means here.** "Loopback" is the network address 127.0.0.1, the machine's own internal address that no other computer on the network can reach. The code binds the server to exactly that address. Code fact: the constant is `public const string LoopbackUrl = "http://127.0.0.1:0"` (DynamicGraphCompositionServerOptions.cs:13). Code fact: the host is told to use only that URL (Program.cs:43, builder.WebHost.UseUrls(...LoopbackUrl)). The trailing ":0" means the operating system chooses a random free port, which the server writes into a local "ready file" so a caller on the same machine can find it. Net effect: only a process on this same machine can reach the server.

**(a) End to end, in human terms.** A caller on this machine walks four steps. Step 1, session create: the caller POSTs a deterministic graph "composition profile" to /api/v1/dynamic-graph/sessions (Program.cs:148), or picks one profile from a catalog at /catalog-sessions (Program.cs:265); the server composes a read-only session and returns a session id shaped dgs_<32 hex>. A "composition session" is just a short-lived server-side record of that one composed graph. Step 2, evidence admit: the caller POSTs "selected-target" evidence (a target, an authority result, a novelty request) to /sessions/{id}/selected-target-evidence (Program.cs:456). "Selected target" means one specific edge/flow the caller has already chosen; the server does not choose it. On success the server hands back an ephemeral evidence handle shaped dgeh_<32 hex> (handle id minted at DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:118) together with an expiry timestamp (Program.cs:1274-1275; the JSON field is expires_at_utc, DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:47 and :78). Step 3, one-shot run: the caller POSTs that handle plus a run id, scenario id, and user request to /sessions/{id}/selected-target-runs (Program.cs:561); the server executes exactly that one selected target exactly once and returns a receipt. Code fact: the run is rejected as a 500 unless the receipt reports exactly one direct invocation (`if (receipt.DirectTargetInvocationCount != 1)` returns HOST_SELECTED_TARGET_RUN_RESULT_INVALID, Program.cs:671-672). Step 4, ephemeral handle expiry: the handle is single-use and time-bounded. Code fact: claiming the handle to run it both retires the id and removes the evidence in one atomic step (ClaimSelectedTargetEvidence, DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1668-1694), so a second run with the same handle returns 404 HOST_SELECTED_TARGET_EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND. Code fact: the handle carries the session's own ExpiresAtUtc (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1626), and the session TTL defaults to 900 seconds / 15 minutes (DynamicGraphCompositionServerOptions.cs:29); once the session expires a run returns 410 HOST_SESSION_EXPIRED (Program.cs:643-644). So "ephemeral" = single-use and expiring with the 15-minute session. Every hop also enforces a configured single-tenant binding via the X-XIIGen-Tenant-Id header (Program.cs:1080-1096).

**(b) Which plan block does it serve? Out-of-plan.** I re-confirmed the grep counts precisely. Across the requirement-defining plan files (active-codex-execution-plan-*.md, the subset the orchestrator checked) all four patterns are zero: "selected target" 0, "selected-target" 0, "loopback" 0, "composition session" 0. Across the FULL plan-of-record set (active-codex-execution-*), "selected-target" is still 0 and "selected target" is still 0, but "loopback" = 5 matching lines and "composition session" = 3 matching lines (and "127.0.0.1" = 2). Document fact: every one of those non-zero hits lives in a single file, active-codex-execution-41-current-20-cycle-rag-library-20260719.md, which is a 20-cycle RAG-library review/simulation ledger dated 2026-07-19, and the lines describe the already-built server (for example line 2768 "loopback URL: http://127.0.0.1:0" and line 2202 a test-process walkthrough) rather than defining a requirement for it. So the loopback composition-session HOST is described only after the fact in one review ledger and is commissioned by no requirement-defining plan file. The specific selected-target evidence-admission-plus-one-shot-run surface appears in ZERO plan-of-record files (0 hits for "selected-target" anywhere, including the file-41 ledger). Conclusion: this is out-of-plan work, and no document claims otherwise - no plan block, and not even the descriptive file-41 ledger, authorizes a "selected-target" feature. The honest boundary this must not cross is plan anchor active-codex-execution-plan-04-dynamicgraph-machine.md:529 (mode=honest_untrained, "no claim to semantic quality"; the baseline branch at :518 sets SemanticQualityClaimed=false).

**(c) Does it respect the three invariants? Yes.** Invariant (i), no training: the process trips a fail-closed assembly wire. Code fact: the StartupReadinessState constructor first subscribes to the .NET assembly-load event (Program.cs:1534, AppDomain.CurrentDomain.AssemblyLoad += OnAssemblyLoad) and only then scans the already-loaded assemblies (Program.cs:1537, AppDomain.CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies().Any(IsForbidden(...))), latching _forbiddenAssemblyLoaded=1 on any hit. Because it subscribes BEFORE it scans, there is no already-loaded gap: an assembly already loaded before subscription is still caught by the scan at :1537, and anything that loads during or after is caught by OnAssemblyLoad (Program.cs:1650). Code fact: once latched, IsReady is permanently false (Program.cs:1555-1558), ReasonCode returns FORBIDDEN_TRAINING_ASSEMBLY_LOADED (Program.cs:1568), and the flag is never reset. Answer to the tripwire-gap question: it DOES scan already-loaded assemblies too, so there is no gap for assemblies loaded before the subscription. One informational nuance, not a gap in that question: the forbidden-name list IsForbidden (Program.cs:1727-1729) is an EXACT, case-insensitive match on exactly two assembly simple names, "DPO" and "TrainingLoop", which are precisely the two real training assemblies in the repo (11.DPO\DPO.csproj and 04.TrainingLoop\TrainingLoop.csproj); because it is exact and not substring, sibling training assemblies with other names (for example 18.CapabilityTraining, ProjectWikiTrainingRunner, InternalTrainReadyDpoRunner) would trip the wire only if loading them transitively pulls in DPO or TrainingLoop.

Invariant (ii), no trained-input claims: code fact - ProfilePolicy.ContainsForbiddenAuthority (DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:131-142) rejects any node whose descriptor is trained, claims deterministic final authority, claims runtime semantic quality, carries a checkpoint or manifest ref, or has a null or "trained" authority role, returning HOST_TRAINED_NODE_INPUT_FORBIDDEN (DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:71-72). Code fact - the /sessions endpoint also rejects a body.TrainedInput up front with the same code (Program.cs:198-199). This enforces the 04:529 honest boundary at the HTTP door.

Invariant (iii), no quarantine/legality/quality token leak: grep counts across the tools folder AND the three selected-target production files are DpoTrainReady = 0, trained_edge_unit = 0, honest_untrained = 0, isLearned = 0, quarantine = 0, legality = 0. The token SemanticQualityClaimed = 1, and code fact: that single hit is a REJECTION guard, `definition.Descriptor.RuntimeSemanticQualityClaimed` inside ContainsForbiddenAuthority (DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:136) - it appears only to FORBID a quality claim, the opposite of a leak. Supporting document claims in the code back this up: the L3 executor doc-comment states it "does not retry, use registry route fallbacks, inspect selection evidence, mutate topology, or make aggregate quality, training, DPO, promotion, or lifecycle claims" (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:10-14), and the factory composition RunAdmittedAsync doc-comment states the path "does not establish provenance, re-measure evidence, or expose an HTTP/client, aggregate-quality, DPO, training, promotion, or lifecycle boundary" (DynamicGraphFactorySelectedTargetExecutionComposition.cs:97-101). These are comment claims; the enforcing code that makes them true is the ContainsForbiddenAuthority fence and the DirectTargetInvocationCount==1 check quoted above.

NOT CHECKED (coverage boundary): I did not build or run any test suite, did not execute the server, and did not trace the ~2,400-line persistence file or the full 1,892-line registry beyond the selected-target run/handle paths and the tenant/tripwire hot spots; those are out of scope for a read-only status audit and are stated here as omissions, not as passes.

**Verdict:** selected-target loopback server = OUT-OF-PLAN but INVARIANT-RESPECTING - the selected-target evidence-admission-plus-one-shot-run surface appears in ZERO plan-of-record files (plan-*.md subset 0/0/0/0 for selected target / selected-target / loopback / composition session; full active-codex-execution-* set still 0 for selected-target, with the 5 loopback and 3 composition-session hits confined to the descriptive review ledger active-codex-execution-41-current-20-cycle-rag-library-20260719.md, which commissions nothing), so no document authorizes it; and it respects all three invariants - (i) the no-training assembly wire scans BOTH already-loaded (Program.cs:1537, after the :1534 subscribe, so no already-loaded gap) and future assemblies and latches permanently to FORBIDDEN_TRAINING_ASSEMBLY_LOADED (Program.cs:1555-1568), the only nuance being an exact two-name list "DPO"/"TrainingLoop" (Program.cs:1727-1729) matched to the real training assemblies; (ii) no trained input, via ContainsForbiddenAuthority and HOST_TRAINED_NODE_INPUT_FORBIDDEN (DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:131-142 and Program.cs:198-199); (iii) no legality/quality/quarantine leak (DpoTrainReady/trained_edge_unit/honest_untrained/isLearned/quarantine/legality all 0; the single SemanticQualityClaimed hit at DynamicGraphCompositionProfilePolicy.cs:136 is a rejection guard, not a claim).

### Finding 14 — Caller-attested selection evidence over HTTP: overclaim, hidden authority, and handle atomicity (Cycle 2, Task 3)

What the two endpoints do, in plain words. The loopback server (a localhost-only helper server) exposes two POST endpoints on a composition session. The first, `.../selected-target-evidence` (tools/DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer/Program.cs:456-559), accepts a request body that is entirely client-supplied: a measurement target, a full `EdgeAuthorityExecutionResult` (the record that normally represents "a learned unit made this routing decision", carrying its decision, per-target confidences, and proof strings — 21.AgentInfrastructure/Logical/DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityContracts.cs:332-376), and a novelty request. Code fact: the server hands that client-fabricated triple to `registry.AdmitSelectedTargetEvidence(...)` (Program.cs:510-517), which runs a consistency-only meter and, on success, stores the admitted measurement in a bounded in-memory buffer and returns a one-use handle (an opaque `dgeh_...` token that is the only way to trigger the run). The second endpoint, `.../selected-target-runs` (Program.cs:561-677), takes that handle plus a run_id / scenario_id / user_request, claims the handle, and executes the L3 logical flow named by the evidence exactly once (registry.RunAdmittedSelectedTargetAsync at Program.cs:622-630; the executor `DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor` resolves the flow by target id at DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:224 and runs it at :329). Code fact: the run path never re-runs any fail-closed learned edge-authority resolver server-side to decide which target should run — it runs the target the caller's evidence names.

(a) Overclaim — NO. Code fact: not one response field represents the caller's claims as server-verified learned quality. The admission response record `SelectedTargetEvidenceAdmissionHttpResponse` (DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:73-79) carries exactly request_id, session_id, status, evidence_handle, expires_at_utc, boundaries; the emitted status string is the literal word "admitted" (Program.cs:1273), and the token field is named evidence_handle, not "verified_*". Code fact: the run response record `AdmittedSelectedTargetRunHttpResponse` (DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:81-90) carries request_id, session_id, status, target_id, execution_id, evidence_ids, direct_target_invocation_count, boundaries; its emitted status string is the literal word "completed" (Program.cs:1290). Code fact: both responses embed a `CompositionBoundaryHttpResponse` whose defaults are emitted unchanged (DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:107-113) — applies_graph = false and performs_training = false — so the server states, inside the response itself, that it did NOT apply the learned graph authority and did NOT train. Code fact: the input contract even labels the caller's own normalization/tokenization values as caller_attested_fingerprint_normalization_id and caller_attested_tokenization_id (DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:249-252), naming them as attested-by-the-caller rather than confirmed. Document claim (a code comment, not runtime behaviour): the evidence meter's summary states it "makes no overall quality verdict and does not interpret AblationProofRef as an evaluation identifier" (DynamicGraphEdgeQualityEvidenceMeter.cs:210-215). Code fact backing that comment: the meter body only checks that the caller's own sub-records are mutually consistent — matching EdgeId, AuthorityUnitId, EvaluationId, RowsEvaluated, and split proofs (DynamicGraphEdgeQualityEvidenceMeter.cs:249-279) — and never checks any claim against server-held trained state. No field name anywhere in the two response records contains "verified", "learned", "trained", or "quality_confirmed"; the honest words used are "evidence", "admitted", "completed", and "execution".

(b) Hidden deterministic routing authority — NO violation of R-12 (requirement R-12 forbids a deterministic mechanism acting as the graph's final routing authority); this surface is an honest caller-driven one-shot that makes no authority claim. Document claim (executor class-summary comment): the executor "does not retry, use registry route fallbacks, inspect selection evidence, mutate topology, or make aggregate quality, training, DPO, promotion, or lifecycle claims" (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:10-14). Document claim (receipt type-summary comment): the invocation count "is the fixed fact established by this one-shot probe, not an aggregate execution, quality, training, DPO, promotion, or lifecycle verdict" (DynamicGraphEdgeAuthoritySelectionTargetExecutionProbe.cs:48-51). Code fact backing those comments: two independent guard layers reject any target flagged as a deterministic final authority — DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.Validate fails with FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT when MechanismKind is not LearningSubject or DeterministicFinalAuthority is true (DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.cs:37-43), and the executor's association gate returns AssociationInvalid when the target is not an L3 LearningSubject, is a DeterministicFinalAuthority, or its target id does not equal the bound authority edge id (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:204-212). Code fact: the one-shot property is structural — DirectTargetInvocationCount is a fixed `=> 1` expression-bodied property on the receipt (DynamicGraphEdgeAuthoritySelectionTargetExecutionProbe.cs:76), and the HTTP layer defensively rejects the run with HOST_SELECTED_TARGET_RUN_RESULT_INVALID if the receipt ever reports anything other than 1 (Program.cs:671). The honest residual, stated plainly: the caller does effectively choose which target runs, because the caller supplies the evidence whose target id names the flow and the server runs exactly that flow. Why this is NOT the thing R-12 forbids: the plan block that defines R-12 is titled "Замена скрытого детерминизма ... cutover на обучаемый авторитет ребра" (replacement of hidden determinism, cutover to a learned edge authority), and its rule is that deterministic finals must not masquerade as the learned edge authority, with baselines that must be labelled (active-codex-execution-plan-12-no-hidden-determinism.md:36 and :55); the sibling plan states the edge authority itself must be learned (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:24). Here there is no masquerade: the response's applies_graph = false announces that no learned routing ran, the executor and receipt announce that no authority / quality / training claim is made, and the measurement is bound to a session-scoped carrier — the coordinator requires ownedTarget.TargetId to equal AuthorityEvidence.EdgeId (DynamicGraphMeasurementCoordinator.cs:469-477) rather than letting the measurement float free (the carrier-binding requirement of active-codex-execution-plan-11-measurement.md:22-24). So the surface is an honestly-labelled caller-driven one-shot invocation, not a hidden deterministic final routing authority.

(c) Handle atomicity — ATOMIC; two concurrent runs with the same handle cannot both execute. Code fact: the consuming claim is `SessionEntry.ClaimSelectedTargetEvidence(handleId)` (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1668-1694); its entire body runs inside one `lock (_evidenceGate)` (line 1671) and performs, as a single critical section, the guard check (revoked / retired-set-contains / live-dict-contains), then `_retiredSelectedTargetEvidenceHandleIds.Add(handleId)` (line 1680), then `_selectedTargetEvidence.Remove(handleId, ...)` (line 1685). Code fact: both collections are a plain HashSet and Dictionary guarded only by that one lock (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1518-1523), so the check-then-act is not a race — the second thread to enter the lock finds the handle already in the retired set (or absent from the live dictionary) and returns null, which the run path maps to status Unknown (a 404). Code fact: the pre-claim probes in the run method at Program-path lines 589 and 614 use the NON-consuming ResolveSelectedTargetEvidence, so even if two runs both pass those probes, only the one atomic ClaimSelectedTargetEvidence can win. Document claim (a test, read not run): DynamicGraphCompositionSelectedTargetSessionRegistryTests.cs:835-890 (test RunAdmittedSelectedTargetAsync_ConcurrentSameHandleHasOneWinner) launches two runs on the same handle with maxConcurrentRuns = 2 and a barrier that forces both threads into the second gate simultaneously, then asserts exactly one result Completed, exactly one Unknown, session.AdmittedCallCount == 1, and the handle afterward resolving Unknown — i.e. the target flow was invoked exactly once. This is the double-claim / concurrency assertion attributed to commit 3a20b653a; I read it and did not execute it.

NOT CHECKED (coverage boundary): I did not build or run any code or test (read-only status audit); I did not trace the full 1,892-line registry or the persistence layer beyond the admit / run / claim / handle paths named above; I confirmed the reservation-and-publication path (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1584-1641) uses the same _evidenceGate lock plus an Interlocked one-shot consume but did not exhaustively model its every interleaving; and I did not audit the JSON converters' full field coverage beyond the response and boundary field names decisive for verdict (a).

**Verdict:** (a) overclaim = NO — every response field is honestly caller-supplied ("evidence" / "admitted" / "completed"; boundaries applies_graph=false and performs_training=false at DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:107-113 emitted via Program.cs:1273 and :1290; caller_attested_* inputs at DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:249-252; no "verified" / "learned" / "trained" / "quality_confirmed" field anywhere), and the meter itself makes no quality verdict (DynamicGraphEdgeQualityEvidenceMeter.cs:210-215); (b) hidden deterministic authority = NO R-12 violation — an honestly-labelled caller-driven one-shot with no authority or quality claim (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:10-14 and :204-212; DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.cs:37-43; DirectTargetInvocationCount => 1 at DynamicGraphEdgeAuthoritySelectionTargetExecutionProbe.cs:76 enforced at Program.cs:671), with the caller-picks-target fact surfaced (applies_graph=false) rather than masqueraded as the learned edge authority; (c) handle atomicity = ATOMIC — the claim's check / retire / remove is one locked critical section (DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:1668-1694), proven one-winner by DynamicGraphCompositionSelectedTargetSessionRegistryTests.cs:835-890.

### Finding 15 — FLOW-38 quarantine under concurrency, tampering, and the second server (Cycle 2, Task 4)

Plain-language framing: FLOW-38 is the design-conversion feedback flow in which a client asserts "this answer was better than that one". The quarantine handler accepts that unverified client evidence and must keep it permanently non-trainable — the boolean `DpoTrainReady` (the flag that, if true, would let a row become a DPO training pair) can never become true. The store is a single append-only JSONL file (`flow38-quarantined-source-rows.jsonl`) plus a sibling `.lock` file, both under one root directory; JSONL means one JSON object per line. Cycle 1 proved the straight-line path is fail-closed; this finding stresses four sharper questions. Every file:line below is inside C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation. The single handler-and-store source is 25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler.cs, called "the handler file" here.

Store-constructor grep (`grep -rn "Flow38QuarantineStore\|flow38-quarantined-source-rows" --include=*.cs`): the concrete store class `JsonlDesignConversionFlow38QuarantineStore` is constructed in exactly two production places — the primary ASP.NET host DesignConversionModelServerAspNetHostStartup.cs, which receives an already-built store and wraps it (:521-522, :543-544, :842-843), and the second local server tools\DesignConversionLiveLocalServer\Program.cs (:919-921, :985-987, :1136-1138). Every other match is either the type/constant definition inside the handler file or a test (Tests\...). Code fact: the only production caller of the store's write method `AdmitAsync` is the handler at the handler file:1319 (a grep for `.AdmitAsync(` excluding /Tests/ returns that one line), and the only route binding is `RegisterDesignConversionFlow38Quarantine` / `MapDesignConversionModelServerFlow38Quarantine`, both of which wrap the same handler. So nothing writes quarantine data except through the one guarded handler.

(a) CONCURRENCY — HOLDS. Code fact: two gates guard every admission. The in-process gate is a `SemaphoreSlim(1,1)` shared per records-path through a static dictionary (declared handler file:121-122, fetched or created at :164); `AdmitAsync` waits on it at :182 and releases it in a `finally` at :245, so within one operating-system process all admissions to the same file are strictly one-at-a-time. The cross-process gate is the `.lock` file opened `FileShare.None` on Windows (which is itself the exclusive lock, :634-635) or `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on Unix (:642-649), acquired in `AcquireCrossProcessLockAsync` with up to 250 retries at 20 ms (:114-115, :439-471); if it never acquires it throws (:470), which the handler converts to a 503 (:1335-1341). Both the read-back scan (:194 / :249-309) and the append (:233 / :413-437) run while holding BOTH gates. Code fact: no admission can skip validation under a race, because the scan re-reads and re-validates the whole file each time (:249-309), and before writing `AppendFrameAsync` re-checks that the file length still equals what the scan saw (:422), that the last byte is a newline (:424-428), and that the byte cap still holds (:431) — any change between scan and append throws. Code fact: a duplicate event identity cannot be produced under a race, because the serialized scan-then-append returns Replayed or Conflict on the second admission of the same EventId+TenantScope (:293-298) instead of writing a second line. Code fact: a half-written final line cannot be accepted, because a frame is always the payload plus one trailing newline written in a single `WriteAsync` and then flushed to disk (:227-229, :435-436), and on read-back a file that does not end on a newline boundary throws "incomplete terminal frame" (:305-306), with a total-bytes/length consistency check alongside it (:303-304).

(b) PASS-THROUGH at the Snapshot copy (handler file:517) — HOLDS. Code fact: `Snapshot(row)` (:488-518) deep-copies every string and the provenance list and copies the two client booleans `SemanticQualityClaimed` (:516) and `DpoTrainReady` (:517) verbatim. Code fact: `Snapshot` has exactly one caller — `AdmitAsync` at :176 (a grep for `Snapshot(` in the file returns only the definition at :488 and that single call). Code fact: the copied `DpoTrainReady` value reaches only three fenced destinations, all inside the already-locked validate/persist path: (1) `IsPersistableQuarantineRow(snapshot)` at :177, which runs `ValidateRow` (:1384-1385, :1387-1430) and makes `AdmitAsync` throw at :178 if the flag is true (:1421-1422) — and the handler has already run the same validation on the raw request and returned 422 before ever calling the store (:1313); (2) the fingerprint hash at :180 (:522-526); and (3) the persisted envelope's `Source` sub-object at :222, where the envelope-level `DpoTrainReady` is hard-coded false regardless of the client (:221) and the snapshot's own flag can only be false because validation already forced it so. Code fact: the value never reaches an HTTP response — the success body hard-codes `dpoTrainReady = false` (:1535) and echoes only the event id and the store's refs (:1528-1533), the error body hard-codes it false (:1566), and the response headers hard-code `x-flow38-dpo-train-ready = False` (:1584). Code fact: the value never reaches a log (neither the handler nor the store constructs any logger) and never reaches a downstream consumer (the store's result record `DesignConversionFlow38QuarantineStoreResult` at :42-48 has no train-ready field at all — only EventId, RecordRef, Fingerprint, Created, Replayed, Conflict).

(c) SECOND SERVER — HOLDS. Code fact: the second local server tools\DesignConversionLiveLocalServer\Program.cs builds its store with its own state root `Path.Combine(privateRuntimeDataRoot, "flow38-quarantine")` (:412) and hands each store into the same startup factories (`CreateNonTrainingLocalRouteRegistry...` and `CreateLocalRouteRegistry...`) that wrap it in the identical `DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler` (Program.cs:919-921, :985-987, :1136-1138 feeding DesignConversionModelServerAspNetHostStartup.cs:521-522, :543-544, :842-843). Code fact: the second server exposes no extra write route — the only route registration is `RegisterDesignConversionFlow38Quarantine` and the only `AdmitAsync` caller is the handler (handler file:1319), so both hosts reach the store through the same guards. Reasoned fact: two hosts pointing at DIFFERENT state roots do not weaken the invariant — each store applies the same fail-closed guards to its own file and each hard-codes `DpoTrainReady=false`, and there is no cross-root aggregation that could launder a flag; if two hosts share the SAME root the cross-process file lock (:439-471) serializes them, and if they use different roots they touch different files and never interfere. Either way the flag stays false in both.

(d) TAMPERING — HOLDS, fail-closed by whole-store rejection rather than skip-and-continue. Code fact: the scan loop (:269-301) contains no try/catch/continue — every anomaly throws, aborting the entire scan so that the handler returns 503 (:1335-1341) and NO admission succeeds; a tampered file is rendered wholly unreadable rather than letting one bad line slip through. Code fact for the three Cycle-2 tamper modes: a truncated final line throws "incomplete terminal frame" at :305-306 (with the pre-append terminal-newline check at :424-428 as a second layer); an oversized record throws "oversized record" during the scan at :283-284 (plus the admit-time payload cap at :224-225); and a duplicated event identity across two persisted lines throws "duplicate event identity" at :296-297. Code fact for the flag itself on read-back: the parser re-runs `IsPersistableQuarantineRow(persisted.Source)` (:342) and re-checks that the envelope-level `SemanticQualityClaimed`/`DpoTrainReady` are false (:352-353), so a store hand-edited to set the flag true is rejected on the very next read.

Document-claim versus code-fact on promotion. Document claim: the plan `active-codex-execution-plan-08-legal-teacher-gate.md` states that affected candidates stay `DpoTrainReady=false` and produce no `GraphDpoPair` (:13, :18, :20), that even an ACCEPT verdict "still requires the full predicate and G_DPO_FINAL" and leaves `DpoTrainReady=false` (:357, :360-362), and that missing or invalid proof "remains QUARANTINE, DpoTrainReady=false" (:28). Code fact: this quarantine store has no promotion or read-for-training path at all — its interface exposes only `AdmitAsync` (handler file:50-55), the persisted envelope hard-codes `DpoTrainReady=false` (:221), and the only production reader of the JSONL is the dedup scan during the next admission (:249-309); the file is append-plus-dedup only and cannot itself flip the flag, which is consistent with the plan's claim that only a separate legal projection (`TriadLegalProjection.Project`, plan :360-362) may ever promote.

Tested versus untested edge cases (one allowed read of Tests\DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineTests.cs, 24 [Fact] tests; read, not run). TESTED: valid quarantine persists and replays across a store restart (dedup) — test file:28; client `DpoTrainReady=true`, and `AllowedUse=CommonGlobal`/`Route=common`, rejected 422 with no file written — :85; same event id with different evidence returns 409 conflict and leaves one line — :117; an extra raw-output property returns schema-invalid 400 and a spoofed X-Tenant-Id header returns 422 transport-identity-mismatch — :144; Kestrel loopback happy path (202) plus an oversized request body (413) — :177; a chunked invalid-UTF-8 body returns 400 and an oversized chunked body returns 413 — :266; missing/wrong bearer token return 401 and an unconfigured store returns 503 — :351; missing, duplicate, and escaped-duplicate REQUEST properties return 400 — :387; read-back of a corrupted store (truncated last byte, truncated JSON suffix, oversized line, invalid-UTF-8 line, whitespace-only, and "{\n") all return 503 with the file left byte-identical — :422 (this is the direct test for the (d) truncated-line and oversized-record modes); read-back of persisted envelope/source schema corruption (unknown outer property, missing outer fingerprint, duplicate and escaped-duplicate outer eventId, unknown source property, missing source field, duplicate and escaped-duplicate source eventId) all 503 with the file unchanged — :464; total-byte and record-count capacity refuse the append at both scan-time and append-time with the file unchanged — :513; the record counter rejects int.MaxValue+1 without overflow — :593; Unix native open/lock flag profiles for Linux and macOS — :603; an unsupported storage platform returns a stable 503 and creates no directory — :642; mutable provenance is snapshotted before the file-lock wait, proven by mutating the caller's list mid-admission while blocked on a held lock and confirming the persisted record keeps the ORIGINAL value — :673 (the direct concrete test for the (b) Snapshot pass-through isolation); reparse-point root rejected with no outside write — :720; record/lock reparse leaves rejected — :746; dangling record/lock links fail without creating outside targets — :787; a leaf swap between validation and safe-open for the LockFile, RecordRead, and RecordAppend stages never follows an outside target — :823 (a TOCTOU race); root/ancestor swap before the safe lease — :884; root mutation after the safe lease — :935; missing root-segment creation after the parent lease — :1002; eight concurrent distinct-instance admissions of the same evidence produce exactly one Created, seven Replayed, zero Conflict, and one line — :1090 (the direct test for (a) in-process concurrency); and two concurrent distinct-instance admissions of different evidence produce one Created and one Conflict and one line — :1115 (the direct test that a race produces no duplicate). UNTESTED (gaps in this file; reported, not run): (1) a tampered store holding TWO fully-valid lines with the same EventId+TenantScope — the cross-line "duplicate event identity" branch at handler file:296-297 — is never exercised, because the "duplicate" cases in the :464 test are duplicate-property-within-one-object caught earlier by the exact-property check, not two separate valid lines; (2) a persisted line with `dpoTrainReady=true` or `semanticQualityClaimed=true` flipped in the file — the read-back rejects it at :342 and :352-353 but no test asserts this specific flip (the flag-flip at intake is tested at :85, the read-back flip of these two booleans is not); (3) the cross-process lock-exhaustion path (250 failed attempts leading to an IOException then 503, :444-470) — no test holds the lock beyond the retry budget, though the lock-wait itself is exercised briefly by the provenance test at :684-693; (4) a genuine SECOND operating-system process racing on the same root — only same-process distinct-instance concurrency is tested, not true multi-process contention on the shared flock / FileShare.None lock; and (5) the second server's own wiring (tools\DesignConversionLiveLocalServer\Program.cs) and the two-hosts-different-roots scenario are not exercised by this file (a separate Tests\DesignConversionLiveLocalServerTests.cs references the store, outside the one allowed test read).

**Verdict:** FLOW-38 under stress = HOLDS fail-closed on all four sub-questions — (a) concurrency HOLDS (in-process semaphore at handler file:121-122/:164/:182/:245 plus cross-process file lock at :439-471, scan-and-append under both gates, TOCTOU re-checks at :275-276/:303-304/:422, torn-line reject at :305-306, duplicate-identity reject at :296-297); (b) pass-through HOLDS (Snapshot :488-518 has the single caller :176; the copied DpoTrainReady is rejected-if-true at :177 and :1421-1422, is hard-coded false in every response at :1535/:1566/:1584, has no result-record field at :42-48, and is never logged); (c) second server HOLDS (Program.cs :919-921/:985-987/:1136-1138 reach only the same handler via :521-522/:543-544/:842-843, the sole AdmitAsync caller is :1319, and different roots do not weaken the false-flag invariant); (d) tampering HOLDS (whole-store reject with no skip-and-continue: truncated :305-306, oversized :283-284, duplicate identity :296-297, persisted-flag flip :342/:352-353) — no code hole found; the open items are test-coverage gaps (the cross-line duplicate-identity read-back branch, the persisted dpoTrainReady/semanticQualityClaimed flip, the lock-exhaustion 503, a genuine second OS process, and the second-server wiring), not code holes.

### Finding 16 — Can the combination runners lose an error or fake a success? (Cycle 2, Task 5)

Plain-language framing: two "combination runners" take one input and run it through several unit-combination plans. The many-plan runner is 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs (called "the bounded runner" here); the one-plan runner is 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs ("the single runner"). Each run returns one AsyncCandidateBatch whose Status string is one of "OK", "PARTIAL_FAILURE", "EMPTY_CANDIDATES", or a top-level failure code "CANCELLED" / "TIMEOUT" / "ALL_PLANS_FAILED". Cycle 1 proved the happy paths and the concurrency cap; this finding asks whether an error can be silently swallowed, or a run mislabelled successful, when a plan fails, the shared timeout fires, and the caller cancels — possibly all in one run. Every file:line below is inside C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation.

(a) STATUS HONESTY — HOLDS in both dangerous directions (a timeout hidden as a clean partial-failure, and error codes dropped from a success). Code fact (bounded): the timeout is not a separate signal that competes with cancellation — the timeout's CancellationTokenSource is linked into the single run token inside ExecutionCancellationScope.Create (BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:730-748), so any timeout also trips executionCancellation.Token. Code fact: the status decision Aggregate (BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:363-392) is only reachable when that run token is NOT cancelled — the post-loop guard returns before Aggregate if the token is set (:172-181) and again if ClassifyCancellation is non-null (:183-188). Code fact: ClassifyCancellation (:642-653) returns "CANCELLED" when the caller or embedded token is set and "TIMEOUT" only when the timeout source fired (:647-650), so a genuine timeout can never reach Aggregate and can never be printed as PARTIAL_FAILURE — it is returned as TIMEOUT (or as CANCELLED if the caller also cancelled; see the precedence note). Code fact: inside Aggregate, "OK" requires allClean — every plan outcome of kind Clean, which by construction carries an empty error array (:387; PlanOutcome.Clean at :697-698) — plus an exact candidate-and-count match (:388-392); any plan that carries even one error code is Partial or Failed, forces "PARTIAL_FAILURE", and all such codes are gathered in plan-index order (:368-371). So no error code can be dropped from an "OK" batch. Code fact (single): status is "OK" only when aggregatedErrorCodes.Length == 0 (SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:301); every candidate's error codes are accumulated (:269) and echoed into both the candidate and the batch ErrorCodes (:299, :310), so nothing is dropped. Code fact: a cancellation/timeout check runs immediately before the OK/PARTIAL batch is built (:222-227), and after each async step (:141-146, :189-194), with the same "CANCELLED"-beats-"TIMEOUT" ordering at :518-529 (timeout at :525-526), so a timeout during node execution returns "TIMEOUT" and never a mislabelled "OK"/"PARTIAL". Informational (safe direction, not a hole): (i) cancel beats timeout by design — when both fire in the same run the batch reports "CANCELLED" even though the timeout also elapsed (bounded :647-650, single :523-526); this is a deterministic priority that names a real cause (the caller did cancel), not a swallow. (ii) A false-positive "TIMEOUT"/"CANCELLED" can be reported if the token trips at the exact instant the last plan finishes cleanly, because the post-loop guard re-reads the token after the active set drains (bounded :172); this over-reports failure and can discard already-completed clean candidates, but it never fakes a success, so it is safe-direction.

(b) THE NULL STREAM — HOLDS at runtime; half-wired only in the spec. Code fact: the batch contract declares an optional live stream CandidateStream of type IAsyncEnumerable<GraphCandidateOutput>? (00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:586, inside AsyncCandidateBatch at :582-591), and both runners always set it to null (BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:413; SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:306 and the empty-batch path :501). Code fact: no production code reads it — a repository grep for CandidateStream (grep -rn "CandidateStream" --include=*.cs) returns only the contract declaration, the three null assignments above, and test files; the real batch consumer, DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator, reads only Status, Candidates, ErrorCodes, and the two counts (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:433-474) and never touches CandidateStream, so there is no null-reference and no silent-skip risk (nobody iterates the stream). Document claim: the plan spec active-codex-execution-plan-06-async-combinations.md lists CandidateStream as a field of the canonical record (:725, :1057) and its note calls the batch a "Producer output batch/stream for many-to-many async candidates" (:731), while marking the whole record "Статус: ПРОЕКТ" (status: DRAFT). So the field name advertises a live stream the producers never populate — a half-wired promise at the spec level, not a runtime defect; this report's own Finding 4 table already records "stream null at :413" honestly (this report file:114).

(c) THE FAN-IN MERGER — HOLDS, and it is NOT on the runner path. Code fact: 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DeterministicGraphFanInMerger.cs is not called by either combination runner — the bounded runner folds its per-plan results together in its own private Aggregate (:358-395) and the single runner builds its one candidate inline; a grep for the merger (grep -rn "FanInMerger" --include=*.cs excluding Tests\) shows its only production caller through IEdgeFanInMerger is DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:343 (merger.Merge(mergeInput)), a separate fan-in over GraphAsyncBranchResult. So the merger's correctness is adjacent to, not inside, the runner error path. Code fact (drop a candidate?): no — duplicate canonical (MergeKey, PathOrderRef) and duplicate BranchId are rejected before any merge (ValidateBranchSet, DeterministicGraphFanInMerger.cs:182-194), and every branch is folded in via SelectMany across all ordered branches (metrics, evidence, warnings, final human result, times at :589-604), with MergedBranchCount = orderedBranches.Length (:72), worst-of verdict (:552-554), minimum confidence (:560), and a Passed that is true only if ALL branches passed with no errors, no failure verdict, and no conflicts (:555-559) — a failing branch cannot be hidden into a pass. Code fact (drop an error code?): no distinct code is lost — BuildFailureReasonCodes concatenates envelope, active-branch, and rejected-branch error codes and appends "CONFLICT_UNRESOLVED" when conflicts exist (:738-751). Informational nuance: StableDistinct deduplicates (:823-824), so two branches emitting the SAME code collapse to one entry and top-level per-branch multiplicity/attribution is lost — but each branch's own error codes are still retained in its context fragment (:705) and in the merged warnings (:600), and any error forces Passed=false, so no error silently vanishes into a success. Code fact (reorder nondeterministically?): no — ordering is ordinal and content-derived throughout: OrderBranches by (MergeKey, PathOrderRef) (:489-493), StableDistinct sorts ordinally (:823-824), SortedDictionary for metrics/context (:636, :815), transition signals keyed by length-prefixed content in a SortedDictionary (:788-809), and a commutative worst-of verdict (:858-866); the code never uses caller enumeration order, and permutation-invariance is asserted by tests (Tests\DynamicGraphFanInMergerTests.cs:107-110, :328-338, :592-660).

Tested versus untested failure combinations (one allowed read of Tests\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs, read not run). TESTED as single axes and as one pair: fewer plans than requested marks PARTIAL_FAILURE (:241-256); a mix of candidate/empty/failure plans returns an ordered PARTIAL_FAILURE with plan-prefixed codes (:259-285); child partial codes normalized and prefixed (:316-335); an invalid child code mapped to the generic stable code (:338-357); all plans failed collapses to one ALL_PLANS_FAILED (:304-313); a single global timeout with all plans hanging returns TIMEOUT and drains the active set (:455-493); method-or-embedded cancellation returns CANCELLED (:407-451); and the ONE two-axis precedence case — caller cancel armed together with a 5-second timeout, all plans hanging, cancel fires — asserts CANCELLED wins over TIMEOUT (:496-533). UNTESTED (reported as coverage facts, not run): (1) timeout-during-partial-failure — a run in which one plan has ALREADY completed with a failure or partial while another plan is still running when the timeout fires; every timeout/cancel test uses uniformly-hanging nodes so nothing completes first, so the code-traced behaviour (the run token trips and the whole batch returns TIMEOUT, discarding the accumulated partial) is not asserted; (2) cancel-during-partial-failure — the same interleaving with a caller cancel instead of a timeout; (3) the full three-way of a plan failing plus the timeout firing plus the caller cancelling in one run is never assembled as a single scenario — the precedence is proven only with all-hanging nodes. These are test-coverage gaps, not code holes: the code trace above shows each combination resolves to a single honest failure status.

**Verdict:** runner error-handling = HOLDS on all three sub-questions — (a) status honesty HOLDS (a real timeout can never reach the PARTIAL_FAILURE decision because the timeout is linked into the run token and caught at BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:172-188 / :642-653 and SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:222-227 / :518-529; "OK" requires all-clean with zero error codes at :387-392 and :301, so no error is dropped into a success); (b) the null stream HOLDS at runtime (always null at :413 / :306 / :501, no production reader — the consumer at DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:433-474 ignores it), half-wired only in the DRAFT spec active-codex-execution-plan-06-async-combinations.md:725 / :731; (c) the fan-in merger HOLDS (no branch dropped, no error lost into a pass, fully ordinal-deterministic ordering at DeterministicGraphFanInMerger.cs:182-194 / :555-559 / :489-493 / :823-824) and is not even on the runner path (its sole IEdgeFanInMerger caller is DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:343). No code hole found where an error is swallowed or a success faked; the open items are the cancel-over-timeout precedence and a safe-direction false-TIMEOUT race (both honest, non-dangerous), the spec's unwired CandidateStream promise, and the untested mixed timeout/cancel-during-partial-failure combinations.

### Finding 17 — Is the learned-authority gate unbypassable on every routing path? (Cycle 2, Task 6)

Plain-language framing: a "routing decision" is the graph choosing which node or flow runs next, and every such decision is stamped with an "authority-source" label that says WHO decided. The plan requires that any FINAL routing authority be a LEARNED unit — one produced by training and registered with IsLearned=true — and that a deterministic rule must never wear the learned label. The single learned label is the exact string "trained_edge_unit", defined once as the constant RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.TrainedEdgeUnitRole (00.Core\RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.cs:15); the honest deterministic labels are "baseline_candidate_generator" (same file :12) and "topology_return_loop". Cycle 1 proved that WHEN the edge-authority resolver runs it fails closed — it rejects any unit whose registry descriptor is not flagged learned (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\RegistryDynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityResolver.cs:81-82, message "Resolved edge authority unit is not learned").
