Cycle 2 asks the complementary question: does EVERY production path that produces a routing decision or writes an authority-source label either pass through that fail-closed pipeline, or honestly label itself deterministic? Every file:line below is inside C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation unless a plan file is named.

The grep classification (both greps excluded Tests\). Code fact: the learned string literal "trained_edge_unit" exists in exactly ONE place in production code — its definition at 00.Core\RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.cs:15; every other production reference is the named constant RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.TrainedEdgeUnitRole (aliased locally as ExpectedAuthoritySource inside the pipeline, the edge flow, and the ablation probe, and as LearnedEdgeAuthoritySource inside the orchestrator). Code fact: grep -rn "AuthoritySource" (non-Tests) returns 34 lines; classified by whether each one WRITES a value into a decision/handoff or only READS/compares one, the WRITE sites that stamp the LEARNED label number only three, and all three sit downstream of the fail-closed gate: (1) 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityExecutionPipeline.cs:1251 re-stamps the learned label inside TrySnapshotDecision, reachable only after the same method's fail-closed check at :1173 (source.AuthoritySource must already equal trained_edge_unit, else it returns false); (2) 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:748 (and its metric twin :778) stamps the label only after invoking the pipeline (pipeline.ExecuteAsync at :555) and re-checking the returned decision's label at :637; (3) 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityAblationProbe.cs:639 re-stamps it inside a diagnostic snapshot, gated by its own fail-closed check at :581. Code fact: every OTHER AuthoritySource write either stamps a DETERMINISTIC label — DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:484 (and metric :505) writes "topology_return_loop" for the return-loop edge — or is a neutral carrier/pass-through (the DynamicGraphEdgeTargetHandoff constructor stores whatever string it is handed at 00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:182; the defensive copy at :377; the HTTP serializer copies the field through verbatim at tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:356/:369). Code fact: the parallel role field AuthorityRole defaults to the deterministic "baseline_candidate_generator" (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\SubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:48), so a decision that forgets to set a role is deterministic, not learned — a fail-safe default; and the only production assignments of AuthorityRole = TrainedEdgeUnitRole are the two orchestrator sites (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:2299 and :2538), each bound to a non-return-loop handoff kind that was already authority-validated.

Path 1 — the adaptive orchestrator's stamping sites (AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:2290-2291 and :2297-2299). Code fact: these lines build a SubPhaseRoutingDecision and set its authority to LearnedEdgeAuthoritySource / TrainedEdgeUnitRole for a normal edge, or to ReturnLoopAuthoritySource / BaselineCandidateGeneratorRole for a return-loop. Code fact: this code (ResolveEdgeTargetSubPhases) runs only on a ValidatedEdgeHandoff, and the validator immediately upstream requires the incoming handoff to ALREADY carry the matching authority-source — for a non-return-loop edge it must equal LearnedEdgeAuthoritySource, checked at :2101-2106 (else "Dynamic graph target handoff scope or authority is invalid"). Because the only writer that mints LearnedEdgeAuthoritySource onto a handoff is the pipeline-gated edge flow (Path 2), the learned label can NEVER attach here to a non-pipeline result. Verdict for the learned branch: PASSES-GATE (AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:2299, gated by :2106). Verdict for the return-loop branch: HONEST-DETERMINISTIC (it stamps baseline_candidate_generator / topology_return_loop, AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:2290/:2298). The sibling helper AssociateDecisionWithHandoffAuthority (:2536-2538) applies the identical kind-bound rule.

Path 2 — the edge flow that constructs the edge-target handoff (DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs). Code fact: the file has two disjoint branches. The return-loop branch builds the handoff with "topology_return_loop" (:484) and the matching metric (:505) — it never touches the learned label. The learned branch (ExecuteLearnedTargetHandoffAsync at :536) calls the fail-closed pipeline once (pipeline.ExecuteAsync at :555), and its result builder TryBuildTargetHandoffResult refuses the result unless the pipeline's returned decision already carries trained_edge_unit (:637) before it writes trained_edge_unit into the outgoing handoff (:748) and metric (:778). Verdict: return-loop branch HONEST-DETERMINISTIC (:484/:505); learned branch PASSES-GATE (:748, gated by :555 + :637).

Path 3 — the deterministic multi-router still operative as a decision-maker (MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs). Code fact: its own header comment states it "makes deterministic routing decisions on the existing product path" and "never trained authority" (:11-12); every candidate it scores is tagged BaselineCandidateGeneratorRole (:116) and every decision it emits carries AuthorityRole = that baseline role (:197). Code fact: it actively refuses to launder a non-baseline label through its single-router fallback — if the fallback decision's role is anything other than baseline_candidate_generator it fails with SINGLE_ROUTER_AUTHORITY_ROLE_INVALID (:392-400, comment "Never normalize or launder learned, blank, or unknown authority through the baseline fallback"). Verdict: HONEST-DETERMINISTIC (MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:197, launder-guard :392-400).

Path 4 — the loopback composed-session run path (tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:480-515). Code fact: RunAsync delegates to entry.ExecuteAsync (:501), which is SessionEntry.ExecuteAsync (:1558) forwarding straight to _runSession.RunAsync (:1561); the run session is the composed dynamic-graph orchestrator run, so the router that actually decides inside a composed session is the same edge-authority pipeline (for a learned edge) or the same deterministic return-loop / multi-router (for a deterministic edge) already classified in Paths 1-3 — the composed session adds no new decision-maker. Code fact: the session registry and its HTTP transport never mint an authority-source — the wire serializer copies decision.AuthoritySource through unchanged (DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:356/:369) and only null-checks it (:339). Verdict: PASSES-GATE — the composed session inherits the orchestrator's gate and introduces no independent producer of the learned label.

Path 5 — the selected-target direct invocation (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs). Code fact: its class contract says it "resolves and executes exactly one admitted L3 logical flow by its selected target identity" and makes no "aggregate quality, training, DPO, promotion, or lifecycle claims" (:10-14). Code fact: it refuses any target that is not a learning subject or that claims deterministic final authority — it requires target.MechanismKind == LearningSubject and rejects target.DeterministicFinalAuthority (:204-206), so a deterministic final authority cannot be routed through it at all. Code fact: it does NOT mint an authority-source of its own — its single AuthoritySource reference only READ-validates that an incoming handoff's field is a canonical identity string (IsValidEdgeTargetHandoff at :450), and its output is an execution receipt, not a fresh authority claim (Finding 14 covered the evidence side). Verdict: PASSES-GATE / NO-LABEL-MINTED — it executes only an admitted learned-subject target and presents no deterministic decision under the learned label.

Document-claim versus code-fact on the requirement. Document claim: the plan block active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md is titled "Ребро графа ... + ОБУЧАЕМАЯ авторитетность ребра" (LEARNED edge authority) and its EdgeAuthorityUnit note requires an "ОБУЧАЕМЫЙ финальный авторитет ребра ... IsLearned=true" — a learned final edge authority (:545); the plan block active-codex-execution-plan-12-no-hidden-determinism.md is titled "Замена скрытого детерминизма (классификация 9 механизмов + cutover на обучаемый авторитет ребра)" and requires all nine deterministic mechanisms to be classified and demoted to candidate-generator / baseline / eval-only / model-component, and until they are cut over "they must not masquerade as learned" (:36, :55, :156). Code fact: the code enforces exactly this — the only registry gate that admits a learned authority fails closed on IsLearned (RegistryDynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityResolver.cs:81-82); the only three production writers of trained_edge_unit all sit downstream of that gate (pipeline :1251 behind :1173, edge flow :748 behind :555/:637, ablation probe :639 behind :581); and every deterministic decision-maker honestly stamps baseline_candidate_generator or topology_return_loop (MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:197, SubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:197, edge flow :484/:505, orchestrator return-loop :2290/:2298) over a baseline fail-safe default (SubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:48).

Scope boundary (what this finding did NOT verify): this is a read-only static trace of who WRITES the label, not a runtime proof; no build and no tests were run (structural reads of the resolver, pipeline, edge flow, orchestrator, multi-router, session registry, and L3 executor only). It confirms that the registry IsLearned flag is the gate and that every learned-label writer sits behind it, but it does not re-verify that the registered edge-authority unit is a genuinely trained model rather than an honest untrained scaffold — that is the PASS_AS_TRAINED_FEATURE question owned by the training/checkpoint cycles, not this routing-path-coverage question.

**Verdict:** learned-authority gate coverage = UNBYPASSABLE on every routing path — no path presents a deterministic decision under the learned label "trained_edge_unit". Five-path result: (1) adaptive orchestrator = PASSES-GATE on the learned branch (AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:2299, gated by :2106) and HONEST-DETERMINISTIC on the return-loop branch (:2290/:2298); (2) edge flow = PASSES-GATE on the learned branch (DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:748, gated by :555/:637) and HONEST-DETERMINISTIC on the return-loop branch (:484/:505); (3) multi-router = HONEST-DETERMINISTIC (MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:197, launder-guard :392-400); (4) composed-session loopback = PASSES-GATE (inherits the orchestrator gate; transport only serializes, DynamicGraphCompositionSessionRegistry.cs:501/:1561 and DynamicGraphCompositionHttpContracts.cs:356/:369); (5) selected-target invocation = PASSES-GATE / NO-LABEL-MINTED (DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:204-206 rejects deterministic final authority; :450 only read-validates). Zero MASQUERADE paths found; the sole learned-label literal has one definition (RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.cs:15) and three gated writers.

### Finding 18 — Do all measurement doors reject the forbidden shortcut mechanisms? (Cycle 2, Task 7)

**Scope.** This finding verifies the single measurement admission guard and the coordinator that fronts it, in the audited tree C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation at branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89. A "measurement door" here means a public method on the measurement coordinator that admits a measurement target and then binds a measured number to that target. "Mechanism kind" is a label the caller attaches to a target saying what kind of thing is being measured; the only allowed label is LearningSubject, and the four labels Knn, Scorer, Counter, Regex name shortcut mechanisms that would fake real learning. "Deterministic final authority" is a boolean flag on a target meaning the target is a fixed rule that always decides the outcome; such a target must also be rejected.

**The guard itself (the code).** The file 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.cs is a static class with one method, Validate. At lines 27-35 the code rejects a null target, a non-canonical target id, or an undefined level/mechanism enum with error code INVALID_DYNAMIC_GRAPH_MEASUREMENT_TARGET. At lines 37-43 the code rejects any target whose MechanismKind is not LearningSubject, OR whose DeterministicFinalAuthority flag is true, with error code FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT. The forbidden mechanism labels are defined by the enum at 00.Core\DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:25-32 (LearningSubject, Knn, Scorer, Counter, Regex); because the guard admits only LearningSubject, each of Knn, Scorer, Counter, Regex is rejected. A document claims this same forbidden set: the plan table at active-codex-execution-plan-12-no-hidden-determinism.md:55-59 lists the nine-mechanism classification whose forbidden verdict is spelled FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT, matching the code error code exactly.

**(a) Does every door call the guard first? HOLDS (the code).** The coordinator file 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphMeasurementCoordinator.cs exposes eleven public admission methods. Ten are named Measure* and one is named BindL2UntrainedHonesty (a Bind* method that admits a target the same way). Each method's FIRST executable statement is DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.Validate(target) and it returns immediately on failure, before any DynamicGraph*Meter.Measure(...) evidence is composed. The eleven doors and their guard-call lines are: BindL2UntrainedHonesty:82, MeasureAblation:219, MeasureAccuracy:251, MeasureNovelty:276, MeasureRunSpeed:305, MeasureTrainingSpeed:326, MeasureEdgeAuthorityControl:354, MeasureEdgeQualityEvidence:398, MeasureEdgeAuthoritySelectionQualityEvidence:443, MeasureEdgeIdentityQuality:493, MeasureEdgeOrderQuality:530. A repo-wide grep for DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.Validate returns exactly these eleven production sites and no more (all inside the coordinator). Count: 11 public admission entry points = 11 target-guard call sites, one per door, none skipping. There is no _targetGuard instance field anywhere (the guard is a static class, so that grep token returns zero hits). Verdict for (a): HOLDS.

**(b) Can production code build a wrapper directly, bypassing the guard? HOLDS in practice, with one honest caveat (the code).** The "wrapper" records are the DynamicGraphTarget...Measurement records that pair a validated target identity with a measured value (for example DynamicGraphTargetAblationMeasurement at 00.Core\DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:149-151). A grep for `new DynamicGraphTarget` returns thirteen hits: eleven are inside the coordinator file (the guarded paths, lines 206, 236, 264, 293, 314, 342, 382, 427, 481, 518, 555) and two are inside Tests\ (DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityControlMeterTests.cs:345 and DynamicGraphEdgeAuthoritySelectionTargetExecutionProbeTests.cs:335). There are ZERO production constructions of a wrapper record outside the coordinator. So in the current tree no production code bypasses the coordinator or the guard. The honest caveat is a code-fact about the type system, not a document claim: these wrapper records are declared public sealed record with public positional constructors (contracts file, for example lines 103-105, 149-151, 157-159), and the target identity record DynamicGraphMeasurementTarget is also publicly constructible (contracts file lines 38-42). Nothing in the compiler stops a future caller from writing new DynamicGraphTargetAblationMeasurement(new DynamicGraphMeasurementTarget("x", level, Knn, true), ...) and pairing a forbidden target with a number. The guarantee today is enforced by grep/convention (no such call site exists), not by an internal or private constructor. Verdict for (b): HOLDS today; no compiler-level seal.

**(c) Is the caller-attested re-validation accurate? ACCURATE (the code vs the honesty comments).** Cycle 1 established that all measured NUMBERS are caller-attested: the meters receive numbers already computed by the caller and project them; they do not re-score raw data. This finding pins the exact re-validation spot per meter and confirms the meters' honesty comments describe that behavior accurately, not aspirationally.

- Split guard — 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphHeldOutSplitGuard.cs. The comment at lines 11-13 claims it "validates evidence already computed by a caller; it does not calculate hashes, near-duplicate scores, accuracy, training state, or promotion decisions." The code (Validate, lines 28-84) re-validates STRUCTURE (canonical, unique train/eval/hash collections at 49-51; non-negative near-duplicate count at 52; method in {simhash,minhash} at 53; finite non-negative threshold at 54-55) and recomputes ONE relation itself (exact train-vs-eval root overlap via a HashSet at 60-67), and rejects any self-reported near-duplicate count greater than zero (69-74). It never recomputes the similarity score. Plain sentence: a false caller CANNOT smuggle train/eval roots that exactly overlap or a self-declared count above 0, but CAN smuggle roots/hashes that are truly near-duplicates while attesting a count of 0, because the guard trusts the attested count and never recomputes it. The comment is ACCURATE.

- Novelty meter — DynamicGraphNoveltyMeter.cs. The comment at lines 11-13 claims it "does not select rows, calculate pattern or token rates, run training, or decide promotion, full quality, or lifecycle state." The code (Measure, lines 20-63) admits the split (line 26) then re-validates ARITHMETIC and RANGE of the caller numbers: canonical evaluation id (30), RequestedRows above 0 (31), 0 to RequestedRows bound on RowsEvaluated (32-33), SelectionShortfall exactly equal to RequestedRows minus RowsEvaluated (34-35), and the three rates finite in [0,1] (36-38). It derives the gate boolean from the caller own MaxAccuracy (43-48); it never recomputes NewPatternRate, SourceTokenSeenRate, or MaxAccuracy from data. Plain sentence: a false caller CANNOT smuggle internally-inconsistent counts (shortfall must equal the subtraction) or out-of-range rates, but CAN smuggle fabricated-but-consistent rates that never came from real held-out rows. The comment is ACCURATE.

- Accuracy meter — DynamicGraphAccuracyMeter.cs. Comment lines 10-13 describe a projector of "live and source-disjoint held-out accuracy into numeric Q30 evidence." The code (Measure, lines 32-83) admits the split (38), re-validates shape and range of before/after (42-55), enforces before and after describe the SAME evaluation set (same id and same row count, 57-63), then RECOMPUTES the delta after-minus-before itself (65) and the Q30 gate from fixed floors (66-69). It copies the caller after.Accuracy and after.RewardMargin as the held-out numbers. Plain sentence: a false caller CANNOT smuggle a delta inconsistent with before/after or a mismatched evaluation set, but CAN smuggle a fabricated held-out accuracy and reward margin. Behavior matches the description.

- Ablation meter — DynamicGraphAblationMeter.cs. Comment lines 10-13 claim numeric-evidence-only with no proof of runtime state, novelty, trained state, promotion, or lifecycle. The code (Measure, lines 32-74) admits BOTH split proofs (39-45), re-validates shape (47), enforces subject and ablated share the same evaluation and field-for-field identical split proofs (52-54, SplitProofsMatch at 133-141), then RECOMPUTES the accuracy drop (61) and the Q11 gate (62-63). Plain sentence: a false caller CANNOT smuggle a subject/ablated pair measured over different splits or an inconsistent drop, but CAN smuggle two fabricated-but-matching held-out accuracies. The comment is ACCURATE.

The universal pattern (the code): every meter re-validates structure, numeric range, internal arithmetic consistency, and cross-field identity, and recomputes any derived delta/drop/gate itself, but COPIES the caller raw attested magnitudes because by design it is a projector, not a re-scorer. This is exactly what the honesty comments say, so they are accurate, not aspirational. The consequence for the admission gate is that FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT is a MECHANISM-and-authority gate (it blocks a target that declares itself Knn/Scorer/Counter/Regex or a deterministic final authority), NOT a number-authenticity gate: a caller who lies by labeling a shortcut target "LearningSubject" and attaches in-range, internally consistent numbers is not caught by this layer, which is consistent with the Cycle 1 caller-attested nuance and is honestly documented.

**Verdict:** measurement admission gate = HOLDS. (a) Every one of the 11 public admission doors (10 Measure* plus BindL2UntrainedHonesty) calls DynamicGraphMeasurementTargetGuard.Validate as its first statement before composing evidence (coordinator lines 82/219/251/276/305/326/354/398/443/493/530); no door skips it. (b) No production code constructs a DynamicGraphTarget...Measurement wrapper outside the coordinator (grep: 11 in-coordinator plus 2 in Tests\, 0 elsewhere); the only residual is that the wrapper records expose public constructors, so the seal is by-convention, not compiler-enforced. (c) The caller-attested re-validation is ACCURATE: the honesty comments at DynamicGraphHeldOutSplitGuard.cs:11-13 and DynamicGraphNoveltyMeter.cs:11-13 (and the accuracy and ablation meters) correctly state that structure, range, consistency, and identity are re-validated and derived values recomputed while raw measured magnitudes are trusted; the gate rejects the forbidden shortcut MECHANISMS but not fabricated-yet-consistent numbers.

### Finding 19 — Wired vs decorative: the graph-slice inventory (Cycle 2, Task 8)

**Plain-language summary.** This finding sorts the "graph slice" (the DynamicGraph* classes that were meant to let the orchestrator learn edges, hand work out to next targets, and measure quality) into three buckets: PRODUCTION-WIRED (a real, non-test caller drives it), TEST-ONLY (only the test suite constructs or calls it), and DECLARED-ONLY (a field or contract that nothing consumes). Headline result: the slice has a small, genuine spine that is really wired — the profile-selection catalog behind the loopback HTTP server, the runtime/topology bootstrap it composes, and exactly one of eleven quality-measurement "doors". But the marquee capability of the slice, the "dynamic graph edge target handoff" that fans learned work out to the next targets, is never produced in a non-test run; and nine of the eleven measurement doors, two whole helper classes, and three individual budget/stream fields are exercised only by tests or are hardwired to a dead value. So a large part of the graph slice is decorative-in-production: fully written, contract-complete, heavily unit-tested, and driven by nothing but the test suite.

**How "production" is defined here.** A caller counts as production if it is reachable from a runnable host: the library entrypoints in 15.OrchestratorModel (AgentLauncher, LogicalOrchestratorAssistant, TeachingSessionBuilder) or the loopback HTTP host in tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer. A caller that lives under Tests\ is test-only. This audit was read/grep only at HEAD 0f221ac89 — no build, no test run.

**(a) The edge-target handoff — produced-then-dropped, or fanned out? Answer: in a non-test run it is never produced, so the elaborate fan-out consumer is dead code outside tests.**

The record (the code). DynamicGraphEdgeTargetHandoff is defined at 00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:142 as an immutable "inference-only carrier" whose getters return fresh snapshots and whose TargetsExecuted and TopologyMutated properties are hardcoded to false (00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:214-216). It is a passive data record; it executes nothing itself.

The consumer that WOULD fan out (the code). AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.ConsumeEdgeTargetHandoffAsync (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:1833, called on the main run path at :373) is a genuine fan-out: it validates the handoff, resolves each ordered target's sub-phase and flow binding, then EXECUTES every target either in parallel or sequentially via ExecuteResolvedEdgeTargetAsync (:1947 parallel, :1974 sequential; the executor is at :2357). So the consumer is real and does drive OrderedTargets — it is not a stub.

But the producer never fires in production (the code). A handoff is only built by DynamicGraphEdgeFlow (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphEdgeFlow.cs:475 and :739), and DynamicGraphEdgeFlow is only run from ExecuteProjectedDynamicGraphEdgeAsync (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:1678), which returns early at :1500-1502 whenever transitionView.NodeOutputProjection is null. NodeOutputProjection is populated only on the "provided node outputs" path (RunWithInvocationNodeOutputsAsync, 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\AdaptiveLogicalOrchestrator.cs:230), never on the default path (RunAsync, :219, which passes useProvidedNodeOutputs:false at :224). RunWithInvocationNodeOutputsAsync is reached only through the internal RunPreparedDynamicGraphAsync (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.cs:648-669), whose only caller is 15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:523, and that invocation orchestrator is composed only by DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorComposition.CreateAxisSweepInvocation (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorComposition.cs:119/:151) — which has no production caller (only Tests\ reach it). The production entrypoints, AgentLauncher.cs:37 and LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.cs:631, both call the default RunAsync with no node-output source, so NodeOutputProjection stays null and no handoff is ever produced. Net: in a non-test run the handoff is neither fanned out nor "produced then dropped" — it is never produced. The validator IsValidEdgeTargetHandoff (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphRegistryL3SelectedTargetExecutor.cs:443-475) and the whole consumer chain are exercised only by tests.

A document claims more (the document vs the code). active-codex-execution-plan-03-orchestrator.md describes the orchestrator's duty to hand learned edge targets out to the next L4 targets; the code implements that fan-out completely but wires its trigger only to a test-only invocation entrypoint. That is an overclaim relative to the shipped wiring.

**(b) edgeAuthorityPreload — re-confirmed: no production caller passes a non-null preload.**

The door (the code). 15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.cs:395 loads trusted-host, already-learned edge-authority units when edgeAuthorityPreload is not null. The preload-bearing factory overloads are LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.CreateDefaultCore (:171/:197), DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorRuntime.CreateDefault (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorRuntime.cs:114) and CreateDefaultWithEdgeAuthorityPreload (:143), and DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorComposition.Create (:90) / CreateAxisSweepInvocation (:119) / CreateCore (:186/:203).

The callers (the code). Every production composition path passes NO preload: the loopback host and the machine factory go through the 2-argument Create (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorComposition.cs:60) -> ExecutorAdapter.Compose -> CreateCore (:174) -> runtime CreateDefault (null preload). A targeted grep for any production caller that supplies a preload or node-output argument returns empty; the only callers that pass a non-null preload are under Tests\ (Tests\DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestratorTests.cs:242, Tests\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorRuntimeTests.cs:246-323). RE-CONFIRMED at HEAD 0f221ac89: no production caller passes a non-null edgeAuthorityPreload. Classification: DECLARED-ONLY.

**(c) The profile catalog — there is no fixed profile set; it is a per-request selector, and every submitted profile is reachable.**

The code. DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorProfileCatalog (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorProfileCatalog.cs:24) does not define any built-in profiles. Its Select method (:93) deserializes a caller-supplied UTF-8 JSON array of profiles (:134), bounded to at most 64 root items (MaximumRootItemCount, :28), rejects duplicate ids, and returns the one whose ProfileId matches the requested id. The loopback endpoint POST /api/v1/dynamic-graph/catalog-sessions (tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\Program.cs:265) reads the request body and calls exactly this Select (tools\DynamicGraphCompositionLiveLocalServer\Program.cs:338) with the caller's selected_profile_id query. So the honest answer to "how many profiles exist and are they all reachable" is: zero are hardcoded; the catalog is request data (up to 64 profiles), and the endpoint forwards the whole submitted catalog to Select, so every submitted profile is reachable by id — there is no hidden or unreachable built-in profile. Classification: PRODUCTION-WIRED (the selector is driven by the loopback host). No overclaim in code.

**(d) Re-confirmation of the three Cycle-1 incidental items at this HEAD.**

- MaxParallelBranches consumed nowhere (the code). AsyncCombinationBudget.MaxParallelBranches (00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:561) is only validated (rejected when < 1 at 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\AxisSweepCombinationStrategy.cs:136, BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:510, SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:393), copied into snapshots/proofs (BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:220/:539, SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:446, DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:542), and equality-checked (DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:592). The actual degree of parallelism comes from MaxParallelCombinationRuns, not this field: parallelism = Math.Min(Budget.MaxParallelCombinationRuns, plans.Count) at 21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:135. (The identically named TopologyBuildBudget.MaxParallelBranches at 00.Core\DynamicGraphTopologyBuildContracts.cs:32 is a separate structural cap and is not this field.) RE-CONFIRMED DECLARED-ONLY. A document claims more: active-codex-execution-plan-06-async-combinations.md:22 states that "MaxParallelBranches means branch concurrency inside one traversal" — an overclaim relative to code that never uses it to bound concurrency.
- Always-null CandidateStream (the code). AsyncCandidateBatch.CandidateStream (00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:586) is constructed null at every production site (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:413, SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:306 and :501), has no production reader, and tests assert it null (Tests\SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs:111/:444). RE-CONFIRMED DECLARED-ONLY (always-null placeholder).
- Run-speed observation only tests construct (the code). DynamicGraphRunSpeedObservation (00.Core\DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:188) has no production construction; its only non-test mentions are parameter-type declarations on the meter (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphRunSpeedMeter.cs:23) and the coordinator door (21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphMeasurementCoordinator.cs:303). The only `new DynamicGraphRunSpeedObservation(...)` calls are in Tests\DynamicGraphRunSpeedMeterTests.cs:236-237 (and sibling tests). RE-CONFIRMED TEST-ONLY.

**(e) Sweep for graph-slice classes with zero production references.**

Method (the code). I enumerated 133 DynamicGraph* type declarations across 00.Core, 15.OrchestratorModel, 21.AgentInfrastructure, 19.DynamicGraphComposition.Contracts and tools\, then for each counted references outside its own definition file and outside Tests\. Two classes have zero production references beyond their own declaration and are called only by the test suite:

### Finding 20 — The project-wiki DPO runs: the first real trained DPO in the repository? (Cycle 3, Task 1)

**Plain-language framing.** "DPO" (Direct Preference Optimization) is a training method that nudges a model to prefer a "chosen" answer over a "rejected" one. Cycles 1-2 established that the plan's *graph* DPO chain — the typed pipeline the plan wants — is zero code. This finding looks at a *different* place: the folder `42.ProjectWikiDomain` and the data folder `43.LearningLocalWikiData\artifacts\runner-metrics\`, which appear to hold real DPO training runs. The question is whether real gradient training genuinely happened here, or whether this is another simulation.

**(1) TRAINS — this is a genuine gradient trainer, not a simulation.** The code runs real gradient steps. `ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner` (the class that drives one project-wiki DPO experiment) builds two live transformer models — a trainable "policy" and a frozen "reference" — and hands them to the numeric trainer: `new DpoTrainer(policy, reference, checkpointRoot, ...)` at 42.ProjectWikiDomain\ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:168, inside the train-and-evaluate entry method `TrainAndEvaluateAsync` at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:68, on the class declared at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:19. It then loops epochs times mini-batches and calls `trainer.StepMany(...)` at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:192. The trainer itself is real. In 11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs the DPO loss is computed arithmetically — the code `float loss = -MathF.Log(Sigmoid(margin) + 1e-8f);` sits at DpoTrainer.cs:54, where `margin` is the chosen-minus-rejected reward built from policy and reference log-probabilities at DpoTrainer.cs:49-51. The backward pass then pushes gradients into the model — `BackwardLogProbs(...)` at DpoTrainer.cs:58-59 (and DpoTrainer.cs:107-108 for the batched path) accumulates a softmax-minus-one-hot logits gradient and calls `logits.Backward(...)` at DpoTrainer.cs:322 — and the weights are actually updated by an optimizer step: the code `_optimizer.Step(_policy.Parameters(), lr: _learningRate);` sits at DpoTrainer.cs:61 (batched path at DpoTrainer.cs:117), using an AdamW optimizer constructed at DpoTrainer.cs:32. Per-step metrics are written to disk as `dpo_step_NNNNNN.json` by `SaveMetrics` at DpoTrainer.cs:338, and a per-step weight checkpoint `dpo_policy_step_NNNNNN.bin` is saved by `SavePolicyCheckpoint` at DpoTrainer.cs:346-347. So the code does real gradient descent (loss, backward, AdamW step) on a small transformer — a 1-layer, 64-hidden model per the runner defaults at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:44-49 — not a random-number simulation.

**(2) SAVES + real numbers — two artifacts, read honestly.** Artifact A is the run summary at 43.LearningLocalWikiData\artifacts\runner-metrics\2026-05-15-code-trace-batch-001\dpo-runs\code-trace-builder-unit\code-trace-builder-flow-batch-001-code-trace-builder-unit-20260515134605\project-wiki-dpo-metric-summary.json. The file records `trainingRowCount` 50 and `heldOutRowCount` 50 (summary lines :8-9), `finalLoss` 0.6913825 (:10), `finalAccuracy` 0.62 (:11), `finalRewardMargin` 0.003609 (:12), `heldOutLoss` 0.692244 (:13), `heldOutAccuracy` 0.60 (:14), `heldOutRewardMargin` 0.0018717 (:15), plus resource telemetry (`peakWorkingSetMb` 102.9, `totalElapsedMs` 212568, i.e. about 3.5 minutes of wall time). Artifact B is one per-step checkpoint metric file, checkpoints\dpo_step_000010.json, which holds a single real record: `{"Step":10,"Loss":0.6884302,"ChosenReward":0.014881,"RejectedReward":0.0053099,"RewardMargin":0.009571,"Accuracy":0.6875}`. The final weight file checkpoints\policy_final.bin exists and is 1,188,744 bytes (about 1.13 MB) — a real serialized model, not a zero-byte placeholder. Honest interpretation: a loss of ~0.69 is essentially ln(2) = 0.6931, which means the model is only barely better than a coin flip; held-out accuracy 0.60 and a *tiny positive* held-out reward margin (~0.0019) are a weak-but-real learning signal — on average the trained policy does prefer chosen over rejected on unseen pairs, but only slightly. The run did measurably move: batch-level reward margins swing between -0.0032 and +0.0096 across the 20 logged mini-batches (summary :27 through :255) and the held-out margin stays positive. So this is real training with a real held-out measurement, but a *marginal* effect size.

**(3) RELOADS-FRESH — no fresh-load re-evaluation of the saved policy.** The held-out evaluation is done on the *same in-memory* trained policy object at save-time: the code `trainer.EvaluateMany(heldOutRows...)` sits at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:210, immediately before the weights are written out at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:221. There is no code path that loads `policy_final.bin` back into a fresh model and re-scores it. Evidence: a whole-repository grep for `policy_final` returns exactly one hit — the *write* site at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:219 — and zero read sites. The runner does contain a checkpoint-load capability (the code `policy.LoadCheckpoint(...)` at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:142 and reference at :151), but it is gated on the constructor parameters `initialPolicyCheckpointPath` / `initialReferenceCheckpointPath`, and every one of the ten pipelines that construct the runner passes only `epochCount` / `learningRate` / `deterministicFreshInitialization` and never a checkpoint path (for example CodeTraceBuilderTrainingPipeline.cs:43 and ProjectWikiRequestRoutingTrainingPipeline.cs:30). So in practice the reload path is dead for these runs — every run ends at save-time with no fresh-load proof. This is the same gap the plan itself lists as still open: a document claim at active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:12 states that "lifecycle/fresh-load/continue-training ... gates remain OPEN."

**(4) LEGALITY + MAPPING.** First, the legality terms are absent from the project-wiki code. A case-insensitive grep of 42.ProjectWikiDomain for `provenance`, `teacher`, and `quarantine` returns zero hits in real logic; the only matches for `legal` / `license` / `copyright` are the AGPL file-header comment block present on every file (lines 1-4 of each `.cs`) plus one business-domain token `"license"` inside an unrelated word list at CrossStackBusinessFlowGraphDpoGenerator.cs:81. So the code enforces no provenance gate, no teacher-output gate, and no quarantine gate in this subsystem. Second, the pairs are self-authored from deterministic seed templates. The method `CodeTraceBuilderDpoGenerator.BuildSeeds` (CodeTraceBuilderDpoGenerator.cs:357-408) hardcodes fixed domain-name lists (training domains "Campaign","Order",... and held-out domains "Invoice","Profile",... at CodeTraceBuilderDpoGenerator.cs:360-361), synthesizes the C# "source" text inline via a template at CodeTraceBuilderDpoGenerator.cs:371-398, then builds the `chosen` and `rejected` strings deterministically (`BuildChosen` at :77, `BuildRejected` at :120), where each rejected string carries a `[WRONG: ...]` annotation that the runner then *enforces* at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:318. No external corpus, no scraped code, and no external teacher model is read anywhere. This matches the plan's stated permission — a document claim at active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:11 (the `authoring_boundary` line) says DPO chosen/rejected material "may be authored ... by the project's own deterministic ... route." Third, the mapping: this subsystem is a PARALLEL, self-contained numeric DPO, not the plan's typed target chain, and at most a stepping stone toward it. The code fact is that the runner uses its own private path — `ProjectWikiDpoMetricPair` to `DpoBatch` to `DpoTrainer.StepMany` (ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:191-192). The document claim is that the plan's *target* is a different, typed chain: active-codex-execution-plan-07-dpo-builder.md:29 lists "GraphDpoSourceRow -> GraphDpoPairCandidate -> TriadLegalProjection ... -> GraphDpoPair(Level=unit) -> ... DpoDataset.Load -> TrainBatchAsync -> checkpoint manifest -> promotion gate" — the typed chain that Finding 5 found is absent. The project-wiki runner touches none of those named types (no `GraphDpoPair`, no `TriadLegalProjection`, no `DpoDataset.Load`, no `TrainBatchAsync`), so it does not implement the plan's target; it is a working prototype of the *idea* (gradient DPO on self-authored code-trace pairs) that the graph pipeline could later consume, but today it is wired independently. And a document claim at active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:5-6 records `dpo_status: NOT_STARTED` and `dpo_priority: LAST` for the plan's DPO — meaning the plan does not yet count this project-wiki work as its DPO deliverable.

**Sibling — OrchestrationPlanMachine (a different mechanism entirely).** The sibling class `OrchestrationPlanMachine` (42.ProjectWikiDomain\OrchestrationPlanMachine.cs:12) is NOT a gradient DPO trainer; it is a nearest-neighbour / pattern-map "learning machine" (it holds `OrchestrationKnnStore` KNN stores and a `PromptFlowGroundingModel`, its fields declared at OrchestrationPlanMachine.cs:20-23, and it implements the `ILearningMachine` interface). Running the same four-part test on it: (a) TRAINS = it "learns" by memorising pairs into KNN stores and promoting pair IDs — that is retrieval/memorisation, not gradient descent; (b) SAVES = its published artifact folder models\orchestration\orchestration-v1\ holds a 580,003-byte JSON checkpoint `orchestration-v1-checkpoint.json` full of `knn` and `weights` arrays, plus a manifest; (c) RELOADS = unlike the DPO runner, its reload path is real and *actually invoked* — the code `OrchestrationPlanMachine.LoadCheckpointAsync(...)` (declared at OrchestrationPlanMachine.cs:420) is called from OrchestrationPilotLoopRunner.cs:49; (d) MEASURES = it reports held-out-style numbers. Honest reading of its accuracy: the manifest claims `accuracy_at_export: 0.9` (models\orchestration\orchestration-v1\manifest.json:9), but the run report shows that figure is dominated by in-sample recall — `learned_flow_grounding_accuracy: 1` and `human_explanation_accuracy: 1` (perfect, i.e. memorisation), with only `unseen_style_accuracy: 0.8` as a genuine held-out generalisation number and `exact_prompt_leakage_rate: 0` (runner-report.json:6-9). So the 0.9 headline is a KNN retrieval-accuracy figure, not a DPO reward margin, and its high value is largely memorisation with one modest (0.8) held-out slice.

**Honesty nuance — where the runs actually executed.** The summary's `resultRoot` is an absolute path *outside* this repository: `C:\Users\lubao\Documents\xiigen\llm mvp\orchestrator extension\runner-metrics\...` (project-wiki-dpo-metric-summary.json:5-7). This implies the runs were executed in the user's personal Documents working area (an "orchestrator extension" scratch location), and the copies now living under `43.LearningLocalWikiData\artifacts\runner-metrics\` are IMPORTED SNAPSHOTS, not evidence generated in place inside the repo. Two corroborating facts: the imported run directory kept only the metrics JSON files plus `policy_final.bin`, while the per-step `dpo_policy_step_*.bin` checkpoints and the `reference_base.bin` that the code writes (DpoTrainer.cs:346-347 and ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:220-222) are absent — a curated import, not a raw run directory. The runner *code* is present and could regenerate equivalent runs, so the numbers are reproducible in principle; but these specific in-repo files are imported artifacts consistent with the code, rather than in-repo-rooted proof produced by a run under the repository.

**Verdict:** project-wiki DPO = a REAL trained numeric DPO — it genuinely trains (gradient loss/backward/AdamW step at DpoTrainer.cs:54/58-61), saves (policy_final.bin, 1,188,744 bytes) and measures a held-out set (heldOutAccuracy 0.60, heldOutRewardMargin ~0.0019) — but the learning signal is weak (loss ~ ln2, margin barely positive), it does NOT reload the saved weights into a fresh model to re-prove them (policy_final.bin is write-only, one grep hit at ProjectWikiDpoMetricRunner.cs:219), it enforces NO legality gate (provenance/teacher/quarantine absent; pairs are self-authored deterministic seed templates, which the plan permits at active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:11), and it maps to the plan's graph-DPO target only as an independent PARALLEL prototype / stepping stone, not the typed GraphDpoPair to TrainBatchAsync chain (which remains absent per Finding 5, target at active-codex-execution-plan-07-dpo-builder.md:29); the sibling OrchestrationPlanMachine is a separate KNN memorisation machine whose 0.9 accuracy is mostly in-sample recall (unseen-style held-out only 0.8), and both subsystems' in-repo artifacts are imported snapshots (resultRoot under C:\Users\lubao\Documents\...), reproducible from present code but not in-repo-rooted evidence.


### Finding 21 — The capability trainer: what does the one production isLearned:true prove? (Cycle 3, Task 2)

**What this settles.** Cycle 1 (Finding 2) established that the only production code that stamps a logical unit as "learned" — a boolean flag `isLearned: true` meaning "this unit's answers come from a training checkpoint, not a fixed hand-written rule" — is the capability trainer in `18.CapabilityTraining`. This finding traces, line by line, what that stamp actually proves. Short answer up front: it proves that a gradient DPO optimizer step ran and a checkpoint file was written; it does NOT prove that the trained model got measurably better on any held-out (previously unseen) test set.

**(1) Does it genuinely TRAIN before it registers?** Yes, a real gradient step runs — but no before/after gain is ever measured. The code fact is that the trainer's DPO path is real: `18.CapabilityTraining\LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:360` constructs `new DpoTrainer(...)` (the actual Direct-Preference-Optimization trainer — the component that nudges the model to prefer a "chosen" answer over a "rejected" one), and `:377` calls `trainer.StepVector(batches, step)`, which is the genuine gradient optimizer update; the path labels itself with the ingredient-id string `"11.DPO:DpoTrainer.StepVector"` at `:403` (an ingredient-id is just a human-readable tag naming which real sub-component did the work). The code fact is that this DPO step is invoked BEFORE any registration: `18.CapabilityTraining\DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:213` calls `ExecuteDpoTeachingAsync(...)`, and the taught weights are written to disk at `:281` via `policyModel.SaveCheckpoint(policyCheckpointPath)` (a checkpoint = the serialized model-weights file).

But the code fact is that NOTHING measures a before/after gain between the DPO step and the `isLearned: true` stamp. The flag is set at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:334-341` (`request.Units.Register<string, string>(..., isLearned: true, learnedUnit.Value!)`) unconditionally, as soon as three things merely succeeded without throwing: the DPO step returned success (`dpoResult.IsSuccess`, checked at `:227`), the checkpoint saved, and the model got wrapped into a unit (`:298`). The loss and accuracy numbers the DPO step produces (`lastMetrics.Loss`, `lastMetrics.Accuracy`, built at `LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:394-395`) are only recorded into evidence-file paths at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:233` — they are never compared against a threshold or a prior value, and never gate the registration. The one reload that exists anywhere in the path is NOT a re-evaluation of the just-saved model: `LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:349` loads the PARENT checkpoint (`request.Input.PolicyModel.LoadCheckpoint(request.Input.ParentPolicyCheckpointPath)`) in order to CONTINUE training from a prior row, not to score the fresh one. Corroborating code fact: a grep of the entire `18.CapabilityTraining` directory for `held-out`/`heldOut` returns zero hits. The second `isLearned: true` site at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:808-815` behaves the same way — it registers a domain-classifier unit (trained by `classifier.Train(...)` at `:805`, a predicate-profile-to-domain mapper) as learned, again with no held-out measurement.

The test fact confirms this reading: `Tests\DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainerTests.cs` (read, not run) asserts only that a per-step metrics file exists (`Assert.NotEmpty(Directory.GetFiles(..., "dpo_step_*.json", ...))` at `:78`), that at least one DPO step ran (`BatchedDpoStepCount >= 1` at `:233`), that the step took wall-time (`timing.DpoMs > 0` at `:235`), and that the unit's flag reads `IsLearned == true` (`:66`). No test asserts any accuracy gain, loss threshold, or held-out comparison.

**(2) What is the registered "generated-capability" unit AT INFERENCE TIME?** It is a nearest-neighbour LOOKUP over stored template answers, ranked by the trained embedder — not a generator producing novel text from the checkpoint. The code fact is that the object registered as the learned unit (`learnedUnit.Value!` passed at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:341`) is a `TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit<string>`, built at `:289` (`TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit<string>.Train(...)`). "KNN" = k-nearest-neighbour, a retrieval method that answers a query by finding the closest stored example. The code fact for what it does at inference is `LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:134` (`ExecuteAsync`): it embeds the incoming query with the DPO-trained tiny model (`:147`), searches an HNSW nearest-neighbour index (`:150` — HNSW is just a fast approximate-nearest-neighbour data structure), fuses a vector-similarity + BM25-lexical + pattern score (`:203`), and then RETURNS `example.Output` at `:168` — the STORED output text of the winning training example. The code fact for where those stored outputs come from is `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:753` (`BuildKnnTrainingExamples`): each example's output is a templated "chosen" string produced by `BuildChosenCapabilityText` at `:685` (literally `"Generated capability for sub-phase {id}: {name}. Execute the requested work through task type {t}."`) or a prior row's stored output text. The code fact for the final step is `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:1110`: the flow passes that retrieved string through `CapabilityResultTranslator.Translate(...)` / `TranslateFromKnnOutput(...)` (`:1128` / `:1132`), a deterministic string transform. So the learned state at inference is split in two: the trained checkpoint weights only decide WHICH stored example ranks first (they act as the embedder/ranker), while the actual ANSWER is retrieved verbatim from a lookup table of templated strings and then deterministically reshaped. Notably the KNN unit's own manifest declares `DpoStepCount: 0` at `LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:120` — the unit itself carries zero DPO steps; the DPO touched only the embedding model it borrows. (The token "generated_capability" the task references is the node-name field in the flow summary at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:1099`; the unit-role constant `"generated-capability"` is defined at `:20`.)

**(3) MAPPING to the plan's learned EDGE-AUTHORITY requirement — a different kind of unit.** The document claim is that the plan demands a learned edge-authority unit — a trainable trust/authority decision that lives ON a graph edge (an "edge" is a directed link between two work-steps; "authority" is the final say over which target to route to and in what order). `active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:24` titles the plan block "LEARNABLE edge authority" (`ОБУЧАЕМАЯ авторитетность ребра`), and `:545` describes `EdgeAuthorityUnit` as a NEW skeleton targeting `PASS_AS_TRAINED_FEATURE` whose input must physically EXCLUDE six leakage fields (`candidate_index, source_path, source_id, file_order, correlation_id, exact_prompt`) and which holds "a K-NN retriever as a model-component inside", flagged `IsLearned=true`. The document claim continues that this unit has a typed contract `EdgeAuthorityUnit : ILogicalUnit<EdgeAuthorityInput, EdgeAuthorityDecision>` (`active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:741-746`), is trained ONLY by `ILearningMachine<DynamicGraphDomain>` (the single declared owner of training, `:591`), and that its training must produce a measured `LearningBatchResult{ AccuracyAfter > Before, RewardMargin > 0 }` on a SOURCE-DISJOINT held-out set (`:639`, `:1047`).

Against that, the code fact is that the capability path's unit cannot satisfy the requirement, because it is a different unit in five concrete ways: (a) wrong role — it is registered under role `"generated-capability"` (`DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:20`), not `"edge-authority"`; (b) wrong contract — it is `string -> string` (`Register<string, string>` at `:334`), not `EdgeAuthorityInput -> EdgeAuthorityDecision`; (c) no leakage guard — it embeds the raw query text directly (`LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:147`) and constructs no leakage-free input, so none of the six forbidden fields are excluded; (d) wrong trainer/owner — it is produced by `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer`, not the plan's mandated `ILearningMachine<DynamicGraphDomain>`; (e) no held-out gain — as established in part (1), nothing measures `AccuracyAfter > Before`, which is the very thing that would make it "trained" in the plan's sense. The one point of genuine overlap is that the plan's own `:545` wants "a K-NN retriever as a model-component INSIDE" the edge-authority unit — so this capability trainer's `TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit` is the same FAMILY of mechanism the plan envisions embedding, but delivered here as a standalone string capability unit rather than the typed, leakage-guarded, held-out-measured edge-authority unit itself. It is a precursor/analogue, not a fulfillment.

**(4) LEGALITY — none enforced here.** The code fact is that `18.CapabilityTraining` enforces no legality at all: a case-insensitive grep of the whole directory for `provenance`, `teacher`, and `quarantine` returns zero hits, and the only matches for `legal`/`licen` are the AGPL-3.0 license-header comment block at the top of every file (lines 1-4). There is no source-license check on the material it teaches from, no record of which teacher/model produced the training text, and no quarantine path for untrusted input. This is a gap relative to the plan, whose document claim repeatedly requires a "Legal Teacher Gate" and a `TeacherProvenance` record (`active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:59`, `:89`, and the async-fan-out legal/provenance requirement at `:1370`). The trainer teaches from whatever branch text and compiled prompt it is handed, with no legality gate standing between input and checkpoint.

**Verdict:** capability trainer isLearned = a WEAK registration, not proof of a held-out gain — it does run a genuine gradient DPO step (`DpoTrainer` at `LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:360`, `StepVector` at `:377`) and does save a checkpoint (`SaveCheckpoint` at `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:281`), but it stamps `isLearned: true` (`:340`, and again for the domain classifier at `:814`) purely on "DPO step ran + checkpoint written + unit wrapped", with NO reload-and-re-score and NO before/after held-out measurement (zero `heldOut` hits in the directory; the tests assert only that a `dpo_step_*.json` file exists, `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainerTests.cs:78`); at inference the registered "generated-capability" unit is a transformer-embedding K-NN LOOKUP that returns a stored templated answer (`LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:168`) ranked by the trained embedder and then deterministically translated (`DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:1128`), i.e. a retrieval unit, not a checkpoint-generated answer; it CANNOT satisfy the plan's learned edge-authority requirement (`active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:24`, `:545`) because it is a different unit — role `generated-capability` not `edge-authority`, contract `string->string` not `EdgeAuthorityInput->EdgeAuthorityDecision`, no leakage-6 exclusion, wrong trainer, and no `AccuracyAfter>Before` gain — at best the same K-NN family the plan wants embedded inside the real unit; and it enforces NO legality (no provenance/teacher/quarantine anywhere in `18.CapabilityTraining`; only the AGPL header), unlike the plan's required Legal Teacher Gate.


### Finding 22 — The translation machine: real checkpoint lifecycle, memorize-and-match learning (Cycle 3, Task 3)

**Plain-language framing.** "The translation machine" is a self-contained C# class named `TranslationMachine` (in `27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs`) that implements the full learn / evaluate / save / load lifecycle contract `ILearningMachine` — the same lifecycle the project's dynamic-graph domain deliberately refuses to complete (it "fail-closes"). This finding checks three things: is that lifecycle honest, what kind of "learning" it actually performs, and whether it enforces any legality.

**(1) LIFECYCLE HONESTY.**

*The cited train method at line 50 is NOT the real learning path — and it says so itself.* The code fact is that `TranslationMachine.TrainBatchAsync` (`27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:50`) takes the old flat "one training row" batch shape and, instead of touching any learned table, just nudges a stored accuracy number up by a hundredth: `accuracyAfter = Math.Min(accuracyBefore + 0.01, 1.0)` (`:69`), and its own comment calls this the "Legacy scaffold path ... this path does not claim unit-level translation learning" (`:68`). So the method at line 50 does NOT genuinely mutate learned state; it is an honestly-labelled placeholder.

*The real training path lives next door and does mutate learned state.* The code fact is that `TranslationMachine.TrainStructuredBatchAsync` (`:88`) loops over the non-held-out examples and calls `trainer.TrainOnExample(example)` (`:111`), and `ExecutorUnit.TrainOnExample` (`27.TranslationDomain/TranslationTrainedUnits.cs:404`) writes the chosen answer into two real in-memory tables: an exact source→answer map (`_exactSourceMap[exactSourceKey] = chosen.Text`, `:415`) and a signature→answer K-NN store (`_knnStore.AddPair(...)`, `:418`). "K-NN" here means "k-nearest-neighbour": a lookup that finds the closest stored signature and returns its saved answer. So genuine state mutation happens — but only on the structured path, not the line-50 path the task cited.

*Evaluate does measure on held-out rows, but only after structured training.* The code fact is that `TranslationMachine.EvaluateAsync` (`:152`) re-runs each evaluation prompt through the real inference orchestrator and counts exact-string matches to compute `accuracy = correct / count` (`:172`–`:180`) — but only when the flag `_hasStructuredTraining` is true (`:167`); otherwise it just echoes the stored scalar with no measurement (`:166`). The held-out split itself is real: `TrainStructuredBatchAsync` trains only on `!example.HeldOut` rows (`:106`) and measures accuracy on the `example.HeldOut` rows via `MeasureHeldOutAccuracy` (`:123`–`:125`, and the method at `:503`). So evaluation is honest measurement, conditioned on real training having happened.

*The metadata export/import is honestly bounded and cannot be mistaken for a weight load.* The code fact is that `ExportAsync` (`:204`) returns a small metadata record whose checkpoint pointer is a placeholder string, `CheckpointRef: $"{MachineId}/{_currentVersion}/checkpoint"` (`:216`), with the comment "CheckpointRef here is not a file path — it is a placeholder" (`:210`); and `ImportAsync` (`:227`) restores only two scalars — `_accuracyAfterLastBatch = export.AccuracyAtExport` (`:250`) and `_currentVersion = export.Version` (`:251`) — never touching any learned table, with the comment "restores only scalar metadata ... not a trained-model load path" (`:224`–`:226`). The document claim it is held against is in the plan file `active-codex-execution-plan-09-auto-dpo-loop.md:10`, which states `restore_canon: ImportAsync(metadata) -> LoadCheckpointAsync(weights); metadata import never substitutes for trained-weight restore.` The code honours this: the metadata import is clearly labelled and structurally incapable of loading weights.

*A real fresh-load-then-evaluate path exists and is separate.* The code fact is that the true weight round-trip goes through `ExportCheckpoint` (`:259`) / `ImportCheckpoint` (`:306`), which call `_runtime.KnnStore.ExportCheckpoint()` and `ImportCheckpoint(...)` (`:289`, `:338`) plus the tokenizer, executors and verifier, and that `LoadCheckpointAsync` (`:452`) reads the JSON file, builds a brand-new machine `new TranslationMachine()` (`:484`), and restores every learned table into it via `ImportCheckpoint` (`:485`) — its comment says "This is the only valid post-restart load path. No replay or retraining is performed" (`:450`). The code fact is that this fresh-load path is actually exercised by the loop runner: `TatoebaPilotLoopRunner` calls `TranslationMachine.LoadCheckpointAsync(...)` (`27.TranslationDomain/TatoebaPilotLoopRunner.cs:194`), and on a checkpoint resume sets `initialTrainingSkippedDueCheckpoint = true` (`:256`), i.e. it continues from restored weights without retraining, then measures a `RuntimeProductAccuracy` per cycle (`:729`). Separately, the held-out runner `TranslationProductHeldOutRunner` trains a fresh machine (`TranslationProductHeldOutRunner.cs:126`) and evaluates it against a contamination-checked held-out file, rejecting the run with error code `TRAIN_HELDOUT_OVERLAP` if any held-out input also appears in training (`:115`, `:505`). So a real fresh-load AND a real held-out evaluation both exist, distinct from the metadata import.

**(2) MECHANISM CLASSIFICATION — memorize-and-match, and honest about it.**

The code fact is that at inference `ExecutorUnit.Execute` tries the memorized literal table first: `if (_exactSourceMap.TryGetValue(normalizedSource, out var exactTranslation)) return new TranslationCandidate(exactTranslation, 0.93, ...)` (`TranslationTrainedUnits.cs:267`), then a phrase map, a word map, and finally a K-NN signature lookup (`:270`–`:283`); its own comment describes the path as "KNN lookup → exact match → return; fallback → E26 word map + E27 reorder" (`:263`). The published artifact confirms this shape: the checkpoint file `models/translation/translation-v1/translation-v1-checkpoint.json` contains a `knn_store.entries` list of signature→answer rows (`:9`–`:27`), an `executors[0].exact_source_map` holding one literal input→output row `{ "key": "add null check validatetoken", "value": "Add a null check to ValidateToken." }` (`:38`–`:44`), and `accuracy_after_last_batch: 0.333...` (`:6`) — one memorized sentence, three K-NN signatures for that same sentence, and every other map empty. This is a memorize-and-match mechanism, not semantic understanding.

This matters because the code fact in `00.Core/DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:25`–`:32` is that the graph domain's measurement law lists `Knn`, `Scorer`, `Counter`, `Regex` as mechanism kinds that are NOT a `LearningSubject`, with the comment "the other values are explicit forbidden-shortcut evidence for the admission boundary" — i.e. K-NN and exact-lookup are exactly the shortcuts the graph forbids for a graph/semantic claim (this is the Finding 18 law). The question is whether translation OVERCLAIMS. The code fact is that a case-insensitive search for `semantic_quality` across all of `27.TranslationDomain` returns zero hits — the code makes no semantic-quality claim. The manifest fact is that `models/translation/translation-v1/manifest.json` reports only `accuracy_at_export: 0.333...` (`:9`) and `loader_type: "TranslationMachine"` (`:10`) with NO `semantic_quality_claimed` field at all, and the registry `models/index.json` lists translation-v1 with a bare `accuracy: 0.333...` (`:127`) and, like the thin `orchestration-v1` row, carries NO `semantic_quality_claimed` field — whereas the genuinely-trained models in the same index (orchestration-reasoning-v1, prompt-to-task-v1, skill-selection-v1) each explicitly stamp `semantic_quality_claimed: false` (`index.json:17`, `:46`, `:95`). So no field anywhere asserts `semantic_quality_claimed: true`; the only claim made is a bare `accuracy` number (0.333 = one-third, matching the checkpoint), an honest lexical-match score, not a semantic claim. Translation neither claims semantic learning nor carries the explicit `semantic_quality_claimed: false` disclaimer the trained models carry — an absence, not an overclaim.

**(3) MAPPING + LEGALITY.**

*No teacher / provenance / quarantine legality is enforced inside the domain.* The code fact is that a case-insensitive search of every `.cs` file in `27.TranslationDomain` for `quarantine`, `provenance`, and the whole word `teacher` returns zero hits. What legality DOES exist is corpus-LICENSE enforcement, not teacher legality: `TranslationCorpusSourcing.cs` holds an allowed-license set (`DefaultAllowedLicenses`, `:62`) and rejects any source row whose license is not on it with reason `"license_not_allowed"` (`:221`–`:222`), and `WriteAttributionAsync` (`:735`) emits an attribution manifest recording `license = "CC BY 2.0 France"`, `attribution_required = true`, and a `raw_text_policy` that "raw sentence text is private; git-safe artifacts store IDs, patterns, and attribution only" (`:742`–`:748`). So the subsystem gates the legality of its *source corpus* (license + attribution + raw-text privacy), but it does NOT implement the graph's teacher/provenance/quarantine gate. The document claim it is measured against is `active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:11`, whose `authoring_boundary` says DPO chosen/rejected material may be authored "only by the project's own deterministic graph/self-best route or by a permitted open-weight model after [the gates] pass," and that closed proprietary APIs "never author chosen, rejected, a DPO label, or a training target." Translation authors its own chosen/rejected pairs deterministically from its own license-checked corpus, so it sits inside that authoring boundary — but the boundary's teacher-provenance machinery is a graph concern this domain does not touch.

*Mapping: a PARALLEL learner, not a stepping stone into the graph's DPO.* The code fact is that `TranslationMachine` is its own machine (`MachineId = "translation-machine"`, `TranslationMachine.cs:14`) implementing the shared `ILearningMachine<TranslationDomain>` lifecycle, and it neither declares a `DynamicGraphMeasurementTarget` nor feeds the graph's DPO — it reuses the DPO-pair vocabulary (chosen / rejected / reward margin) in isolation. The `[WRONG: weakness - gap - consequence]` string named in the Cycle-1 crumb is documented at `27.TranslationDomain/TranslationUnitContracts.cs:140` as the annotation on the "RejectedOutputJson ... non-preferred output" — i.e. the rejected half of a DPO-style chosen/rejected preference pair. The code fact is what it is actually used for: batch builders write it into the rejected field (`TranslationStructuredBatchBuilder.cs:166`, `TranslationFrequencyQuoteMap.cs:130`, `TatoebaPilotLoopRunner.cs:1147`) and parse the label back out with a regex (`TranslationStructuredBatchBuilder.cs:546`); and `TrainStructuredBatchAsync` grants a non-zero `rewardMargin` of `0.03` only when EVERY example's rejected output contains the marker, else `0.0` (`TranslationMachine.cs:128`–`:131`). Critically, the training write `TrainOnExample` reads only `example.ChosenOutputJson` (`TranslationTrainedUnits.cs:410`, `:415`) and never the rejected text — so the `[WRONG:]` marker is a well-formedness gate on a *reported* reward-margin telemetry constant, not an actual preference gradient. The "learning" is memorize-the-chosen; the rejected / `[WRONG:]` side is DPO-shaped scaffolding that does not drive any weight update.

**Verdict:** translation machine = an HONEST, fully-wired checkpoint lifecycle whose "learning" is lexical memorize-and-match, not semantic. The cited `TrainBatchAsync` at line 50 is a self-labelled legacy scaffold (`+0.01`, no state change) while the real mutation is `TrainStructuredBatchAsync`→`TrainOnExample` writing an exact-source table plus a K-NN signature store (`TranslationTrainedUnits.cs:415`, `:418`); `EvaluateAsync` genuinely measures on a contamination-checked held-out split (`TranslationMachine.cs:172`; `TranslationProductHeldOutRunner.cs:115`); the metadata `ImportAsync` is honestly bounded to two scalars and structurally cannot load weights (`:250`–`:251`, comment `:224`–`:226`), while the real fresh-load-then-evaluate exists via `LoadCheckpointAsync`→`new TranslationMachine()`→`ImportCheckpoint` and is exercised by `TatoebaPilotLoopRunner` (`:452`, `:484`; `TatoebaPilotLoopRunner.cs:194`, `:256`) — satisfying plan-09:10's `ImportAsync(metadata) -> LoadCheckpointAsync(weights)` canon; the mechanism is the K-NN + exact-lookup pair the graph names FORBIDDEN shortcuts (`00.Core/DynamicGraphMeasurementContracts.cs:25`–`:32`), but translation is a SEPARATE domain that makes NO graph/semantic claim (`semantic_quality` absent from all code, manifest and index; only a bare honest `accuracy: 0.333`) and therefore does not overclaim; it enforces corpus-LICENSE legality (allowed-license reject + attribution manifest — `TranslationCorpusSourcing.cs:62`, `:221`, `:735`) but NOT teacher/provenance/quarantine legality (zero hits); and it maps to the plan as a PARALLEL DPO-shaped learner whose `[WRONG:]` rejected annotation (`TranslationUnitContracts.cs:140`) gates only a reported reward-margin constant and never drives a weight update — not a stepping stone feeding the graph's DPO.


### Finding 23 — Smart-skills machines: curated patterns or trained models? (Cycle 3, Task 4)

This finding audits the three "machine" families in the smart-skills domain (folder `43.SmartSkillsDomain`, the project's collection of prompt/skill helpers) that publish artifacts under `models\`: PromptToTaskMachine (turns a raw prompt into a structured task), OrchestrationReasoningMachine (produces reasoning-plan guidance) with its OrchestrationReasoningTrainer, and SkillSelectionMachine (picks which skills to load). The question is whether the published "models" are trained neural models with mutating weights, or curated pattern lists that get materialized from data files.

**(1) WHAT KIND OF LEARNING — no gradient anywhere; "training" means materializing curated pattern/count state.**

The code fact is that a search across the whole `43.SmartSkillsDomain` folder for any of `gradient`, `optimizer`, `StepVector`, `DpoTrainer`, `backprop`, `learningRate`, `Adam`, `softmax`, or `logit` returns zero matches. There is no numeric weight-mutation step, no loss, no optimizer object anywhere in the domain. Every "trained" artifact is produced by copying authored data into a typed state record, not by descending a gradient.

For PromptToTask the code fact is that a "learned pattern" is a hand-authored record: `PromptToTaskLearnedPattern` (PromptToTaskMachine.cs:23-34) is a named list of signal words plus a `Confidence` double, and the checkpoint manifest type at PromptToTaskMachine.cs:49 (`PromptToTaskCheckpointManifest`) simply records a `LearnedPatternCount` and a `SemanticQualityClaimed` flag. The code fact is that `ExportLearnedState` (PromptToTaskMachine.cs:220) does no computation — it wraps the already-loaded descriptor into a checkpoint — and `Translate` (PromptToTaskMachine.cs:117) answers by matching the request's redacted signals against those authored patterns, with the machine's own default status being `not_trained_no_loaded_state` (PromptToTaskMachine.cs:85). The data fact is that the published checkpoint `models\prompt-to-task\prompt-to-task-v1\prompt-to-task-v1-checkpoint.json` is exactly a JSON list of twelve `learnedPatterns`, each a `learnedSignals` word-list with a scalar `confidence` (for example `q3-01-prototype-planning-approval-boundary`, confidence 0.95, at lines 12-54); the confidence numbers 0.72-0.95 are written into the file, not derived by fitting. So for PromptToTask "training" is materializing a curated pattern list, not weight learning.

For OrchestrationReasoning the code fact is even plainer in the naming: the trainer's entry method is called `MaterializeAsync` (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:34), not Train. It reads NDJSON payload rows, builds `OrchestrationReasoningLearnedPattern` records with a hard-coded `Confidence: 0.8` (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:187), and returns a report (`OrchestrationReasoningTrainerReport`, OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:13) whose flags are set to `TrainingStarted: false`, `ModelAssetCreated: false`, `CheckpointCreated: false` (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:97-99). The report carries a `TrainingStarted` flag precisely so downstream steps can assert no gradient training happened.

For SkillSelection the code fact is that "training" is counting. `LearnPair` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:737) adds the pair id and a hashed prompt key to sets, routes the pair to a per-unit `Learn*Pair` method that accumulates integer feature weights into typed skill-profile states, and increments an integer candidate-position tally: `_candidatePositionWeights[index] = _candidatePositionWeights.GetValueOrDefault(index) + 1` over a `Dictionary<int,int>` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:70, :772). The test evidence confirms these are signed integer tallies, not floats from an optimizer: a chosen skill's feature weights are all positive while the rejected skill's are all negative (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:685, :687). This is a perceptron-style count-and-KNN memory, not a trained network.

The code fact is that the honesty flags are consumed as hard load-time gates, not decoration. A checkpoint or manifest that dishonestly set `SemanticQualityClaimed` true is rejected: PromptToTaskMachine.cs:634 fails validation if `checkpoint.SemanticQualityClaimed` is set, and PromptToTaskMachine.cs:714 fails if the manifest claims it; OrchestrationReasoningMachine.cs:639 and :724 apply the same rejection. The `external_ai_used` and `training_started` flags are enforced too: the OrchestrationReasoning trainer aborts the whole run if the manifest reports `external_ai_used` (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:63-64) or `training_started` (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:65-66), and rejects any single payload row carrying those flags (OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:151). So "no external AI, no gradient training" is not a comment — a file that violated it would fail to load.

On whether `held_out_accuracy = 1` on only 4 rows is honest: the data fact is that `models\index.json` records `training_row_count 8`, `held_out_row_count 4`, `held_out_accuracy 1` right next to `semantic_quality_claimed false` and `external_ai_used false` for both orchestration-reasoning (index.json:14-20) and prompt-to-task (index.json:43-49). The runtime gate that actually computes held-out accuracy is `EvaluateAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:350): it re-runs each held-out request through `DecideFromTrainedState`, computes `accuracy = correct / count` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:386), passes at the 0.8 threshold (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:63, :388), and its own gate note appends `semantic_quality_claimed=false` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:396). The honest reading: the perfect score is disclosed together with the tiny denominator (4 rows), and the code both stamps and enforces the disclaimer that a perfect count is NOT a semantic-quality claim; `index.json` is a locator/publication record (`locator_proof_type: json_index_file_resolver_proof`), so the `1` is presented, not used to unlock anything. It is presented honestly, but 1.0 on 4 held-out rows is a small-sample pass, not evidence of a generalizing semantic model.

**(2) LIFECYCLE — for SkillSelection the fresh-load round-trip is real and behavioral.**

The document claim is the guide's lifecycle gate, in words "Fresh machine can load checkpoint and pass held-out evaluation" (active-codex-execution-05-dpo-data-training-measurement-guide.md:137). The code fact is that this loop is wired: `TrainMachineBatchAsync` / `TrainBatchAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:96, :307) call `LearnPair` to mutate the typed skill-profile state; `ExportAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:402) via `SaveCheckpointAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:455) writes the checkpoint JSON plus a separate manifest with `SemanticQualityClaimed: false` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:494); `ImportAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:417) validates the manifest, checks the checkpoint file hash, then `LoadCheckpoint` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:247) clears and reloads all state; `EvaluateAsync` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:350) re-scores held-out rows.

The single allowed test read confirms it end to end. The code fact is that `SkillSelectionMachine_ExportAsyncImportAsync_RoundTripsStoredCheckpointFiles` (Tests\SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:38-140) trains one human-corrected pair that prefers the skill `plan-review` (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:66), calls `ExportAsync` which writes checkpoint and manifest files that are asserted to exist on disk (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:80, :86-87), asserts the raw prompt text is NOT present in the checkpoint JSON (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:93, a privacy/leakage guard), then constructs a genuinely fresh `new SkillSelectionMachine()` (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:118), calls `ImportAsync` (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:119), and asserts the fresh instance's `DecideFromTrainedState` returns exactly `["plan-review"]` with `RequiresSecondPass` false and confidence above zero (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:125-128). Critically the code fact is that the decision is asserted to carry the semantic prompt-understanding checkpoint evidence and explicitly NOT the candidate-position scaffold evidence (SkillSelectionMachineTests.cs:129-131). So this is a real fresh-load round-trip proven behaviorally, not a `result.success`-only stub.

Scope it honestly: the round-trip proves typed learned state persists and a brand-new instance reproduces the corrected choice from counting-based skill profiles. It is not a proof of semantic quality. The code fact is that the integer candidate-position weights are self-labelled scaffold only — the constant `CandidatePositionScaffoldEvidence = "CANDIDATE_POSITION_WEIGHT_SCAFFOLD_ONLY"` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:25) and the route mode `candidate_position_scaffold_not_final_semantic_authority` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:62) — and `DecideFromTrainedState` (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:112) refuses to let those weights select a skill, returning empty with `RequiresSecondPass` and routing final authority to the SS-02..SS-12 semantic units (SkillSelectionMachine.cs:133-147). So "pass held-out evaluation" is satisfied by the counting/KNN skill-profile units, with the machine itself flagging the candidate-position weights as baseline diagnostics, not authority.

**(3) THE RESULT-GAP DPO WRITERS — authored, fenced, staged; nothing trains on them.**

The code fact is that `ResultGapDpoWriters.cs` defines the record `ResultGapTrainableDpoCandidate` (:67), a privacy router `ResultGapSourcePrivacyRouter` (:114), and a schema validator `ResultGapDpoSchemaValidator` (:242); the writer `ResultGapDpoCandidateWriter.WriteCandidate` (ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:423) appends a candidate to an NDJSON file only when `candidate.TrainReady` is true (ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:433), otherwise writes nothing and returns `Quarantined` (ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:447-448).

Does anything consume these candidates into actual training? The code fact is no. The only consumers of `ResultGapTrainableDpoCandidate` in the repository are the writer itself, one runner tool `tools\ResultGapPhase07Runner\Program.cs`, and tests.
