**Reading the table for a human.** The chain splits cleanly in half. The typed legal FRONT half — links 1, 2, 3, 4 (`GraphDpoSourceRow`, `GraphDpoPairCandidate`, `TriadLegalProjection` + `G_DPO_FINAL`, `GraphDpoPair`) — does not exist in code at all: each named type returns zero hits (Finding 5, re-confirmed), so all four must be built from scratch, and the one near-hit (`CrossStackBusinessFlowGraphDpoPair`) is a sibling format the canon must absorb rather than an implementation of it. The TAIL half — links 5, 6, 7 (`LearningTrainingItem`, `DpoDataset.Load`, the gradient step) — is the opposite: every tail carrier already ships and works today (the training item is live and consumed by four sibling domains; the loader is real; `DpoTrainer.Step` is a genuine gradient DPO step already run by the project-wiki experiment), so what is missing there is not the carriers but the connective tissue — a materializer that projects the (still-absent) 14-field pair into the training item, and the activation of the graph machine's deliberately fail-closed training entry — plus the legal gate that must sit in front of the tail. In one sentence for the reader: none of the four legal-front-half types exist and must be built, while all three tail carriers already exist and only need to be fed by the built front half through a gate.

**Verdict:** typed DPO chain gap = FRONT HALF ABSENT, TAIL HALF ALREADY LIVE. Four of seven links (the entire typed legal front half — `GraphDpoSourceRow`, `GraphDpoPairCandidate`, `TriadLegalProjection`/`G_DPO_FINAL`, `GraphDpoPair`) are 0-hit and must be built new (Finding 5, re-confirmed: 0/0/0/0 and the 9 `GraphDpoPair` substring hits are the unrelated `CrossStackBusinessFlowGraphDpoPair` at `ProjectWikiDomainModels.cs:923-931`); three of seven links (the tail: training item, dataset load, gradient step) already exist as working code (`LearningTrainingItem` at `00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:35-44`, `DpoDataset.Load` at `11.DPO\DpoDataset.cs:23-31`, `DpoTrainer.Step` at `11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs:38-78`) and need only projection-plus-gate wiring, not new carriers; and the single most reusable asset is `LearningTrainingItem` (`00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:35-44`) — the already-shipped, already-consumed tail carrier the whole typed chain is designed to land on (with `DpoTrainer.Step`, the gradient engine already exercised by the project-wiki runner, the close runner-up).


### Finding 30 — Gap map: the 20-field teacher gate and provenance (Cycle 4, Task 2)

**Plain-language framing.** The plan's legal teacher gate is the paper trail that must accompany every training text a stronger "teacher" model produced: who hosted the model, which model and license, whether the provider's terms were checked, whether the text was actually used for training, and whether any raw private text was copied. The plan packages that paper trail as a named 20-field sheet called `TeacherProvenance` (13 base fields + 5 benchmark-delta fields + 2 service-mode fields), plus a SEPARATE sheet called `JudgeProvenance` for models that only score/critique and never author. On top of the two sheets the plan adds a six-value legal-verdict list (an "enum" — a fixed set of allowed answers) and two admission gates that must both hit a perfect 1.0 rate before any row may be marked train-ready: the provenance-field-presence rate (every one of the 20 fields is present on every row) and the row-to-sidecar reference-match rate (every row points at a matching side-file/manifest record). This finding builds the GAP MAP: what the plan requires, which fragments already exist inside ANY sibling record in the code, the honest field-by-field count, the delta that must still be built, and which existing enforcement pattern is reusable. Audited code: repository `C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation`, branch `DpoImplementation`, HEAD `0f221ac89`, working tree verified clean (`git status --porcelain` empty, `git rev-parse HEAD` = `0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45`) before and after; read/grep only, no build, no tests, no edits.

**Method note (reuse over re-audit).** Proven facts are carried forward by Finding number, not re-derived: Finding 6 established the CODE FACT that a repository-wide `*.cs` search returns 0 hits for `TeacherProvenance`, 0 for `JudgeProvenance`, 0 for `ProviderTermsProof`, 0 for `AdmittedModel`, and that the only legal term present, `LegalVerdict`, is a single hardcoded `"QUARANTINE"` string on the FLOW-38 path, plus the FLOW-38 force-false-on-write / reject-true-on-read discipline. Finding 24 established the design-conversion judge-only receipt at `44.DesignConversionDomain\DesignConversionHumanApproval.cs:864-937` and the non-DPO approval record at `:599-602`. Finding 22 established the translation license allowlist and attribution manifest. Finding 23 established the result-gap privacy router plus the 14-field schema validator. Finding 27 established the `external_ai_used` hard-reject at `43.SmartSkillsDomain\OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:63-64` (the existing load-time legality-enforcement pattern). Two fresh grep-level checks were run this task. Fresh check A (delta terms absent in code): a `*.cs` search returns 0 hits each for `TeacherProvenanceGateUnit`, `provenance_field_presence`, `row_sidecar_ref_match`, `BLOCK_FOR_COMMON`, `EVAL_ONLY`, `DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED`, `geography_export_constraint` / `GeographyExportConstraint`, `service_mode` / `ServiceMode`, and `grounding_used` / `GroundingUsed` — so every named gate, rate, verdict-beyond-QUARANTINE, and the two service-mode fields are absent. Fresh check B (analogue field names present): the scattered analogue field names `SourceCategory`, `TermsSnapshotRef`, `RedactionPolicyRef`, `ContainsRawPrivateText`, `OutputLicenseRef` return 49 hits across 14 files, concentrated in `25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler.cs`, `43.SmartSkillsDomain\ResultGapDpoWriters.cs`, and the smart-skills contract files — so the field CONCEPTS exist, but (per Finding 6) never inside a record named `TeacherProvenance` or `JudgeProvenance`.

**What the plan demands (document claims, not code facts).** The plan document lists the exact 20 fields of `TeacherProvenance`: 13 base (`teacher_access_mode`, `hosting_provider`, `model_id`, `model_license`, `model_version`, `request_id`, `provider_terms_checked`, `teacher_output_used_for_training`, `closed_proprietary_model`, `source_ref`, `source_sha256`, `contamination_markers`, `judge_model`), 5 benchmark-delta (`terms_snapshot_ref`, `geography_export_constraint`, `source_category`, `raw_private_text_copied`, `redaction_policy_ref`), and 2 service-mode (`service_mode`, `grounding_used`), and states full judge evidence "is carried by separate `JudgeProvenance`" (active-codex-execution-plan-08-legal-teacher-gate.md:389-409). The same document draws the closed-model boundary "closed proprietary APIs may judge/evaluate only ... they never author chosen/rejected text, a DPO label, or a training target" (active-codex-execution-plan-08-legal-teacher-gate.md:11), and admits its own status in its own words — the `JudgeProvenance` carrier and the provider-terms leg are "SPEC CONTRACT DEFINED ... but implementation/runtime evidence is `NEEDS_LEGAL_MACHINE_REVIEW`" (active-codex-execution-plan-08-legal-teacher-gate.md:13-15), i.e. spec on paper, not shipped code. The master plan's binding row states, in words, that the 20 `TeacherProvenance` fields are nested in the 14-field pair and "backed by sidecar/manifest projection proof", and that `provenance_field_presence_rate==1.0` and `row_sidecar_ref_match_rate==1.0` are preconditions of train-ready, with the manifest carrying only `verdict==ACCEPT` from a "6-value" enum "including `BLOCK_FOR_COMMON` and `EVAL_ONLY`" (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:451, row R-05). The canonical six values are enumerated as `ACCEPT | QUARANTINE | BLOCK | DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED | BLOCK_FOR_COMMON | EVAL_ONLY` (active-codex-execution-plan-08-legal-teacher-gate.md:502-506, section 3.3). The architecture brief names the quarantine routing table that a missing/incomplete `TeacherProvenance` forces to `QUARANTINE` / `DpoTrainReady=false` (active-codex-execution-06-ilearningdynamicgraph-architecture-brief.md:138-149). All of these are document claims. Every code fact below is read directly from the audited commit.

**The 20-field analogue map (code facts).** Each row keeps the plan field (a document concept) apart from the closest existing code field (a real record in the repository, with `file:line`). The three sibling records searched are: the FLOW-38 client-feedback quarantine row `DesignConversionFlow38QuarantinedSourceRow` and its `PersistedEnvelope` (both in `25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler.cs`); the result-gap candidate `ResultGapTrainableDpoCandidate` (`43.SmartSkillsDomain\ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:67-99`); the translation attribution manifest `WriteAttributionAsync` (`27.TranslationDomain\TranslationCorpusSourcing.cs:735-749`); and the design-conversion judge receipt bindings (`44.DesignConversionDomain\DesignConversionHumanApproval.cs:864-937`).

**Honest count.** 16 of 20 fields have SOME existing analogue somewhere in a sibling record; 4 of 20 have none. The four with no analogue anywhere are `model_version` (field 5), `geography_export_constraint` (field 15), `service_mode` (field 19), and `grounding_used` (field 20) — confirmed absent by Fresh check A (0 hits each for the last three; no model-version field exists). The named analogues are: from the FLOW-38 quarantine row — `ChosenOrigin`/`RejectedOrigin`, `SourceCategory`, `TermsSnapshotRef`, `OutputLicenseRef`, `RedactionPolicyRef`, `ProvenanceRefs`, `ChosenOutputRef`/`Hash`, `PromptHash`, `EventId`/`CorrelationId`, `DpoTrainReady`, `LegalVerdict`; from the result-gap candidate — `TrainReady`, `ContainsRawPrivateText`, `ContainsRawVendorOutput`, `NearMissTaxonomyCode`, `SourceIdUsedAsTrainingFeature`, `ProvenanceRefs`; from the translation manifest — `license`/`license_normalized`, `provider`, `source_id`, `raw_text_policy`; from the design-conversion judge receipt — `provider_name`, `provider_model`, `provider_endpoint_host`, `request_id`, `real_external_provider_executed`, `server_bound_expected_sha256`. Two honest qualifiers weigh against the raw 16/20. First, many analogues are weak: `teacher_access_mode` maps only to an origin string, and `hosting_provider`/`model_id` map only to the JUDGE's provider/model, not the teacher's. Second, and decisively, the CODE FACT (Finding 6) is that ZERO of the 20 fields lives inside a record named `TeacherProvenance` or `JudgeProvenance` (both 0 hits), and there is no gate that computes `provenance_field_presence_rate` or `row_sidecar_ref_match_rate` (both 0 hits, Fresh check A). The single richest donor is the FLOW-38 client-feedback quarantine row `DesignConversionFlow38QuarantinedSourceRow` (Flow38 handler :17-40), which alone carries roughly nine of the twenty concepts — but it is a client-supplied feedback row that must stay quarantined, not a teacher-authoring provenance sheet, and its `LegalVerdict` only ever holds the constant `"QUARANTINE"` (Finding 6).

**Delta — what must be built (named contract, near-zero exists).** (1) The `TeacherProvenance` record itself — the named 20-field sheet (0 hits); it must consolidate the ~16 scattered analogues into one contract and add the 4 net-new fields (`model_version`, `geography_export_constraint`, `service_mode`, `grounding_used`). (2) The separate `JudgeProvenance` record (0 hits) — the design-conversion judge receipt (`DesignConversionHumanApproval.cs:864-937`, provider name/model/endpoint/request-id + "real external provider executed") is the closest working analogue but is a design-conversion visual-judge receipt, not a general judge-provenance carrier. (3) The `TeacherProvenanceGateUnit` — the admission gate unit the architecture brief names (0 hits, Fresh check A); it must enforce that a missing/incomplete sheet routes to `QUARANTINE` / `DpoTrainReady=false` per the brief's routing table (active-codex-execution-06-ilearningdynamicgraph-architecture-brief.md:138-149). (4) The sidecar/manifest projection plus the two rate gates `provenance_field_presence_rate==1.0` and `row_sidecar_ref_match_rate==1.0` (both 0 hits) — the closest working analogue is the result-gap 14-field schema validator, which does field-presence/leakage checks but has no 1.0-rate admission gate. (5) The six-value verdict enum `ACCEPT | QUARANTINE | BLOCK | DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED | BLOCK_FOR_COMMON | EVAL_ONLY` (plan §3.3, :502-506) — the code has only the single hardcoded `LegalVerdict="QUARANTINE"` string (Finding 6); `BLOCK_FOR_COMMON`, `EVAL_ONLY`, and `DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED` are 0 hits.

**Reusable enforcement pattern (already proven in code).** The gate does NOT need its enforcement machinery invented from scratch — three proven patterns are reusable. First, the load-time hard-reject: `OrchestrationReasoningTrainer` aborts the whole run when a manifest reports the forbidden `external_ai_used` flag (`43.SmartSkillsDomain\OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:63-64`, Finding 27) — exactly the "reject-on-forbidden-flag at load" shape the teacher gate's admission needs. Second, the fail-closed write/read fence: the FLOW-38 handler forces the train-ready flag false on write (`DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler.cs:221`) and rejects any true value before persistence at both the HTTP entry (:1313) and the store (:177) via the invariant at :1422, and again on read-back (Findings 6/15) — the proven force-false / reject-true discipline the `TeacherProvenanceGateUnit` should copy. Third, the field-presence validator: the result-gap 14-field schema validator (`43.SmartSkillsDomain\ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:242-262`, Finding 23) is the closest working analogue of the two rate gates, checking no-raw-private-text, no-raw-vendor-output, no-common-mutation-claim, and source-id-not-a-feature. A fourth, weaker analogue is the design-conversion server-owned judge-receipt validation (`DesignConversionHumanApproval.cs:864-937`, Finding 24), the nearest existing model for `JudgeProvenance`'s "real external provider executed, judge authored nothing" proof.

**Verdict:** teacher gate gap = NAMED CONTRACT NEAR-ZERO, ENFORCEMENT PATTERNS ALREADY EXIST. Of the 20 fields, 16 have some scattered analogue and 4 have none (`model_version`, `geography_export_constraint`, `service_mode`, `grounding_used`), yet ZERO of the 20 lives in a named `TeacherProvenance`/`JudgeProvenance` record (both 0 hits, Finding 6), no `provenance_field_presence_rate` or `row_sidecar_ref_match_rate` gate exists (both 0 hits), and the verdict list is a single hardcoded `"QUARANTINE"` string rather than the six-value enum (`BLOCK_FOR_COMMON`/`EVAL_ONLY`/`DATASET_REBUILD_REQUIRED` all 0 hits) — so the five deltas (the `TeacherProvenance` record, the separate `JudgeProvenance` record, the `TeacherProvenanceGateUnit`, the sidecar/manifest projection with the two 1.0-rate gates, and the six-value enum) must all be BUILT, but the three enforcement patterns they need (the `external_ai_used` hard-reject at `OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:63-64`, the FLOW-38 force-false/reject-true fence at `DesignConversionModelServerFlow38QuarantineHandler.cs:221`/`:1422`, and the result-gap 14-field validator at `ResultGapDpoWriters.cs:242-262`) are proven and reusable — the gate must be assembled as a named contract, not engineered from nothing.


### Finding 31 — Gap map: the auto-DPO loop, stage by stage (Cycle 4, Task 3)

**Plain-language framing.** The plan calls this loop "сердце проекта" — the heart of the project (a document claim at active-codex-execution-plan-09-auto-dpo-loop.md:52). It is one closed self-improvement cycle: run one real input through several different unit-combinations, notice where the small local model answered weakly, fetch a stronger allowed "teacher" model's answer as the goal, build a preference pair out of that gap (a "chosen" good answer versus a "rejected" weak one), pass it through a legal gate, train the top graph "machine" on it, measure the result on held-out tasks, then promote the checkpoint only if quality rose — and repeat. ("DPO" is Direct Preference Optimization, the training method that teaches a model to prefer a good answer over a bad one. "L5 machine" is the top, and only trainable, level of the graph — the object that owns training, evaluate, export, import, and promotion.) This finding builds a GAP MAP over the plan's eight named stages. Throughout, a document claim (what a planning file requires) and a code fact (what the audited repository actually contains) are kept in separate sentences, each with its own path:line. Audited code: repository `C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation`, branch `DpoImplementation`, HEAD `0f221ac89`, working tree verified clean (`git status --porcelain` empty, `git rev-parse HEAD` = `0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45`) before and after; read/grep only, no build, no tests, no edits.

**What the plan demands (document claims, not code facts).** The plan names the loop's eight steps and tags the four connective classes NEW, meaning they are to be built, not existing: step 1 async-runs one input through different combinations via `ILogicalFlow`/`ILogicalOrchestrator` (active-codex-execution-plan-09-auto-dpo-loop.md:171); step 2 detects the weak output via `IGraphWeaknessDetectorUnit` (:172); step 3 captures the teacher goal via `ITeacherGoalProviderUnit` wrapping the canonical seam `ITeacherGoalClient` behind `LegalTeacherGate` (:173); step 4 builds the pair via `IGraphDpoPairBuilderUnit` and projects it through `TriadLegalProjection` with the `G_DPO_FINAL` go-token into `TrainBatchAsync` (:174); step 5 measures held-out via `EvaluateAsync` (:175); step 6 promotes on a delta-gate borrowed as a pattern ("образец", Russian for sample) from `16.AutonomousLearning` and repeats (:176). The whole loop is orchestrated by a NEW controller class `AutoDpoLoopOrchestrator` that implements the L5 machine and owns one graph state (:235-237). The plan's typed chain canon is `GraphDpoPairCandidate(false) -> TriadLegalProjection(FullDpoTrainReadyProof,G_DPO_FINAL) -> GraphDpoPair(Level=unit,true) -> LearningTrainingItem/JSONL -> DpoDataset.Load -> TrainBatchAsync` (:9). The guide restates the same twelve-step path from input to trained checkpoint (active-codex-execution-guide-05-auto-dpo-loop.md:85-129, sections 4-7) and the architecture brief draws the same end-to-end flow with `WeaknessDetector + WinnerSelectionDecision` sitting between the async candidates and the legal gate (active-codex-execution-06-ilearningdynamicgraph-architecture-brief.md:99-101). Every code fact below is read directly from the audited commit.

**Method note (reuse over re-audit).** Proven facts are carried forward by Finding number, not re-derived: Finding 7 established that the loop controller, the weakness detector, the teacher-goal provider, and any repeat-until-quality controller all return zero code hits, and listed the disconnected prerequisite blocks; Findings 4/16 established that the stage-1 combination runners exist and are honest (bounded concurrency, deterministic ordering, timeout, cancellation, partial-failure — all test-proven, no error swallowed, no success faked); Finding 26 established that only the delta-gate control-flow SHAPE is reusable from `16.AutonomousLearning`, which is otherwise a hollow pipe; Finding 3 established that the L5 graph machine is fail-closed on all four lifecycle entry points; Finding 29 established the typed-chain gap (the legal front half absent, the tail carriers live). Two fresh grep-level checks were run this task, described next.

**Fresh check A — the winner-selection handoff slot `B12WinnerSelectionRef` is DECORATIVE, with no production writer.** The candidate record `GraphNodeCandidate` carries a string field commented "Winner-selection handoff reference" — a code fact defined at `00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:65`. This slot is the wiring hook that a live stage-2 (winner/weakness selection) and stage-4 (pair builder) would populate. A by-name grep across all `*.cs` returns exactly ten occurrences, and every one classifies as pass-through, presence-validation, or test-only — never as a production writer that computes real content:
- One DEFINITION (`00.Core\DynamicGraphContracts.cs:65`).
- Four PASS-THROUGH copies, all `CopyCandidate` helpers that copy `source.B12WinnerSelectionRef` verbatim into a new candidate: `15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestrator.cs:621`, `21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:584`, `21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\DynamicGraphNodeOutputSourceContracts.cs:86`, `21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunner.cs:492`.
- One READER that is a presence-VALIDATION only, not a content consumer: `21.AgentInfrastructure\Logical\LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNode.cs:400` requires the field be non-empty and otherwise fails with `DYNAMIC_GRAPH_NODE_CANDIDATE_HANDOFF_REF_REQUIRED` — it checks that a string is present, never what it means.
- Four TEST-ONLY assignments: a synthetic `$"b12-{candidateId}"` at `Tests\DynamicGraphCandidateInvocationOrchestratorTests.cs:388`, the honest placeholder literal `"not-invoked"` at `Tests\BoundedCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs:818` and `Tests\SingleCombinationGraphCandidateRunnerTests.cs:616`, and a type-shape check at `Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:89`.

The origin is decisive: the node that would produce candidates does NOT build them itself — it delegates to a caller-injected `candidateProjection` delegate (code fact at `LogicalUnitDynamicGraphNode.cs:65` and `:72`), and the two shipped projectors CONSUME candidates rather than construct them (`DeterministicDynamicGraphNodeOutputProjector.cs:17`/`:36` takes `IReadOnlyList<GraphNodeCandidate> nodeOutputs` as input; `LogicalRegistryDynamicGraphProjector.cs` builds edges/topology, not candidates). So no production path ever fills the winner-selection slot with real content; the only fully-constructed candidates carrying an explicit value live in tests, and the value the tests choose is literally `"not-invoked"`. Conclusion: the stage-2 and stage-4 wiring hooks are present as reserved, validated, copy-preserved slots but are DECORATIVE — no live producer exists behind them.

**Fresh check B — no repeat-until-quality controller exists under any newer name.** A case-insensitive `*.cs` grep for `RetrainUntil`, `IterateUntil`, `RepeatUntil`, `LoopUntil`, `TrainUntil`, `UntilQuality`, `quality_threshold`, and `AutoDpoLoop` finds no production graph-training loop: `AutoDpoLoop`/`auto_dpo` = 0 (re-confirming Finding 7), and the only `quality_threshold` production hits are in the design-conversion domain. That name-alike is a DIFFERENT feature — the design-conversion inference-time output-refinement loop `DesignConversionImprovementLoopCoordinator` (`44.DesignConversionDomain\DesignConversionImprovementLoopCoordinator.cs:16-70`) and its server handler (`25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\DesignConversionModelServerImprovementLoopHandler.cs:83`). A code fact: that loop iterates by generating the NEXT design-to-code candidate through `IDesignConversionImprovementCandidateStrategy.CreateNextCandidateAsync` and re-scoring it with a visual judge (`BestScore` versus `RequiredScore`, stopping on `QualityReached` / `MaxAttemptsReached` / `BudgetCapReached`) — it refines an OUTPUT per request; it never runs a DPO gradient step, never mutates weights per iteration, and never promotes a trained checkpoint. So there is no repeat-until-quality DPO training controller anywhere, under any name.

**The eight-stage gap map.** The "Plan class" column cites a plan-document file:line (a claim on paper). The "Exists today" column cites a code file:line and the reusing Finding (a fact in the repository). "REAL" means a working implementation matches the named stage; "ZERO" means the named type returns zero code hits; "elsewhere" means the capability exists in a sibling subsystem but is not wired to the graph loop.

**Reading the map for a human.** The loop exists as three real pieces standing far apart with no wire between them. The FRONT — stage 1, running one input through several combinations — is genuinely built and honest (Findings 4/16). The MIDDLE — stages 2, 3, 4, 5 (detect the weak output, capture the teacher goal, build the pair, pass the legal gate) — is entirely absent: every named class returns zero code hits, the reserved handoff slots that would carry data between these stages have no production writer (fresh check A), and the one teacher endpoint the loop needs does not exist (Finding 8). The BACK — stages 6 and 7 (train, measure) — exists as real machinery but ELSEWHERE: the DPO gradient engine and the measurement frame ship and are exercised by sibling domains, while the graph's own train and evaluate entries are deliberately fail-closed stubs (Findings 3/27/29). Stage 8's promotion has only a reusable control-flow shape and no controller. Above all of it, the connective class the plan calls the heart of the project — the `AutoDpoLoopOrchestrator` that would sequence stage 1 into 2 into 3 ... into 8 and loop — is not present in any form.

**Verdict:** auto-DPO loop gap = STAGE 1 REAL, MIDDLE (STAGES 2-5) ABSENT, STAGES 6-7 EXIST ELSEWHERE BUT UNWIRED, STAGE 8 SHAPE-ONLY, CONNECTIVE CONTROLLER ABSENT. Stage 1 is a working, test-proven combination runner (Findings 4/16); stages 2-5 (`IGraphWeaknessDetectorUnit`, `ITeacherGoalProviderUnit`/`ITeacherGoalClient`, `IGraphDpoPairBuilderUnit`/`GraphDpoPairCandidate`, `TriadLegalProjection`/`G_DPO_FINAL`) are all 0-hit and must be built new (Findings 5/6/7/29/30); stage 6's `DpoTrainer.Step` (`11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs:38-78`) and stage 7's `LearningBatchResult` frame (`00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:43`) are real but live in sibling subsystems while the graph's own `DynamicGraphMachine.TrainBatchAsync`/`EvaluateAsync` fail closed (`15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphMachine.cs:45-61`, Findings 3/27); stage 8 offers only the reusable delta-gate shape from `16.AutonomousLearning` (`AutonomousLearningPipeline.cs:142-145`, Finding 26). The single largest missing connector is the loop controller `AutoDpoLoopOrchestrator` itself (plan-09:235-237, 0 code hits, Finding 7): without it the real stage-1 runners, the real stage-6 `DpoTrainer`, and the reusable stage-8 delta-gate never join into a cycle, and the reserved between-stage handoff slots (`B12WinnerSelectionRef` at `DynamicGraphContracts.cs:65`, sibling `B6UnitDpoBuilderRef` at :66) have no production writer and stay decorative — the test placeholder value `"not-invoked"` literally names the state of the whole middle.


### Finding 32 — Gap map: checkpoint carriers, fresh-load, and promotion (Cycle 4, Task 4)

Plain words: the plan's graph-training lifecycle needs two "carrier" records and three proofs. The first carrier is **GraphCheckpointManifest** — the checkpoint's passport, the record that says which saved model this is, where its weights live, and how it was trained. The second is **GraphLearningExport** — the portable export you would hand to another machine so it can re-load the same trained state. The three proofs are: **fresh-load == trained** (load a saved checkpoint into a brand-new model/process and get the same accuracy the just-trained model had), **continue-training delta > 0** (train more from an existing checkpoint and the held-out number goes up, where "held-out" means rows the model never trained on), and a **SHA-256 export/import round-trip** (SHA-256 is a hash where identical bytes give an identical fingerprint, so exporting a checkpoint and re-importing it must produce byte-identical state). This finding maps, for each of the five pieces, what the plan demands, what exists in code today, the delta, and which existing sibling asset is reusable.

Audited repo root for every `.cs` anchor below: `C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation` (branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89, tree verified clean).

**Code facts (what the code does today):**
- The code does have exactly one shared export record, `LearningMachineExport`, and it carries only seven fields — MachineId, DomainTag, Version, CheckpointRef, ManifestRef, AccuracyAtExport, ExportedAtUtc (`00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:117-132`). "CheckpointRef" and "ManifestRef" are storage keys into the model-storage module `25.ModelServer`.
- The code does NOT define any type named GraphCheckpointManifest or GraphLearningExport anywhere; a repo-wide grep over `*.cs` returns zero matches (confirms Finding 3).
- The code does fail closed on the graph machine's export: `DynamicGraphMachine.ExportAsync` returns a failure with code "DYNAMIC_GRAPH_EXPORT_NOT_ACTIVATED" (`15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphMachine.cs:63-68`).
- The code does fail closed on import: `DynamicGraphMachine.ImportAsync` returns "DYNAMIC_GRAPH_IMPORT_NOT_ACTIVATED" (`DynamicGraphMachine.cs:70-77`).
- The code does fail closed on training: `DynamicGraphMachine.TrainBatchAsync` returns "DYNAMIC_GRAPH_TRAINING_NOT_ACTIVATED" (`DynamicGraphMachine.cs:45-52`).
- The code does declare its own honesty: the class comment says the training-lifecycle methods deliberately fail until a real trained path exists (`DynamicGraphMachine.cs:21`), and it sets RuntimeMode = "honest_untrained" and SemanticQualityClaimed = false (`DynamicGraphMachine.cs:25-26`).
- The code does run a recursive-by-name manifest loader: `Directory.GetFiles(_checkpointRoot, TeachingRegistryPersistence.GeneratedCapabilityManifestFileName, SearchOption.AllDirectories)` scans every subdirectory under the checkpoint root for files with that one fixed filename and deserializes each hit as `GeneratedCapabilityCheckpointManifest`, keyed by UnitId (`18.CapabilityTraining\DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:586-592`). This is the collision hazard of Finding 28.

**Document claims (what the plan documents say — claims only; the status ledgers are ~2,706 commits stale):**
- A document claims the two carriers must each carry GraphDpoPairId, TeacherProvenanceRef, LegalVerdictRef, source dataset hash, held-out scorer refs, promotion state, and rollback/contamination-rebuild refs into GraphCheckpointManifest and GraphLearningExport (`active-codex-execution-plan-10-training-lifecycle.md:22`). Here "GraphDpoPairId" is the id of one chosen-vs-rejected training pair (DPO = Direct Preference Optimization, learning from a preferred answer over a rejected one), and "TeacherProvenanceRef" points to the 20-field teacher record of Finding 30.
- A document claims the required gates before promoting a checkpoint are fresh-load, held-out evaluation, ablation against a deterministic baseline, source-contamination check/rebuild, a rollback manifest, and legal/provider revalidation (`active-codex-execution-plan-10-training-lifecycle.md:23`). "Ablation" means turning the trained part off to prove it was actually contributing.
- A document claims (master-plan requirement row R-08, i.e. row 8 of the requirements table) the acceptance test is "fresh-load==trained; continue-training delta>0; export/import round-trip SHA-256" (`active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:454`).
- A document claims the mandatory restore order is ImportAsync(metadata) then LoadCheckpointAsync(weights), and that importing metadata never substitutes for restoring the trained weights (`active-codex-execution-plan-09-auto-dpo-loop.md:10`).
- A document claims, in the guide's own gap list, that weight export/import over the model server is not implemented (`active-codex-execution-guide-02-how-to-train.md:882-885`, gap 4), that the continue-training adapter load order is not closed (`guide-02:887-890`, gap 5), that held-out / ablation / fresh-load do not yet exist as separate evidence (`guide-02:896-899`, gap 7), and that manifest registries/schemas are duplicated (`guide-02:915-917`, gap 11); the guide also names the SHA-256 round-trip test `Checkpoint_ExportImport_RoundTrip_Sha256Stable` as an R-08 obligation (`guide-02:857`).

Sibling: smart-skills checkpoint + manifest + SHA-256 verification at load, with an auditable hash-repair history (Finding 23). | Activate `ExportAsync` + a hash-stable export/import round-trip test (`Checkpoint_ExportImport_RoundTrip_Sha256Stable`, guide-02:857) that re-imports to a byte-identical SHA-256. | Smart-skills SHA-256 verify-at-load + hash-repair history (Finding 23) |

**Carrier delta — the seven fields both carriers need BEYOND the generic 7-field `LearningMachineExport`, and who fills each (plan-10:22):**
1. **GraphDpoPairId** — id of the chosen-vs-rejected training pair; filled by the auto-DPO pair builder (plan blocks 07/09).
2. **TeacherProvenanceRef** — pointer to the 20-field teacher-provenance record (Finding 30); filled by the Legal Teacher Gate (block 08).
3. **LegalVerdictRef** — pointer to the legal verdict for that dataset; filled by the Legal Teacher Gate (block 08).
4. **source dataset hash** — hash of the exact training set used; filled by the dataset builder.
5. **held-out scorer refs** — pointers to the held-out evaluation that produced the accuracy; filled by the measurement / held-out runner (block 11).
6. **promotion state** — the promote-or-rollback outcome of this checkpoint; filled by the lifecycle promote/rollback step (block 10).
7. **rollback / contamination-rebuild refs** — pointer to the previous checkpoint to fall back to, plus any dataset-rebuild trigger; filled by rollback + the source-contamination check.
The generic record's CheckpointRef and ManifestRef become meaningful only once weights actually persist through `25.ModelServer`; the code has no working model server (a document claims this is gap 4, guide-02:882-885).

**Filename / loader-isolation rule the Finding 28 collision imposes:** the new GraphCheckpointManifest must NOT be written under the shared checkpoint root using the fixed filename `GeneratedCapabilityManifestFileName` (`generated_capability_manifest.json`). The code does recursively scan every subdirectory under the checkpoint root for that exact filename and deserialize each hit as `GeneratedCapabilityCheckpointManifest`, keyed by UnitId (`DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:586-592`). A graph manifest sharing that name and root would be silently slurped into the capability trainer's cache and mis-keyed. The design rule: give the graph manifest a distinct filename and/or a distinct root subtree so no recursive-by-name loader can collide with it.

**Verdict:** lifecycle carriers gap = both graph carriers (GraphCheckpointManifest, GraphLearningExport) are 100% absent — the only shared DTO is the 7-field generic `LearningMachineExport` (LearningMachineContracts.cs:117-132), missing all 7 plan provenance/promotion fields — and all three Gate-B proofs are fail-closed on the graph machine (Export/Import/Train each return *_NOT_ACTIVATED, DynamicGraphMachine.cs:45-77); the machinery to build every piece already exists across three siblings (translation fresh-load = strongest, smart-skills SHA-256, design-conversion continue-training probe) but NONE is graph-wired, the single most directly reusable asset is translation's honest fresh-load lifecycle (Finding 22), and the new manifest must be filename/loader-isolated from `generated_capability_manifest.json` to avoid the recursive-loader collision at DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:586 (Finding 28).


### Finding 33 — Gap map: the two-phase teacher route and provider seams (Cycle 4, Task 5)

**Plain-language frame.** The plan's "teacher" is the stronger outside model whose answers become the training goals the local model is taught to prefer. The plan rolls the teacher out in two phases. Phase 1 (identifier `TP1_LOCAL`, provenance profile `qwen35-local-v1`) runs a local Qwen3.5-9B model under Ollama on the laptop; because it is self-hosted there is no provider terms-of-service gate, only a weight-license gate. Phase 2 (identifier `TP2_HOSTED`, profile `qwen3-together-v1`) calls a hosted Qwen3 model at the vendor TogetherAI, and it is held behind a terms-proof gate named `G-TP-4` (a bundle of the Together terms-of-service snapshot, zero-data-retention/privacy proof, and output-use/training-rights proof). The plan also designs two provider "seams" — narrow interfaces that every outside call must pass through: `ITeacherGoalClient.GetGoalAsync` (fetch the teacher's goal answer) and `IJudgeScoreClient.ScoreAsync` (fetch a judge's score). And it imposes a mock-first rule: every external call must be seamed, mocked, and unit-tested before any real call is ever made.

**What the documents claim (claims, not code).** A document defines the two phases and the profile names at `active-codex-execution-original-task.md:394-397` (TP1_LOCAL = local Qwen3.5-9B Q4_K_M in Ollama, profile `qwen35-local-v1`; TP2_HOSTED = hosted `qwen3-together-v1` at TogetherAI, business approval `APPROVED_BY_LUBA_2026-07-07` sequenced into Phase 2, technical `ProviderTermsProof` still open). A document lists the seven transition gates at `active-codex-execution-two-phase-local-remote-overlay.md:385-402`. A document defines the two seam interfaces and their signatures at `active-codex-execution-06-ilearningdynamicgraph-architecture-brief.md:311-312`, and states that the owner of the request field-canon is plan-08 Pass03's 18-field `TeacherGoalRequest`, superseding an earlier 15-field Pass-01 list (`...06-...brief.md:314-316`). A document records the local profile fields (HostingProvider=LocalOllama, ModelId=Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B, license Apache-2.0 marked PROVISIONAL_FROM_FAMILY_CANON pending G-TP-2, TeacherOutputUsedForTraining=false until G_DPO_FINAL) at `active-codex-execution-04-dpo-legal-model-policy.md:33-49`, and the hosted business decision (still blocked until G-TP-4 and G_DPO_FINAL) at `...04-...policy.md:16-31`. A document states the mock-first rule at `active-codex-execution-01-authority-requirements-ledger.md:183` and warns at `:187` that "Mock-only evidence must not be reported as real external integration readiness"; the seam-boundary rule B2-001 is marked `CLOSED_IN_PLAN` at `...01-...ledger.md:174`; the architecture brief repeats at `...06-...brief.md:322` that "Real calls remain future execution debt, and mock-call seam tests remain required before runtime."

**What the code does (facts, verified at HEAD 0f221ac89, tree clean).** The code contains none of the teacher route. A `git grep` returns zero hits across all file types for `ITeacherGoalClient`, zero for `IJudgeScoreClient`, and zero for `GetGoalAsync`. The name `ScoreAsync` does exist, but it belongs to an unrelated internal deterministic scorer — `IUnitScorer.ScoreAsync` at `26.TrainableRouter/Contracts/IUnitScorer.cs:12` and `ITrainableRoutingFlow.ScoreAsync` at `26.TrainableRouter/Contracts/ITrainableRoutingFlow.cs:13` — not the outbound `IJudgeScoreClient.ScoreAsync` seam; the judge seam is absent and the method name merely collides. In source/config (`*.cs` / `*.json` / `*.csproj`) there is zero TogetherAI provider config (`togetherai` / `together.ai` / `api.together` all zero) and zero Ollama host/config in code (the only `ollama` hit is a prose link at `README.md:67`; `qwen` in code appears only as licensing/planning prose in `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/plans/`, never in a `.cs` runtime path). The five route identifiers `TP1_LOCAL`, `TP2_HOSTED`, `G-TP-4`, `qwen35-local-v1`, `qwen3-together-v1` return zero across `*.cs`/`*.json`/`*.csproj`/`*.md`/`*.yml` in the code repo — re-confirming Finding 8 (route genuinely zero across all file types) at this HEAD. The one hardened outbound-AI client that does exist is `44.DesignConversionDomain/DesignConversionExternalVisualJudgeNetwork.cs` (191 lines) — a judge-network fence, not a teacher client (Finding 24).

**Per-gate delta table (G-TP-1 .. G-TP-7).** "Label" uses the three requested categories: laptop-environment work / legal-proof bundle / code (the two gates already "closed" are plan-document consistency gates, closed only on paper, and are annotated as such).

**First code delta — the two seam interfaces.** The single most upstream missing piece is the pair of provider seams: `ITeacherGoalClient.GetGoalAsync(TeacherGoalRequest request, CancellationToken ct)` and `IJudgeScoreClient.ScoreAsync(JudgeScoreRequest request, CancellationToken ct)` (owner-canon at `...06-...brief.md:311-312`). Both interfaces are absent from the code (0 hits). The 18-field `TeacherGoalRequest` request-canon they carry (owner plan-08 Pass03; earlier 15-field list superseded, `...06-...brief.md:314-316`) does not exist as a type. Until these two seams and the request/response DTOs exist, no gate G-TP-1..G-TP-3 can be satisfied and the mock-first rule (`...01-...ledger.md:183`) has nothing to attach to.

**Reusable hardening that already exists (to lift into both seams).** Although the teacher route is zero, the repo already proves an outbound-call hardening pattern in `44.DesignConversionDomain/DesignConversionExternalVisualJudgeNetwork.cs`:
- Network fence (DNS pinning): the client resolves the hostname to IP addresses itself and connects the socket to that pinned address via an overridden `SocketsHttpHandler.ConnectCallback` (`:47-58`, `:124-135`), so the destination cannot be swapped by a second DNS lookup (anti-rebinding).
- Destination allowlist/block: every resolved address is checked and a blocked destination fails closed with `EXTERNAL_VISUAL_JUDGE_DNS_DESTINATION_BLOCKED` (`:117-122`); redirects, proxy, and auto-decompression are disabled with a 15s connect timeout and no pooled-connection reuse (`:38-45`).
- Typed fail-closed discipline: four distinct error codes and `OperationResult<Stream>.Fail(...)` on every path, never throwing for a network/business condition (`:14-17`, `:71-146`); raw DNS-exception text (which can contain the target IP) is sanitized away.
- Mock-seam rule honored here (proof it can be done): the outbound path is fully seamed through injectable `resolveAddressesAsync` and `connectSocketAsync` delegates (`:24-34`, `:63-67`), and a real unit test drives them with mocks — pinned address + original port asserted at `Tests/DesignConversionVisualJudgeTests.cs:1627`, DNS-failure/empty fail-closed and text-sanitization at `:1657` — exactly the seam+mock+unit-test the plan demands (`...01-...ledger.md:183`). No real network is touched.
- Receipt discipline: Finding 24 already established the server-owned receipt proving the one real external call in this repo was judge-only (`used_for_training=false`) — directly reusable for the teacher route's legal invariant that judges may be strong models but their output is never a training target (`overlay.md:414-415`).

**Verdict:** teacher route gap = 100% absent in code (both seam interfaces, `GetGoalAsync`, all five route identifiers, and every provider host string are zero at HEAD 0f221ac89 — Finding 8 re-confirmed; `ScoreAsync` exists only as an unrelated internal `IUnitScorer`, not the judge seam), all seven gates unmet in code (execution gates G-TP-1..G-TP-4 open, G-TP-5..G-TP-7 "closed" only on paper), yet a proven outbound-hardening pattern — DNS-pinned + destination-blocked + typed fail-closed + fully mock-seamed with a passing unit test + Finding 24's judge-only receipt — already exists in `DesignConversionExternalVisualJudgeNetwork.cs` and is the ready-made template both seams should reuse.


### Finding 34 — Gap map: the learned edge authority, order resolver, and determinism cutover (Cycle 4, Task 6)

**What this task asks in plain words.** The plan wants the graph's routing decisions — on a fork, which downstream learning unit gets the work and with how much trust — to be made by a LEARNED unit (one produced by training on examples), not by a deterministic hard-coded rule. Three pieces must exist for that to be true: (A) a trained "edge authority" unit (the learned trust/authority carried on a graph edge); (B) a trainable "order resolver" that decides in what order the graph is traversed, together with an experiment that switches it off to prove it is really doing the work; and (C) an explicit classification of the nine deterministic decision mechanisms that exist today plus the cutover that demotes them and promotes the learned unit to be the single final authority. This finding is a GAP MAP: for each piece it states what the plan requires (a document claim), what exists in the audited code today (a code fact), the delta between them, and which already-built pieces are reusable. Audited code: repository C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation, branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89 (verified with git rev-parse HEAD = 0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45 and git status --porcelain = empty/clean); read and grep only, no build, no tests, no edits. Where a fact was already proven in an earlier finding it is cited by number rather than re-derived.

**A note on names before the delta.** The plan uses two names for the SAME learned final edge authority: block 02 calls it `EdgeAuthorityUnit` (the phrase "edge authority unit" means the object that ranks the fork targets), and block 12 calls it `SemanticEdgeRouterUnit` (the phrase "semantic edge router" means the same learned chooser, named for choosing by meaning rather than position). Finding 2 already established that neither name exists anywhere in production code. This finding treats them as one required piece.

---

**Part A — the trained EdgeAuthorityUnit and its training path.**

The plan requires this (document claims). Block 02's title states the edge must carry a TRAINABLE edge authority (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:24). The document defines a NEW class `EdgeAuthorityUnit` marked "СКЕЛЕТ, цель PASS_AS_TRAINED_FEATURE" — a skeleton whose goal is to become a genuinely trained feature — whose input must EXCLUDE the six "leakage" signals (facts a cheating model could memorise instead of learning meaning): candidate_index (the position of a candidate in the list), source_path (the file path it came from), source_id, file_order, correlation_id, and exact_prompt; and whose design puts a K-nearest-neighbour retriever (a memory that finds the k most similar past training examples) INSIDE the unit as a model component, with IsLearned=true (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:545). The typed contract the document commits to is `EdgeAuthorityUnit : ILogicalUnit<EdgeAuthorityInput, EdgeAuthorityDecision>` with Id "unit-edge-authority-v1" and Role "edge-authority" (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:741-746). The single owner of training is `ILearningMachine<DynamicGraphDomain>` (the one interface allowed to run Train* methods, rule A-LM-02); the document states this contract lives at LearningMachineContracts.cs:208 and is implemented, but its DynamicGraphDomain realization is a skeleton (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:591). The held-out-gain requirement is that a training batch of 100 unit-level DPO pairs must yield `LearningBatchResult{AccuracyAfter>Before, RewardMargin>0}` with no UNKNOWN values, and the trained unit is then published latest-wins (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:639); the affected sets are an "edge-authority train + held-out (source-disjoint)" split, i.e. the held-out examples must come from different sources than the training ones so the score cannot be inflated by overlap (active-codex-execution-plan-02-edge.md:1047). The master plan fixes the numeric bar: held-out accuracy lift must be at least +0.02 and held-out accuracy at least 0.95 (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:1107-1108).

What exists in the code today (code facts, from Finding 2 and Finding 21). The trainable-authority CONTRACT, resolver, execution pipeline, and anti-cheat guards are all built and honest, but empty: no production class implements `ILogicalUnit<EdgeAuthorityInput, EdgeAuthorityDecision>` — the only four implementations live in the Tests directory as test doubles, and the label "trained_edge_unit" is merely a string constant (RoutingAuthorityVocabulary.cs:15), not a checkpoint (Finding 2). The six-signal leakage vocabulary the plan wants enforced already exists as an owned type (00.Core/DynamicGraphEdgeAuthorityLeakageSignalVocabulary.cs:9-19, per Finding 2). Finding 21 established that `TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit` is exactly the K-nearest-neighbour family the plan wants placed INSIDE the edge unit, but today it has the wrong role and contract, no leakage guard, and no held-out-gain proof.

The delta (what must be built). A production `EdgeAuthorityUnit` implementing `ILogicalUnit<EdgeAuthorityInput, EdgeAuthorityDecision>` with IsLearned=true, embedding the KNN retriever as an internal model component and enforcing the six-field leakage exclusion at its input boundary; and a working DynamicGraphDomain realization of `ILearningMachine<DynamicGraphDomain>` whose TrainBatchAsync actually mutates state, saves a checkpoint, and publishes latest-wins — proven by a source-disjoint held-out split showing AccuracyAfter minus AccuracyBefore of at least +0.02, RewardMargin above 0, and no UNKNOWN. Reuse source: Finding 2's already fail-closed resolver/pipeline/validator and the existing leakage-signal vocabulary; Finding 21's `TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit` repurposed under the correct role/contract with a leakage guard added; and, per Finding 19, the declared-only "edgeAuthorityPreload" injection door as the intended hand-in point for a trained unit.

---

**Part B — the trainable order resolver and its off-ablation test.**

The plan requires this (document claims). The architecture brief's section B7 defines `GraphOrderResolverUnit` as the trainable `ILogicalUnit` coordinated by `ILearningDynamicGraph`, which ranks or chooses legal traversal/order patterns under hard constraints; the deterministic Kahn/topological/closure traversal (a standard fixed algorithm for ordering a dependency graph) stays as the correctness baseline and fallback but is explicitly "not final learned authority after cutover" (active-codex-execution-06-ilearningdynamicgraph-architecture-brief.md:336-338). The document fixes a five-pattern taxonomy — order-0-direct-unit, order-1-single-predecessor, order-2-fan-out-fan-in, order-3-cross-domain-reference, order-4-async-many-to-many-aggregation (each names a shape of traversal, from a single direct hop up to async many-to-many aggregation) (brief:340-348). Its output type is `GraphOrderDecision(pattern_id, ordered_unit_ids[], ordered_path_refs[], skipped_edges[], rejected_edges[], confidence, legal_status, provenance_status, path_order_ref, trace_ref, reason_codes[])`, where path_order_ref is the stable handoff into unit-level DPO (brief:352). It must be proven by a resolver-off ablation: the named test `GraphOrderResolverOffAblation_DropsOrBlocksOrderClaim` (an "ablation" runs the same held-out split and scorer once with the trained resolver ON and once with it OFF/replaced by the deterministic baseline) accepts the learned-order claim ONLY if resolver-on beats resolver-off by the locked ablation threshold; otherwise the row is baseline/eval-only and no DPO training signal may be drawn from path order (active-codex-execution-06-...brief.md:125; active-codex-execution-plan-13-test-strategy.md:1310, also listed :1249 and :1333). The brief's own status line is `PASS05_GRAPH_ORDER_RESOLVER_DESIGNED_NEEDS_DOWNSTREAM_REVIEW` — designed, nothing implemented (brief:358).

What exists in the code today (code fact — new checks run for this task). Nothing. A git grep of the audited repository returns zero production hits for `GraphOrderResolverUnit` and zero for `GraphOrderResolver`; `GraphOrderDecision` returns exactly one hit, and it is not an implementation — it is the FORBIDDEN name inside a guard test at Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:302 (see the design constraint below). The named ablation test `GraphOrderResolverOffAblation_DropsOrBlocksOrderClaim` also does not exist in code (it appears only in the plan-13 test-strategy document). So the entire order-resolver layer is unbuilt.

The delta (what must be built). A production `GraphOrderResolverUnit` as a trainable `ILogicalUnit` emitting the eleven-field `GraphOrderDecision` and honouring the order-0..order-4 taxonomy; the deterministic Kahn/topological/closure traversal kept and clearly labelled as the baseline/fallback enumerator; and the `GraphOrderResolverOffAblation_DropsOrBlocksOrderClaim` test authored so that the learned-order claim survives only if resolver-on beats resolver-off by the locked threshold, otherwise the order claim is dropped or blocked. Reuse source: the deterministic topology/closure traversal already present in the code is the baseline the brief says must "stay stable for comparison and fallback"; the same held-out scorer/split machinery Part A's edge authority needs (path_order_ref feeds the same unit-level DPO); and Finding 2's fail-closed learned-vs-deterministic discipline as the pattern for refusing an unlearned final order.

---

**Part C — the nine-mechanism classification artifact and the cutover.**

The plan requires this (document claims). Block 12's title is "replace hidden determinism (classify 9 mechanisms + cutover to the learned edge authority)" (active-codex-execution-plan-12-no-hidden-determinism.md:36). The canon is `classified_mechanism_count == 9`, with every mechanism carrying a verdict drawn from a fixed five-value set — OK_ALLOWED_LEVEL, OK_INTERNAL_DETAIL, OK_MODEL_COMPONENT, BASELINE_MUST_BE_LABELLED, FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT — each backed by a file:line citation (plan-12:55-59). The filled nine-row table lives at plan-12:444-454: mechanism 1 `MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.Score` and mechanism 2 `KeywordUnitScorer.Score` are FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_SHORTCUT as long as they are the final chooser and must be demoted to labelled baseline candidate-generators; mechanism 3 `SubPhaseRouterUnit` routing is BASELINE_MUST_BE_LABELLED while its honest "KnowledgeEdge" uncertainty flag is OK_INTERNAL_DETAIL; mechanism 4 `OrchestrationScenarioPhasePlanner` is BASELINE_MUST_BE_LABELLED; mechanism 5 `RoutingProfileTrainer` is OK_INTERNAL_DETAIL (an offline builder); mechanism 6 `KnnLogicalUnit` is OK_MODEL_COMPONENT (kept inside the learned unit); mechanism 7 `InMemoryLogicalUnitRegistry.ResolveLatest` is OK_ALLOWED_LEVEL (the latest-wins cutover switch); mechanism 8 the path-free scoring helper is OK_INTERNAL_DETAIL; mechanism 9 the confidence-floor KnowledgeEdge branch is OK_INTERNAL_DETAIL. The master plan's requirement R-07 (the ban on hidden determinism on the edge) states Gate B as `classified_mechanism_count==9` and "0 unlabeled final authorities" with block 12 as owner (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:453).

What exists in the code today (code facts, from Finding 2 and Finding 17). The nine mechanisms exist individually as real code — for example KeywordUnitScorer.cs and OrchestrationScenarioPhasePlanner.cs and MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs — but there is NO code artifact that enumerates or classifies "the nine" as a set: a search for "nine" / "classified_mechanism" / a mechanism-count list finds nothing, so the enumeration exists only as the plan table at plan-12:444-454 (Finding 2). The cutover has not happened: the default runtime wiring registers only deterministic isLearned:false units (LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.cs:372-390), so the deterministic mechanisms remain the operative deciders (Finding 2). Finding 17 separately proved the enforcement rails are already armed — the fail-closed gate that refuses a deterministic or non-learned final authority is unbypassable on all five routing paths — so the demotion machinery exists; there is simply nothing learned to promote through it.

The delta (what must be built). First, turn the nine-mechanism classification into a real, machine-checkable code/config artifact — a labelled registry in which each of the nine carries its authority_role, its isLearned flag, and its verdict — so that `classified_mechanism_count == 9` and "0 unlabeled final authorities" can be verified from fields, not from prose in a plan. Second, perform the cutover itself: demote mechanisms 1 and 2 from final chooser to labelled baseline candidate-generators (isLearned:false); keep 3, 5, 8, 9 as internal detail; keep 6 as a model component inside the learned unit; keep 7 as the allowed latest-wins switch; and register the trained `EdgeAuthorityUnit` from Part A as the single final authority so that zero unlabeled final authorities remain. Reuse source: Finding 17's already-unbypassable five-path gate is the cutover's safety net (it will refuse the moment a deterministic unit tries to be final); Finding 25's keyword router is the deterministic baseline to demote, and its currently-unconsumed DPO pairs are the training material to route to the L5 learner; and Finding 2's resolver/validator already forbid a deterministic final authority structurally.

---

**A previously-unreported code fact that constrains the whole cutover (design constraint).** There is a negative-surface guard test at Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:280-334. The test is named `AsyncCandidateBoundary_RejectsTrainingSelectionAndUnownedImplementations`, and it asserts that a bounded slice of six async-candidate contract types — `GraphCandidateRunRequest`, `AsyncCombinationBudget`, `GraphCandidateOutput`, `AsyncCandidateBatch`, `CombinationPlan`, and the interface `IGraphCandidateRunner` (Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:283-291) — expose NO type-name or member-name containing any of eleven forbidden fragments: "Train", "Learn", "Fit", "UpdateWeights", "BuildDpo", "Chosen", "Rejected", "SelectWinner", "GraphOrderDecision", "Teacher", "Judge" (checked case-insensitively at Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:292-317). A code comment at Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:325 states the check is "intentionally bounded to this six-type slice and does not ban future owner-approved types." The design consequence is a real code fact, not a plan claim: the guard is scoped, not global, so the cutover cannot simply bolt the new learned names onto that async-candidate contract slice. The new `GraphOrderDecision` type from Part B (and any member named for Teacher, Judge, BuildDpo, or SelectWinner) must be placed OUTSIDE those six bounded types; adding `GraphOrderDecision` to any of them would flip this currently-green guard test red. The cutover work must therefore consciously RELOCATE these names off the forbidden surface (or introduce the new types outside the bounded contract), not merely add code.

**Verdict:** edge-authority gap = ENFORCEMENT-READY BUT CONTENT-EMPTY across all three pieces — Part A: the fail-closed authority scaffold, the leakage vocabulary, and a reusable KNN family (Finding 21) exist, but no production `EdgeAuthorityUnit`/`SemanticEdgeRouterUnit` and no working DynamicGraphDomain training path exist (Finding 2); Part B: the order resolver is entirely unbuilt — `GraphOrderResolverUnit`, `GraphOrderResolver`, and the `GraphOrderResolverOffAblation_DropsOrBlocksOrderClaim` ablation test are zero in code, and the sole `GraphOrderDecision` occurrence is a forbidden-name assertion in a guard test (Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:302); Part C: the nine-mechanism classification exists only as a plan table (plan-12:444-454) with no machine-checkable artifact and no cutover performed (default wiring is isLearned:false, LogicalOrchestratorAssistant.cs:372-390), while the demotion rails are already unbypassable (Finding 17) — and the cutover carries a hard design constraint that the new `GraphOrderDecision` and any Teacher/Judge/BuildDpo/SelectWinner-named members must live OUTSIDE the guarded six-type async-candidate surface (Tests\DynamicGraphContractsTests.cs:280-334, comment :325).


### Finding 35 -- The master plan's three reconnaissance defects, checked against the code (Cycle 4, Task 7)

Context in plain words. The "master plan" is the single file this audit treats as the plan of record: active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md. Inside it, section B.1 is an "Authority Requirements Ledger" -- a numbered list of binding requirements. Three of those rows (labelled R-17, R-18, R-19) are not requirements the plan invented; they are defects the plan's own authors found by reading the code ("reconnaissance"). No earlier report finding (1 through 34) had ever re-tested those three defects against the actual code. This finding does that, at the exact commit under audit (repository C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation, branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89, working tree confirmed clean by `git status --porcelain` before and after). Every check was a read-only grep or file read; no build, no test run, no code edit.

(a) Contaminated training rows tagged with a closed-model name. A "contaminated" row means a training example that may have been produced by a closed, forbidden model (Claude / Sonnet), which the project's legal policy bans as an authoring source.

The plan requires this (document claim). Row R-17 of the ledger states that rows named `sonnet-*` sit inside the folder `training-data/orchestration-reasoning/` and must be marked for a full dataset rebuild; its pass condition (its "gate") is written as `contaminated_rows_marked_REBUILD == contaminated_rows_found`, meaning the number of contaminated rows actually marked for rebuild must equal the number found (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:464).

What the code shows today (code fact). The contaminated rows are still physically present. A grep of `training-data/orchestration-reasoning/` finds the token "sonnet" in 10 corpus and manifest files, resolving to four distinct source rows: `or01u-sonnet-clinerules-001`, `or01u-sonnet-cursorrules-001`, `or01u-sonnet-gitattributes-001`, and `or01u-sonnet-gitignore-001` (for example in training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-dpo-trainer-payloads.jsonl:1-3). Each row names its origin as a source family called "sonnet-local-analyzes" -- analysis files a Sonnet model produced -- in its `source_ref` and `project_adapter_refs` fields (training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-dpo-trainer-payloads.jsonl:1).

Two facts decide the plan's gate, and they are separate. First (code fact): there is NO rebuild marking anywhere in that folder. A grep for "rebuild", "contaminat", or "quarantin" across every file in `training-data/orchestration-reasoning/` returns zero matches. Instead the manifests carry a `status` field whose value is "PASS" (training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-common-corpus-manifest.json:5) or "PASS_PENDING_PARENT_REVIEW" (training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-dpo-pairs-manifest.json:5) -- an accepted / pending-review state, the opposite of a quarantine-for-rebuild mark. So the number of rows marked for rebuild is zero while the number found is four; `0 == 4` is false, and the plan's marked-equals-found gate is NOT met.

Second (code fact), on whether any consuming code rejects these rows: every sonnet row, and every manifest in the folder, carries the flag `external_ai_used` set to false (training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-dpo-trainer-payloads.jsonl:1; training-data/orchestration-reasoning/orchestration-reasoning-dpo-trainer-payloads-manifest.json:22). The code that consumes this corpus -- the smart-skills orchestration-reasoning trainer at 43.SmartSkillsDomain/OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs -- rejects a payload only when its manifest reports `external_ai_used` as true (43.SmartSkillsDomain/OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:63-64), and (per Finding 27, report line 555) also rejects any single payload row carrying that flag (43.SmartSkillsDomain/OrchestrationReasoningTrainer.cs:151). Because the sonnet rows declare `external_ai_used:false`, neither guard fires. In plain terms the row's own label contradicts its own name: the source is literally called "sonnet" yet the row asserts no external AI was used, so the one guard that could have caught it never triggers.

One mitigating code fact, stated plainly so it is not mistaken for resolution. None of the four sonnet rows is currently marked ready for training: a grep shows no `"dpo_train_ready":true` and no `"training_started":true` on any row in the folder (every row carries `dpo_train_ready:false`, `training_started:false`). So nothing contaminated is feeding a live training run at this HEAD. But that "not yet promoted" state is applied to every row in these files, including clean non-sonnet rows; it is a general holding state, not the rebuild marking the plan ordered, and it does not make the plan's marked-equals-found count gate pass.

Per-defect verdict (a): PRESENT / gate NOT met. Four sonnet-sourced rows remain in the corpus, zero are marked for rebuild, and the consuming trainer does not reject them (they are labelled external_ai_used:false). Only mitigation: none are currently train-ready.

(b) Hard-coded reward margin literal. A "reward margin" is the number that says how much more a trained model prefers the good answer over the bad one; DPO means Direct Preference Optimization, the training method that produces that number. A hard-coded constant standing in for that number is dishonest telemetry, because it is reported as if measured when it was never measured.

The plan requires this (document claim). Row R-19 of the ledger states that a hard-coded literal value `0.03`, used as the `RewardMargin`, appears in the translation machine at `27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs` and must be eliminated in favour of the real, computed margin taken from the DPO trainer at `11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs` (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:466). A precision note directly under the row records the exact lines the plan saw at plan-time: `:70` (`RewardMargin: 0.03,`), `:125` (a ternary `? 0.03`), and `:187` (`RewardMargin: _trainingHistory.Count > 0 ? 0.03 : 0.0,`) (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:468-471).

What the code shows today (code fact). All three hard-coded `0.03` literals are still present, only shifted down by five lines. They now sit at 27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:75 (`RewardMargin: 0.03,`), 27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:130 (the ternary `? 0.03`), and 27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:192 (`RewardMargin: _trainingHistory.Count > 0 ? 0.03 : 0.0,`). The plan-cited :70/:125/:187 map exactly onto today's :75/:130/:192 -- a uniform +5 line drift, same code, same literal.

Whether the margin is now sourced from the real trainer (code fact). It is not. TranslationMachine.cs never calls the DPO trainer: a grep of the whole `27.TranslationDomain/` folder finds the string "DpoTrainer" only inside the project file `27.TranslationDomain/27.TranslationDomain.csproj:7`, which is a build-time project reference to `11.DPO`, not a call in the source. Meanwhile the real trainer does compute a genuine margin -- `float margin = chosenReward - rejectedReward;` and `RewardMargin = margin` (11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs:51 and :70), plus averaged forms `RewardMargin = marginSum / count` (11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs:127 and :173). So the honest, measured margin the plan wants used does exist and is reachable through the existing project reference, yet the translation machine ignores it and emits the constant 0.03.

The honesty nuance, stated as two separate true things. A document claim earlier in this report (Finding 22, report line 536-538) read the ternary forms as "honest telemetry" because at :130 and :192 the code returns 0.0 (not 0.03) whenever the preference condition is not satisfied -- so the field does not fabricate a margin where none is warranted (Finding 22 cited that ternary block around TranslationMachine.cs:128-131, consistent with today's :130). That reading and the plan's gate can both be true at once: the code is honest about WHEN a margin exists, but the MAGNITUDE it reports (0.03) is a fixed constant, not a value measured by the reward extractor. Honestly-labelled and plan-noncompliant are not in conflict here.

Per-defect verdict (b): PRESENT / gate NOT met. The three 0.03 literals survive at 27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:75/:130/:192, the margin is not sourced from 11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs, so the plan's "literal eliminated; margin from the engine" gate is unmet -- even though the value is honestly labelled.

(c) Train-ready pointer divergence, "V192 versus V309". A "train-ready pointer" is the single agreed name/version of the dataset that is cleared to be trained on. Row R-18 says two such names, V192 and V309, disagree and one canonical pointer must be chosen.

The plan requires this (document claim). Row R-18 of the ledger states there is a divergence between train-ready pointers "V192 vs V309" located in a document the plan calls "internal learnin 2", and that a single canonical pointer must be named before training; its gate is simply "a canonical pointer is named", and the plan routes the decision to an open question -- section E, Q21, owned by block 07 (active-codex-execution-ILEARNINGDYNAMICGRAPH-PLAN-v1.md:465).

What the code shows today (code fact). The two tokens are unrelated identifiers living in different subsystems, and they never meet. "V192" appears only under `experiments-v2/INTERNAL-TRAIN-READY-V191/`: it is a version of a merged DPO-pairs dataset, and the queue script names its source as an external path outside this repository -- `C:\Users\lubao\Documents\xiigen\llm mvp\internal learnin 2\...\dpo-pairs-merged-v192.jsonl` (experiments-v2/INTERNAL-TRAIN-READY-V191/queue-train-ready-v192-after-v191.ps1:6). "V309" appears only under `26.TrainableRouter/Training/`, where it is the identifier of an orchestration ROUTER unit and its training run -- `OrchestrationV309UnitId`, `V309TrainingRunId`, `V309DatasetSha256` (for example 26.TrainableRouter/Training/OrchestrationRoutingCurriculum.cs and 26.TrainableRouter/Training/DistributedRoutingBootstrap.cs:22). A check of every tracked file that mentions v192 confirms none of them also mentions v309: there is no single file, and no shared asset, where "V192 vs V309" is a live divergence. The document the plan points at, "internal learnin 2", is not tracked in this code repository at all (git lists no such file); it is an external planning/experiment folder under the user's Documents path.

Per-defect verdict (c): NO CODE DEFECT. This is a planning-document reconciliation item -- which of two dataset-pointer versions in an external document is canonical -- with no code referent. The plan itself treats it that way, routing it to an open question rather than a code fix. There is nothing in the code to be "present" or "resolved": V192 and V309 are simply two unrelated identifiers in two unrelated subsystems, not two competing pointers to one asset.

**Verdict:** recon defects = 2 of 3 are live CODE defects still present at HEAD 0f221ac89 -- (a) four sonnet-contaminated rows are unmarked-for-rebuild and are NOT rejected by the consuming trainer because they are labelled external_ai_used:false, so the marked-equals-found gate (0 == 4) is unmet (only mitigation: none are currently train-ready); (b) three hard-coded 0.03 RewardMargin literals survive at 27.TranslationDomain/TranslationMachine.cs:75/:130/:192 and are not sourced from 11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs, so the gate is unmet (the value is honestly labelled yet still a fixed constant, both true); and (c) is NOT a code defect at all -- the "V192 vs V309" divergence is a planning-document concern (an external "internal learnin 2" document), the two tokens are unrelated identifiers in different subsystems that never co-occur in code, so there is no code defect to remain.


### Finding 36 — LoRA adapters are built but never glued into the model's answer path (Cycle 5, Task 1)

**Plain-language background.** LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a cheap way to teach a large language model new behaviour. Instead of re-training the whole model, you freeze the original weights and bolt on small extra "correction" matrices called adapters; during teaching only the adapters change. At answer time the model is supposed to compute the original layer's output PLUS the adapter's learned correction. If that correction is never added in while the model computes an answer, all the teaching is thrown away -- the model answers exactly as if no LoRA had happened. This finding shows, with line evidence, that the second case is what this codebase does today: the adapters are constructed, but nothing in the "forward pass" (the computation that turns input text into the model's next-token prediction) ever runs through them.

**(1) What exists now.**

The adapter factory is `LoraApplicator` (the class that is supposed to prepare a model for LoRA teaching), in `10.LoRA/LoraApplicator.cs`. Its own header comment (lines 11-14), in Russian, says:

> "Подготавливает модель к LoRA fine-tuning: 1. Замораживает все базовые параметры. 2. Возвращает LoraLinear адаптеры для каждого подходящего слоя внимания. Forward pass базовой модели не меняется; адаптеры применяются снаружи."

Translation: "Prepares the model for LoRA fine-tuning: 1. Freezes all base parameters. 2. Returns LoraLinear adapters for each suitable attention layer. The base model's forward pass is not changed; adapters are applied from the outside." That last sentence is a written admission that the glue is missing: the computation that produces answers is deliberately left untouched, and the adapters are expected to be attached "from the outside" by some caller -- a caller that, as shown below, does not exist.

Concretely, `LoraApplicator.Apply` (10.LoRA/LoraApplicator.cs:17-45) does three things: it freezes every base weight (lines 20-21); it walks each transformer block's attention sub-layer and, by reflection, reads out each internal linear projection (lines 25-41 and helper 48-59); and for each matching projection it constructs a new `LoraLinear` adapter and adds it to a list (line 40). Crucially it only READS the projection fields (`field.GetValue`, line 57) -- it never writes an adapter back into the attention layer, and it never attaches the adapters to the model. It just returns the list (`return adapters;`, line 44) -- a detached collection of wrappers with no connection to the running model.

The adapter itself is `LoraLinear` (the object that computes "frozen output + low-rank correction"), in `10.LoRA/LoraLinear.cs`. Its `Forward` method (lines 52-63) does compute the intended math -- `base(x) + scaling * correction` (line 63) -- and it holds the only trainable parameters in the whole LoRA scheme, `lora_a` and `lora_b` (lines 47-48), where `lora_b` starts at zero so the correction starts at zero. There is a latent defect here even if the adapter were wired in: the dropout call hard-codes `training: true` at line 57 (`_dropout.Forward(x, training: true)`). Dropout is a teaching-time trick that randomly zeroes part of the input to prevent over-fitting; it must be turned OFF when the model answers, otherwise every answer gets random noise injected and becomes non-deterministic and degraded. Because that flag is a hard-coded constant instead of "on while teaching, off while answering", this adapter -- if it were ever used at answer time -- would corrupt answers with training noise. (Today that is harmless only because the adapter is never called at all.)

**(2) The forward path never touches an adapter.** The path that turns tokens into a prediction is:

- `LlmModel.Forward` (03.TransformerModel/LlmModel.cs:47-54): token embedding -> loop over blocks calling `block.Forward(x)` (lines 50-51) -> final norm -> output projection `_lmHead`. The embedding-only variant `ForwardHidden` (lines 57-63) is the same minus the last projection.
- `TransformerBlock.Forward` (03.TransformerModel/TransformerBlock.cs:40-46): line 43 calls `_attn.Forward(...)` -- the block's own private attention field, hard-wired.
- `CausalSelfAttention` (03.TransformerModel/Layers/CausalSelfAttention.cs) holds its four projections as `private readonly Linear _qProj/_kProj/_vProj/_oProj` (lines 17-20), and its `Forward` (lines 43-76) calls those fields directly: `_qProj.Forward(x)` (line 53), `_kProj.Forward(x)` (54), `_vProj.Forward(x)` (55) and `_oProj.Forward(combined)` (75).

Because the projection fields are `private readonly`, and because `LoraApplicator` only reads them and never substitutes anything, there is no line anywhere in the forward path that calls a `LoraLinear`. The `Attention` property (TransformerBlock.cs:25, commented "Открытый доступ для LoraApplicator" = "public access for LoraApplicator") exists purely so the factory can READ the projections, not to replace them. Result: the model's answers are computed entirely from the frozen base weights; the adapter correction is never added.

**Callers (non-test).** Two production call sites obtain the adapter list; both are case (c) -- they hold or count the adapters, then compute answers without them:

- `18.CapabilityTraining/LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:344`: `var adapters = LoraApplicator.Apply(...)`. The comment on line 343 claims "Apply LoRA adapters before DPO so the teaching path includes the adapter tier", but the `adapters` list is then used for exactly ONE thing -- its element count is copied into the result record as `LoraAdapterCount` (line 393). It is never passed to the trainer or the model. The subsequent DPO training (line 377, `trainer.StepVector`) runs through `model.Forward`, i.e. the frozen base only. And because `Apply` froze every base parameter and never registered the adapters on the model, the adapters' `lora_a`/`lora_b` are not part of `model.Parameters()`, so the DPO optimizer neither trains nor uses them. The adapters are built, counted for a report field, and dropped.
- `18.CapabilityTraining/TeachingRegistryPersistence.cs:322`: `LoraApplicator.Apply(model, ...)` -- here the return value is discarded entirely (not even captured in a variable). The model is then loaded from checkpoint (line 329) and handed to a k-NN embedding unit (line 345) that reads embeddings through the same base-only forward path. Case (c) again.

**(5) DPO scoring flows through the frozen base only.** `11.DPO/DpoTrainer.cs` (the preference-training step) scores the model in `LogProbs` (lines 240-274) and back-propagates in `BackwardLogProbs` (lines 277-323); both call `model.Forward(ids)` (lines 242 and 279). As established above, `model.Forward` never routes through an adapter, so both the preference score and its gradient are computed on the frozen base. The optimizer step (`_optimizer.Step(_policy.Parameters(), ...)`, e.g. lines 61 and 218) operates on the model's registered parameters, which (a) do not include the detached adapters and (b) were all frozen by `Apply`. So the DPO score does NOT flow through the adapters at any point.

**What is missing to finish the glue (pure code).** The adapters must actually stand in for the attention projections inside the live forward path, and their parameters must be registered on the model. The minimal concrete change is one of:

- Make `CausalSelfAttention`'s four projections swappable (replace the `private readonly Linear` fields with a settable slot, or add a `ReplaceProjection(name, module)` method), and have `LoraApplicator.Apply` write each new `LoraLinear` back into the matching slot AND register it as a sub-module of the model so its `lora_a`/`lora_b` appear in `model.Parameters()`.
