**Stop 1 detail — the "TrainableRouter" project: what, if anything, is actually trained.** The router names three moving parts. (a) `RoutingProfileTrainer.Train` (RoutingProfileTrainer.cs:27) is the only thing called "training": for each worked example it lower-cases the prompt, splits on punctuation, drops stop-words and short tokens (`ExtractKeywords`, :70), and stores the surviving words in a per-unit `HashSet<string>` — a bag of keywords. It attaches metadata (role, domain, a confidence floor, a base prior) taken from a HAND-AUTHORED table, `OrchestrationRoutingCurriculum.CreateDefaultUnitMetadataRegistry()` (called at RoutingProfileTrainer.cs:24, required at :57). So the ONLY thing "trained" is which keywords belong to which unit; there is no weight and no gradient. (b) At decision time `KeywordUnitScorer.Score` (KeywordUnitScorer.cs:43-56) is pure deterministic arithmetic over that bag — count of keyword hits plus the fixed `+20.0` domain bonus (:49), fixed `+2.0` direction bonus (:52), and the fixed confidence formula (:55). None of `20.0`, `2.0`, `0.12` is sourced from any training run; they are literals. (c) The wrapper `TrainableRoutingFlow.RouteAsync` (TrainableRoutingFlow.cs:35-65) simply takes the scorer's top-ranked unit as the decision (or defers to a low-confidence "knowledge edge" if a hand-set confidence/score-gap threshold is missed, :49-50). Every construction of this flow passes a `KeywordUnitScorer` and lives in the router's Training/bootstrap runners — RoutingTrainingRunner.cs:35, DistributedRoutingBootstrap.cs:35, CodeDocumentationTrainingRunner.cs:169 (plus tests). There is no other `IUnitScorer` implementation in production and no learned alternative. Net: behind the word "Trainable" the router is a keyword bag-builder feeding a keyword counter with hand-set constants; the keyword counter is the FINAL chooser of the router's flow, but that flow is only exercised by the router's own training/bootstrap harness, not by the main graph orchestrator (which routes sub-phases through row 2). This matches Finding 25; the one-line inventory entry above suffices — no re-description.

**Stop 2 detail — authority on the sub-phase routing product path.** `MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit` is the operative product-path router (its own header: it "makes deterministic routing decisions on the existing product path" and reports "never trained authority", MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:11-12). Its `ExecuteAsync` calls `DeterministicSubPhaseBaselineScorer.ScoreRelevant` (MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:110), turns each scored candidate into a decision tagged `BaselineCandidateGeneratorRole` (:116), and emits those decisions as the result (:180-227). The fixed blend at DeterministicSubPhaseBaselineScorer.cs:135 is therefore the LAST computation before the routing decision on this path — it is the final authority, not a mere baseline, because there is no learned scorer anywhere on this path to override it. The single-route `SubPhaseRouterUnit` is reached only when the baseline scores nothing (`scored.Length == 0`, MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.cs:120) — i.e. a fallback of a fallback. Crucially, the code actively REFUSES to let any learned authority take over here: if the single-router fallback returns anything other than `baseline_candidate_generator` it fails with `SINGLE_ROUTER_AUTHORITY_ROLE_INVALID` (:392-400, comment "Never normalize or launder learned, blank, or unknown authority through the baseline fallback"). So on the sub-phase product path the deterministic blend is BOTH the baseline AND the final decision-maker, precisely because the learned alternative (the EdgeAuthorityUnit / edge-authority pipeline) is a content-empty scaffold (Findings 2, 17, 34). The name is honest; the gap is authority. Remaining work: the honest replacement is deferred training of the EdgeAuthorityUnit on unit-level DPO pairs (STOP — do not design that training here), plus the in-scope code plumbing to route the final sub-phase decision through that trained unit once it exists. A human can confirm the cutover without running anything by checking that `MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit.ExecuteAsync` no longer stamps `BaselineCandidateGeneratorRole` as the emitted authority for its primary (non-fallback) path, and that the `0.55/0.25/0.20/0.02` blend at DeterministicSubPhaseBaselineScorer.cs:135 is no longer the last computation before the decision (it feeds candidates INTO a learned authority instead of BEING the decision).

**Stop 3 detail — the orchestrator profile catalog is not a hidden decision-maker.** `DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorProfileCatalog` (15.OrchestratorModel\Logical\DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorProfileCatalog.cs) is a bounded JSON catalog reader: `Select` (:93) validates a caller-supplied UTF-8 catalog (size cap, no BOM, canonical ids, no duplicate profile ids) and returns the single `DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorBootstrapProfile` whose `ProfileId` exactly equals the caller's `selectedProfileId` (:153-156); `ComposeSelected` (:55) then hands that profile to the composition factory. It classifies nothing about a prompt and picks no route — the "decision" of which profile is the caller's explicit id, not a hand-written lookup that maps input to an answer. So it is honest config plumbing, not a deterministic algorithm masquerading as a learned one; it does not belong on the replace list.

**Stop 4 detail — the nearest-neighbour lookup (answering with the closest memorized example).** Pinned exactly: `18.CapabilityTraining\LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:168` returns `example.Output` — the stored output text of the top nearest example, chosen by the fixed fusion at :203 over a trained embedder's ranking. It is registered as the `isLearned:true` "generated-capability" unit (DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:340). It IS the final authority for that unit's answer, but the learning only decides which memorized template ranks first; the answer itself is retrieval + a deterministic string translate (Finding 21). One-line inventory entry above; full treatment already in Finding 21 — no re-description.

**Stop 5 detail — mask sweep (every file/type whose name contains Trainable / Learned / Trained, exclusions applied).** Result by file: (a) `DynamicGraphUntrainedHonestyL2Probe.cs` and `DynamicGraphUntrainedHonestyMeter.cs` (21.AgentInfrastructure) are NOT masks — they are honesty PROBES whose job is to PROVE the graph is honestly untrained; the name "Untrained" is truthful. (b) `ITrainableRoutingFlow` / `TrainableRoutingFlow` (26) = the keyword-router flow, row 1 — mask lives at its scorer. (c) `TranslationTrainedUnits.cs` and `TrainedTranslationAdapter` (27) = the memorize-and-match units of row 8 — honest lexical, no semantic claim. (d) `ProjectWikiRequestRoutingTrainableModel` (42) = an HONEST real learned model (contrast section below). (e) `TrainedRouteComposerUnit` (42) = a pure text renderer (`RenderLearnedFlowPlan` / `RenderMode2Plan`, TrainedRouteComposerUnit.cs:10,38) that formats an already-chosen nearest/learned pair into lines — no training and no decision, so the "Trained" name is cosmetic, not a masked authority. (f) `DesignConversionTrainedUnits.cs` / `DesignConversionLearnedWeight*` / `DesignConversionLearnedWeightCheckpoint` (44) = the real gradient bridge of Finding 24 — honest. (g) `43.SmartSkillsDomain` `*LearnedPattern` / `*LearnedStateDescriptor` / `*LearnedGuideStateLoader` = the learned-state carriers for the row-10 materializers — honest, `semantic_quality_claimed:false` (Finding 23). (h) NEW mask found: `35.ExperimentsV2\Experiments\E53HighLevelOrchestrationProof.cs` defines ten `Flow01Node0N Trained…` static classes (e.g. `Flow01Node01TrainedDomainSplitter` :391, `Flow01Node00TrainedLogicalCompiler` :461) whose "learned" content is HAND-AUTHORED literal rule tables — e.g. `LearnedDomainPacketRule[] LearnedRules` holds eleven hardcoded rows (:394-407) and `Split` merely filters those hardcoded rules by which signals are present (:416-419). The "Trained"/"Learned" names are a MASK over hand-written rule tables — but this file lives in an Experiments/PROOF harness (35.ExperimentsV2), not on any production decision path, so it is a naming honesty defect in an experiment, not a live authority. (i) Test files (`Tests\…TrainableRouterOrchestrationTests.cs`, `…UntrainedHonesty…Tests.cs`, `…TrainedSelectorEvidenceTests.cs`) excluded as test-only.

**Contrast — the "Trainable/Trained" names that ARE honest learned mechanisms (so the operator knows which is which).** (1) `ProjectWikiRequestRoutingTrainableModel` (42.ProjectWikiDomain\ProjectWikiRequestRoutingTrainableModel.cs) is a GENUINE trained multiclass perceptron, not a keyword counter: `Train` (:58) runs 18 epochs of perceptron weight updates — for each row it featurizes the prompt into token+bigram counts (`Featurize`, :341), predicts, and when wrong nudges the learned `_tokenWeights` toward the correct label and away from the predicted one (:71-79); at decision time `Route`/`DecideOrDefer` scores with those LEARNED weights (`Score`, :241-253) and only commits when a held-out evaluation gate passed (`HasPassedEvaluationGate` requires RouteAccuracy >= 0.95, WrongAnnotationRate == 1.0, AverageConfidence >= 0.85, :270-275), otherwise it honestly defers to "NeedsSecondPass" (:162-201). Its name is honest and its routing authority is a learned model. (Caveat: the confidence CALIBRATION and thresholds are hand-set constants, `CalibratedConfidence` :322-330 — a deterministic wrapper around a learned core, not a masked core.) (2) Design-conversion's gradient bridge (44, Finding 24) and the project-wiki gradient DPO via `DpoTrainer.StepMany` (Finding 20) are the other two honest trained mechanisms. These three are the evidence that the repo CAN name a learned thing correctly — which is what makes rows 1, 3 and 11 (keyword counter / retrieval / stub, all wearing trainable-family names) stand out as the masks to fix.

**Verdict.** The complete map: FOUR live deterministic mechanisms sit where the plan wants learned decisions — (row 1) `KeywordUnitScorer` keyword-counter with a hand-set `+20.0`/`+2.0`/`0.12` scoring (final chooser of the TrainableRouter flow, but that flow is a training/bootstrap harness, not the main orchestrator; MASK); (row 2) `DeterministicSubPhaseBaselineScorer.cs:135` fixed `0.55/0.25/0.20/0.02` blend, which IS the FINAL authority on the live sub-phase routing product path via `MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit` and actively refuses to let a learned authority launder through (:392-400; HONEST name, authority gap); (row 3) `TransformerEmbeddingKnnLogicalUnit` nearest-neighbour lookup returning a memorized template (`example.Output`, LlmIngredientLogicalUnits.cs:168), registered `isLearned:true` (the learned stamp is the mask); and (row 11) the LoRA stub. Stop 3's `DynamicGraphLogicalOrchestratorProfileCatalog` is NOT on this list — it is an honest caller-keyed config selector, not a hidden decision-maker. Rows 5-10 are already fully described in Findings 20-28 and appear here as one-line entries. The mask sweep adds one NEW naming-honesty defect — the `Flow01Node0N Trained…` classes in `35.ExperimentsV2\Experiments\E53HighLevelOrchestrationProof.cs` are hand-authored rule tables wearing "Trained"/"Learned" names — but in a proof harness, off the production path. Three names in the family are honestly learned: `ProjectWikiRequestRoutingTrainableModel` (a real perceptron with a held-out gate), design-conversion's gradient bridge (Finding 24), and project-wiki's gradient DPO (Finding 20). For every deterministic row the honest replacement is DEFERRED TRAINING of the edge authority on pairs that already exist (STOP — not designed here); the IN-SCOPE code plumbing is to (i) route the row-2 sub-phase decision through that trained unit once it exists so the fixed blend feeds candidates instead of BEING the decision, (ii) demote rows 1/5/6/7 to labelled baseline candidate-generators, and (iii) delete or rename the mask names (row 11 stub, the E53 "Trained" experiment classes, the `isLearned:true` stamp on the row-3 retrieval unit). A human can confirm each fix statically: row 2 done when `MultiSubPhaseRouterUnit` no longer emits `BaselineCandidateGeneratorRole` as its primary authority and the `0.55/0.25/0.20/0.02` blend is no longer the last step before the decision; row 1 done when the `20.0` constant is gone and `KeywordUnitScorer`'s output no longer selects the final unit; row 3 done when the answer path no longer returns `example.Output` verbatim under an `isLearned:true` label.

### Finding 41 — Quality gates hard-coded OFF and a test-only handoff: the unfinished tails of the best-disciplined domain (Cycle 5, Task 6)

**What this checks, in plain words.** The design-conversion domain (`44.DesignConversionDomain` — the subsystem that turns a design mock-up, e.g. a Figma or Canva frame, into HTML/CSS code) is the repo's closest approach to real training discipline. Three tails are unfinished: (1) a MEASUREMENT GATE — the checkpoint that is supposed to demand "the trained thing scored well on held-out examples, and stayed inside runtime/cost/memory budget" before a result counts — whose enforcement is not actually fed real numbers; (2) a HANDOFF — the step that hands a trained/promoted result to its next consumer (a model server) — whose real completion is never reached in the running product; and (3) three hard-coded `0.03` preference-margin literals that Finding 35 already located and that need a single source of truth. Audited repo: C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation, branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45 (git rev-parse verified before and after; git status --porcelain empty/clean before and after); read and grep only — no build, no tests, no edits, no commits. Greps excluded bin, obj, .claire\worktrees, .clone\worktrees; Tests\ treated as test-only evidence.

**Glossary (identifiers translated at first use).** "Held-out examples" = test examples deliberately kept away from training, used to prove real learning rather than memorization. "Measurement gate" = the pass/fail check that a trained result met a held-out quality score and stayed within runtime/cost/peak-RAM budgets. "Thresholds" = the actual numbers the gate compares against (the required score, the max runtime in milliseconds, max cost in US dollars, max peak RAM in megabytes). "Handoff" = handing the trained/promoted result to its next consumer (here, uploading the promoted checkpoint to a model server). "Checkpoint" = a saved file of a trained model's state. "Preference margin" = how much more the model must prefer the good answer over the bad one (the minimum preference strength in preference training). "Fail-closed" = when in doubt the code blocks rather than allows.

---

**TAIL 1 — the measurement gate is fed no real numbers, so it can only ever fail-closed; it is not "switched to always-pass".**

*What exists now.* The gate evaluator lives in `44.DesignConversionDomain\DesignConversionMeasurementGate.cs`. Its threshold record is `DesignConversionMeasurementGateThresholds(RequiredHeldOutQualityScore, MaxRuntimeMs, MaxCostUsd, MaxPeakRamMb)` (:32-36). The four flags the task points at are at lines 214-218:

```
return new ThresholdValidity(
    RequiredHeldOutQualityScore: false,
    MaxRuntimeMs: false,
    MaxCostUsd: false,
    MaxPeakRamMb: false);
```

Read in context, these four `false` values are NOT a switch that makes the gate pass without checking. They are a small internal "is each budget number usable?" record (`ThresholdValidity`) returned by `ValidateThresholds` ONLY on the branch where the caller passed no thresholds object at all (`if (thresholds is null)`, :211) — and on that very same branch the method first records the failure reason `MEASUREMENT_GATE_THRESHOLDS_REQUIRED` (:213). So the honest reading is: "no threshold numbers were supplied, so skip the four comparisons AND fail the gate for missing thresholds." Each flag, when true, would demand exactly one comparison inside `Evaluate`: RequiredHeldOutQualityScore turns on "held-out quality score is below the required score" (:99-102); MaxRuntimeMs turns on "runtime exceeded the millisecond budget" (:108-109); MaxCostUsd turns on "cost exceeded the dollar budget" (:115-116); MaxPeakRamMb turns on "peak RAM exceeded the megabyte budget" (:122-123). While the flags are false those four comparisons never fire.

*What "turning them ON" actually requires.* When a caller DOES pass a populated thresholds object, the same method turns each flag on by numeric validity, not by editing those literals: the quality-score threshold must be a real number in 0..1 (`IsValidScore`, :221 and :283-286), the runtime budget must be > 0 (:228), the cost budget >= 0 (:232), the RAM budget > 0 (:236). So "turning the flags on" = supplying a real `DesignConversionMeasurementGateThresholds` with real numbers. The NUMBERS themselves (what held-out score is "good enough", what runtime/cost/RAM budget is acceptable) can only come from measurement data gathered on real training runs — that part is DEFERRED (boundary marked; STOP — do not invent numbers here). Making the thresholds a required, configuration-sourced input on the production path is pure code work.

*What is missing (why the gate is effectively off in production).* The request that carries thresholds and held-out metrics is an OPTIONAL field defaulting to null: `DesignConversionTrainingPipelineOptions( ... DesignConversionMeasurementGateRequest? MeasurementGateRequest = null)` (DesignConversionTrainingPipeline.cs:21). The training gate runs the evaluator ONLY when that field is non-null: `var measurementResult = options.MeasurementGateRequest is null ? null : _measurementGate.Evaluate(options.MeasurementGateRequest);` (DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGate.cs:391-393). A repo-wide grep for every construction of the request or thresholds (`new DesignConversionMeasurementGateRequest`, `new DesignConversionMeasurementGateThresholds`) matches ONLY test files (9 of them: DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGateTests, DesignConversionMeasurementGateTests, DesignConversionPromotionTests, DesignConversionModelServerTrainingIntakeTests, DesignConversionModelServerImprovementLoopTests, DesignConversionModelServerApiTests, DesignConversionModelLifecycleTests, DesignConversionLocalFullLoopE2eTests, DesignConversionDpoStorageTests); the single non-test match is the `= null` default itself. So in the running product no real threshold numbers exist anywhere, the evaluator's PASS path is unreachable, and the gate is permanently fail-closed rather than "always-pass".

*Where the verdict actually BLOCKS versus is merely recorded.* It blocks in three fail-closed layers — all currently stuck at "blocked" precisely because no real numbers are ever supplied: (a) `DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGate.EvaluateRecords` makes `readyForTraining` require `measurementPassed` (:415-421); with no request it is false, so the status is `blocked`/`training_candidate_only`, never `ready_for_training`. (b) `DesignConversionTrainingPipeline.BuildPromotionStatus` returns `"PROMOTION_BLOCKED_MEASUREMENT_GATE_NOT_PASSED"` whenever the gate did not pass (:720-721). (c) `DesignConversionPromotionPublisher.PublishAsync` — if a decision would otherwise allow upload but the measurement gate did not pass, it REPLACES the decision with Reject and never calls the uploader (:245-260). Additionally `DesignConversionPromotionGateEvaluator.Decide` rejects because, with no measurement present, `BuildLocalPromotionGateReport` fills in a fallback held-out score of `0.0` (DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:7697-7699) against a fallback required score of `1.0` (:7700-7702), a gap far larger than the continue-training margin, so `HeldOutScore < RequiredHeldOutScore` forces Reject (DesignConversionPromotion.cs:140-155). What is MERELY RECORDED (blocks nothing by itself) is the `DesignConversionMeasurementGateResult` object and its evidence strings — e.g. the evaluator, when never run, emits the marker `heldout_measurement_gate:not_run` (DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGate.cs:601) — which the operator can read but which only feed the blocking layers above.

*Code work vs deferred training.* CODE (in scope): make `MeasurementGateRequest` a required, config-sourced input on the production training/promotion path so the evaluator actually runs, and source the thresholds from a configuration document rather than leaving them absent. DEFERRED (training data needed; STOP): the actual threshold numbers, which require measurement runs to justify.

---

**TAIL 2 — the handoff is CALLED in production; what is test-only is the remote uploader and the pass condition. The earlier cycle's "only Tests exercise it" is REFUTED.**

*What exists now.* The handoff pipeline is `DesignConversionPromotionPublisher.PublishAsync` (DesignConversionPromotion.cs:190-206): it runs the promotion gate (`_gateEvaluator.Decide`, :229), publishes the manifest metadata via `DesignConversionManifestPublisher.PublishManifestMetadataAsync` (:305), and — if every gate allows it — performs the actual handoff `await _uploader.UploadAsync(manifest, gateReport, ct)` to the model server (:345). Contrary to the earlier cycle's claim, this publisher IS constructed and invoked from PRODUCTION code, not only tests: `var publication = await new DesignConversionPromotionPublisher(publicationUploader).PublishAsync(...)` at DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:3028, inside `JsonlDesignConversionLocalArtifactDb` (class at :627). The same production method also invokes the checkpoint-to-runtime handoff `DesignConversionDpoPolicyCheckpointBridge.ExecuteAndConvertAsync(...)` at :3013. This method (`RunTrainingIntakeAndPromoteLocalCoreAsync`, :2881, reached via the public `RunTrainingIntakeAndPromoteLocalAsync`, :2817) is wired to a REAL HTTP endpoint: `DesignConversionModelServerTrainingIntakeHandler` calls `_api.RunTrainingIntakeAndPromoteLocalAsync(...)` at DesignConversionModelServerTrainingIntakeHandler.cs:503 and :548; that handler's promote-local route is mapped by `MapDesignConversionModelServerTrainingIntake` -> `endpoints.MapPost(TrainingIntakeEndpointPath, ...)` (DesignConversionModelServerAspNetEndpointMapper.cs:140-142); and the handler is constructed in the ASP.NET host startup (DesignConversionModelServerAspNetHostStartup.cs:825-826), with the usefulness API delegating straight through to the artifact DB (DesignConversionDpoUsefulnessApi.cs:218-223). So the handoff CODE runs on a real product path.

*What is actually test-only / never-real in production (the honest "unfinished" part).* Two things, not the call itself: (a) The REMOTE uploader — the implementation that would actually ship artifacts to an external model server, `DesignConversionModelServerPromotionHttpUploader : IDesignConversionPromotionUploader` (25.ModelServer\DesignConversion\DesignConversionModelServerPromotionHttpUploader.cs:48) — is constructed only when the host is configured with `PromotionHttpUploaderOptions` + `PromotionHttpClient` (DesignConversionModelServerAspNetHostStartup.cs:1250-1257) and is handed to the artifact DB as its `_promotionUploader` (:1259). But every construction of its options record `DesignConversionModelServerPromotionHttpUploaderOptions` is in Tests. With no such config, the default in production is `publicationUploader = _promotionUploader ?? new LocalOnlyTrainingIntakePromotionUploader()` (DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:3027), and that local uploader writes a local receipt and explicitly attempts NO remote upload (returns `UploadAttempted = false`, evidence `EXTERNAL_CLOUD_UPLOAD_NOT_ATTEMPTED`, :7752-7776). (b) The PASS condition — because Tail 1's measurement gate is never fed real numbers, `PublishAsync` always short-circuits to Reject at :245-260 before the uploader is reached. So the production handoff runs but can never COMPLETE a real promotion.

*The production seam where the real handoff is missing.* Not "a caller that constructs the publisher" — that caller already exists (DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:3028). The missing seams are: (i) a production configuration that supplies the real remote uploader (`DesignConversionModelServerPromotionHttpUploaderOptions` today built only in Tests), and (ii) a production caller that supplies a real `MeasurementGateRequest` with real thresholds and real held-out metrics (Tail 1) so the gate can pass and let the upload proceed. Until both exist, the handoff is exercised end-to-end only under Tests.

*Code work vs deferred training.* CODE (in scope): wire a real `IDesignConversionPromotionUploader` from host configuration on the production promotion path, and pass a populated `MeasurementGateRequest` into the training/promotion options. DEFERRED: the held-out metrics and threshold numbers that make the gate actually pass (same boundary as Tail 1).

---

**TAIL 3 — the three `0.03` preference-margin literals: the single-source-of-truth finish (do NOT re-detect; Finding 35 already located them).**

*What exists now.* The three literals are at `27.TranslationDomain\TranslationMachine.cs:75` (`RewardMargin: 0.03` on the legacy scaffold path), `:130` (`? 0.03 : 0.0` on the structured path when every rejected output is tagged `[WRONG:`), and `:192` (`RewardMargin: _trainingHistory.Count > 0 ? 0.03 : 0.0` in evaluation). Each feeds the `RewardMargin` field of `LearningBatchResult` / `LearningEvaluationResult`, which is typed `double` (00.Core\LearningMachineContracts.cs:58 and :103). The "central trainer" `11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs` does NOT currently own a minimum-margin constant: its only margin-related members are `private const float Beta = 0.1f` (:18 — a different hyperparameter, the KL-penalty coefficient, and private so unreachable from outside anyway) and the dynamically COMPUTED `float margin = chosenReward - rejectedReward` (:51, :103, :156, :204) written into `DpoMetrics.RewardMargin` (:70, :127, :173, :227). A grep for any named minimum-margin constant (`MinimumPreferenceMargin`, `PreferenceMargin`, `MinMargin`, `MinimumMargin`) across 00.Core, 11.DPO and 27.TranslationDomain returns nothing — so there is no shared source today.

*What is missing / the exact finish.* Define ONE canonical public constant on the central trainer and point all three literals at it. Concretely: add `public const double MinimumPreferenceMargin = 0.03;` to `DpoTrainer` in 11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs, alongside its existing public constants `PolicyCheckpointFilePrefix` and `PolicyCheckpointFileExtension` (:19-20); then replace the literals at TranslationMachine.cs:75, :130, :192 with `DpoTrainer.MinimumPreferenceMargin`. No new project reference is required: `27.TranslationDomain\27.TranslationDomain.csproj` ALREADY references `..\11.DPO\DPO.csproj` (with the in-file comment "11.DPO provides DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) training infrastructure"); TranslationMachine.cs need only add `using DPO;`. Type note: declare the constant `double` (not `float` like `Beta`) so the double-typed `RewardMargin` fields consume it directly, with no precision-losing cast.

*Code work vs deferred training.* Entirely CODE (in scope): moving a literal into a shared named constant needs no training data. Nothing here is deferred.

---

**How a human could later confirm each fix without running anything.** Tail 1: the production training/promotion path constructs a `DesignConversionMeasurementGateRequest` with a populated `DesignConversionMeasurementGateThresholds` (real numbers) rather than leaving `MeasurementGateRequest` null — grep for `new DesignConversionMeasurementGateThresholds` / `new DesignConversionMeasurementGateRequest` returns a match OUTSIDE Tests, and `DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGate.cs:391` no longer resolves `measurementResult` to null on the product path. (The four flags at MeasurementGate.cs:214-218 stay as-is — they are the honest "no thresholds" fallback; the real proof is that thresholds are now supplied, so `ValidateThresholds`'s populated branch at :221-238 sets the flags true.) Tail 2: a non-test construction of `DesignConversionModelServerPromotionHttpUploaderOptions` exists (a configured remote uploader), and the production caller at DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:3028 can reach `_uploader.UploadAsync` (DesignConversionPromotion.cs:345) because the measurement gate now passes rather than short-circuiting to Reject at DesignConversionPromotion.cs:245-260. Tail 3: grep of `27.TranslationDomain\TranslationMachine.cs` for `0.03` returns ZERO matches, the three sites read `DpoTrainer.MinimumPreferenceMargin`, and `11.DPO\DpoTrainer.cs` declares exactly one `public const double MinimumPreferenceMargin = 0.03`.

**Verdict.** The header's "hard-coded OFF" and "test-only handoff" are the blunt shorthand; the precise state is sharper and more honest. The measurement gate is not switched to always-pass — it is permanently fail-closed because production never feeds it a `MeasurementGateRequest` with real thresholds (every such construction is in Tests), so `readyForTraining` (DesignConversionTrainingMeasurementGate.cs:415-421), the promotion status (DesignConversionTrainingPipeline.cs:720-721), and the upload decision (DesignConversionPromotion.cs:245-260) all sit stuck at "blocked". The handoff is NOT test-only as an earlier cycle claimed — it is called in production from JsonlDesignConversionLocalArtifactDb (DesignConversionLocalArtifactDb.cs:3013 bridge, :3028 publisher) behind a real model-server HTTP endpoint — but its real completion is unreachable because the default uploader is a local-receipt no-op (:7752-7776, remote uploader options built only in Tests) and the unpassable measurement gate rejects before upload. The `0.03` margin is a pure single-source-of-truth cleanup: add `DpoTrainer.MinimumPreferenceMargin` and reference it from TranslationMachine.cs:75/130/192. Only the threshold NUMBERS (Tails 1 and 2) are deferred to measurement data; all the plumbing — thresholds supplied and configurable, a configured production uploader, the margin single-sourced — is code work.

### Finding 42 — One shared manifest file name for all domains plus a recursive by-name loader: silent collision by design (Cycle 5, Task 7)

**What this checks, in plain words.** Every trained capability writes a "manifest" (the passport file describing one trained checkpoint — which model file, which tokenizer file, which domain, which unit identifier, and which training settings rebuild that capability). ALL domains write that passport under the SAME file name, `generated_capability_manifest.json`. On first use the trainer builds an in-memory index of every capability it can restore by walking the WHOLE checkpoint folder tree, grabbing every file that happens to carry that name at any depth, and filing each one into a dictionary keyed by the manifest's unit identifier (its capability id). The filing is a blind overwrite: if two passports carry the same unit id, the second silently replaces the first, with no guard, no warning, and no domain check. This finding proves the collision mechanics from the code and specifies the minimal code fix. Audited repo: C:\Projects\llm_mvp_core-DpoImplementation, branch DpoImplementation, HEAD 0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45 (git rev-parse verified before and after; git status --porcelain empty/clean before and after); read and grep only — no build, no tests, no edits, no commits. Greps excluded bin, obj, .claire\worktrees, .clone\worktrees; Tests\ treated as test-only evidence.

**Glossary (identifiers translated at first use).** "Manifest" = the passport JSON describing one trained checkpoint. "Checkpoint" = a saved file of a trained model's state (here `policy.bin`) plus its `tokenizer.json`. "UnitId" = the unit/capability identifier, the string that names one taught capability. "Checkpoint root" (`_checkpointRoot`) = the top folder under which all capabilities' checkpoint sub-folders live. "`SearchOption.AllDirectories`" = search by file name through every sub-folder, any depth. "Hydrate" (`EnsureManifestCacheHydrated`) = fill the in-memory index once, on first use. "Last-one-wins" = the later file silently replaces the earlier one in the index. "DomainTag" = the label saying which domain (subject area) a capability belongs to. "Sanitize" = a helper that lowercases a string and turns every non-letter/non-digit character into a hyphen.

---

**1. The shared file name and the recursive by-name loader (quotes).**

*The constant.* `18.CapabilityTraining\TeachingRegistryPersistence.cs:18`:

```
public const string GeneratedCapabilityManifestFileName = "generated_capability_manifest.json";
```

This one name is used by every domain and every run — there is no domain or run suffix in it.

*The loader loop.* `18.CapabilityTraining\DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:577-592` (method `EnsureManifestCacheHydrated`):

```
private void EnsureManifestCacheHydrated()
{
    if (_manifestCacheHydrated)
        return;

    _manifestCacheHydrated = true;
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(_checkpointRoot) || !Directory.Exists(_checkpointRoot))
        return;

    foreach (var manifestPath in Directory.GetFiles(_checkpointRoot, TeachingRegistryPersistence.GeneratedCapabilityManifestFileName, SearchOption.AllDirectories))
    {
        var json = File.ReadAllText(manifestPath);
        var manifest = System.Text.Json.JsonSerializer.Deserialize<GeneratedCapabilityCheckpointManifest>(json);
        if (manifest is not null)
            _manifestCacheByUnitId[manifest.UnitId] = manifest;
    }
}
```

The index is the field `private readonly Dictionary<string, GeneratedCapabilityCheckpointManifest> _manifestCacheByUnitId = new(StringComparer.Ordinal);` (:35).

*Order of duplicates — the code imposes none.* `Directory.GetFiles(..., SearchOption.AllDirectories)` returns files in an order the .NET API does not guarantee (it follows the underlying filesystem's enumeration, not a documented sort). The cache-fill loop here applies NO ordering of its own before filling. So when two files share a unit id, WHICH one ends up as the surviving value is decided by undefined filesystem order. (Note the contrast: the separate capture-path loader `TeachingRegistryPersistence.LoadGeneratedCapabilityManifests` at :289-292 does `.OrderBy(CreatedAtUtc).ThenBy(UnitId)` — but only AFTER collecting every file into a list, and it keeps duplicates as separate list entries; the trainer's cache loop above sorts nothing and keeps only one value per unit id.)

*Two manifests, same unit id — silent overwrite.* The fill is a plain dictionary indexer assignment, `_manifestCacheByUnitId[manifest.UnitId] = manifest;` (:591). There is no `ContainsKey` check, no conflict result, no log — the second passport silently overwrites the first (last-one-wins).

*Domain filter — there is none at load time.* Nothing in the loop looks at `DomainTag`. Every passport found anywhere under the checkpoint root is loaded into the one shared index regardless of which domain it belongs to. Domain filtering happens only far downstream, at query time (see point 3), never at hydration.

*Corrupt / mismatched-schema JSON — it throws and aborts the WHOLE hydration; it is NOT swallowed.* The loop body has no try/catch. `File.ReadAllText` and `JsonSerializer.Deserialize` throw on a malformed JSON file or a type-mismatched schema (a `JsonException`), and that exception propagates straight OUT of `EnsureManifestCacheHydrated`, up through its callers `LoadGeneratedCapabilityManifests` (:562-569) and `CountGeneratedCapabilityManifests` (:571-575), aborting the entire index build. The `manifest is not null` check at :590 does NOT cover this — it only guards the case where deserialization RETURNS null (a JSON `null` literal), not the exception path. Worse, `_manifestCacheHydrated = true` is set at :582 BEFORE the loop, so after a throw the index is left partially filled AND already flagged as hydrated; the early-return at :579-580 means it is never re-attempted. (Contrast the capture-path loader at :281-287, which is also unguarded per-file but sits inside `Capture`'s outer try/catch at :41-66, so there a bad file degrades to a graceful `SNAPSHOT_CAPTURE_FAILED` result instead of an unhandled throw.)

**2. Where manifests are WRITTEN, and how a duplicate UnitId realistically arises.**

*Per-checkpoint sub-folders.* `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs` builds one sub-folder per (run, domain, sub-phase) and drops the passport into it under the shared name:

- `:269` `var checkpointDirectory = Path.Combine(_checkpointRoot, Sanitize(request.RunId), Sanitize(domainAdapterTag), Sanitize(subPhaseId));`
- `:275` `var manifestPath = Path.Combine(checkpointDirectory, TeachingRegistryPersistence.GeneratedCapabilityManifestFileName);`
- written by `TeachingRegistryPersistence.SaveGeneratedCapabilityManifest(manifest, manifestPath)` (:319), which just `File.WriteAllText`s the JSON (TeachingRegistryPersistence.cs:85), and is also cached in-process immediately at :329.

So the on-disk layout is `{root}/{run}/{domain}/{sub-phase}/generated_capability_manifest.json`. Every domain uses the identical file name in its own sub-folder — exactly the shape the recursive by-name loader then flattens back into one index.

*How UnitId is assigned.* `:270`:

```
var unitId = $"{GeneratedCapabilityUnitRole}-{Sanitize(domainAdapterTag)}-{Sanitize(subPhaseId)}-{Sanitize(request.RunId)}";
```

So a unit id is `role + sanitized-domain + sanitized-subphase + sanitized-runId`. Because the domain and run are inside the id, two DIFFERENT domains normally get DIFFERENT ids — the header's "silent collision by design" is therefore not automatic on every pair; it is real on two concrete, realistic paths:

- **Same run re-teaching the same domain + sub-phase** (a retry, or re-running teaching with the same `RunId`) produces an IDENTICAL unit id AND the identical folder path, so it clobbers both the file on disk and the index slot — silently.
- **Sanitize collapse.** `Sanitize` (:921-926) lowercases and maps every non-alphanumeric character to a hyphen, collapsing runs of hyphens: `char.IsLetterOrDigit(ch) ? char.ToLowerInvariant(ch) : '-'` then split/rejoin on `-`. So genuinely different domain tags such as `"Legal EU"`, `"legal-eu"`, and `"legal/eu"` all sanitize to `legal-eu`. Two distinct domains (or two distinct run ids) that collapse to the same slug yield the SAME unit id, the SAME sub-folder, and the SAME passport file — a cross-domain clobber, both on disk and in the shared index, with no guard anywhere.

**3. The replay path (`FindReplayManifests`) DOES filter by domain — but reads from the already-polluted shared index.** `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:596-611`:

```
var candidates = LoadGeneratedCapabilityManifests()
    .Where(manifest => string.Equals(manifest.DomainTag, domainAdapterTag, StringComparison.Ordinal))
    .OrderByDescending(manifest => manifest.CreatedAtUtc)
    .Take(10)
    .ToArray();
```

`LoadGeneratedCapabilityManifests()` (:562-569) returns `_manifestCacheByUnitId.Values` — the one shared index hydrated with EVERY domain's passports. The replay query then applies a `DomainTag == domainAdapterTag` filter, so replay does not indiscriminately RETURN foreign domains. But it inherits any damage the shared index already suffered: if a foreign-domain passport overwrote a same-id slot (via the Sanitize collapse above), the target domain's own passport is simply gone from the index, and the domain filter then drops the foreign one too — the target loses its replay history entirely; if instead two runs of the SAME domain collapsed, only the last-written survives and older replay entries vanish. Either way the filter cannot recover data the blind overwrite already destroyed. (The same shared index also feeds `ResolveGeneratedCapabilityManifest`, whose filter at :551-558 likewise checks `UnitRole` + `DomainTag` at query time, never at load.)

**4. Second instance of the same recursive-by-name pattern (pattern evidence only).** `experiments-v2\XIIGEN-DOCS-BATCH-COMPARISON\runner\XiigenDocsBatchComparisonRunner\Program.cs:723`:

```
? Directory.GetFiles(checkpointRoot, "generated_capability_manifest.json", SearchOption.AllDirectories)
```

Same "find every file with that one name, at any depth" shape, inside `BuildLoraAudit`. This is the experiments folder, not production — noted only to show the by-name-everywhere pattern recurs; no deep-dive.

**What exists now / what is missing / is this pure code work.**

- *Exists now:* one shared manifest file name for all domains (constant at :18); a recursive by-name loader that flattens every passport under the root into one index (:586-592); a blind last-one-wins dictionary write with no duplicate guard (:591); NO domain filter at load time; NO per-file error handling, so one corrupt/mismatched file throws and aborts the whole hydration while leaving it flagged hydrated (:582 before the loop); and a UnitId scheme (:270) that collides on same-run retries and on Sanitize collapses.
- *Missing (the guards):* (a) a collision guard that refuses (or deterministically, loudly resolves) a duplicate unit id instead of silently overwriting; (b) domain scoping so foreign domains' passports cannot enter another domain's working set — either restrict the recursive search to the domain's own sub-tree (the folder layout `{root}/{run}/{domain}/{sub-phase}` already carries the domain, the loader just ignores it) or add a `DomainTag == expected` check right after deserialize; (c) defined behaviour for a corrupt/mismatched passport — wrap each file's read+deserialize in try/catch so one bad file is skipped with a recorded note rather than aborting the whole hydration, and do not cement `_manifestCacheHydrated = true` on a partial/failed pass; (d) optionally a deterministic sort of the file list before the fill loop so any surviving winner is defined, not filesystem-order-dependent (moot once duplicates are refused). A domain-scoped file NAME (embedding the sanitized domain in the file name) or a domain-scoped sub-folder search would also stop the by-name sweep from crossing domains at its root.
- *Pure code work — yes, explicitly.* This is file listing, JSON parsing, dictionary-key policy, and folder/name scoping. No training runs, no measurement data, no held-out metrics are involved. There is NO deferred-training boundary here — the entire fix is code, and nothing needs to STOP for data.

**How a human could later confirm the fix without running anything.**

- *Duplicate guard:* grep `DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs` shows the blind `_manifestCacheByUnitId[manifest.UnitId] = manifest;` (:591) replaced by a `ContainsKey`/`TryAdd` check that returns or records a conflict; a test that drops two passports with the same UnitId under two sub-folders asserts a rejection/conflict result instead of a silent last-one-wins survivor.
- *Domain scoping:* the `Directory.GetFiles` call is scoped to the domain's sub-tree, or a `manifest.DomainTag == expectedDomain` filter appears right after the deserialize; a test that places a foreign-domain passport under the root asserts it does NOT enter the target domain's cache or replay set.
- *Corrupt-manifest behaviour:* each file's read+deserialize is wrapped in try/catch and `_manifestCacheHydrated` is set only after a clean pass; a test that drops a malformed JSON file under the root asserts the good passports still load and a skip note is recorded, rather than the hydration throwing and cementing a partial index.
- *Deterministic order:* the file list is sorted before the fill loop, so two same-id passports yield a defined winner regardless of filesystem order (or the duplicate is refused outright, making order irrelevant).

**Verdict.** The header is exact. There is one manifest file name for all domains (TeachingRegistryPersistence.cs:18), and the trainer's hydration walks the entire checkpoint tree for that one name and files each passport into a single unit-id-keyed dictionary with a blind overwrite (DefaultLogicalCapabilityTrainer.cs:586-591) — no ordering, no duplicate guard, no domain filter, no per-file error handling. The unit id embeds domain and run (:270), so collisions are not universal, but they are real and silent on two ordinary paths: a same-run retry, and the Sanitize helper (:921-926) collapsing two distinct domain or run labels to one slug — either of which overwrites both the on-disk file and the index slot. The replay reader does filter by domain (:602) yet reads from that already-polluted shared index, so it cannot undo an overwrite that already deleted a domain's own passport. A corrupt or schema-mismatched file throws and aborts the whole hydration while the index is already flagged hydrated (:582), so the failure is neither swallowed nor recoverable on retry. The same by-name-everywhere pattern recurs once more in the experiments runner (Program.cs:723). The complete fix — a duplicate-id guard, domain-scoped loading or file naming, defined corrupt-file behaviour, and deterministic ordering — is entirely code work with no deferred-training boundary.

### Finding 43 — The code's own confessions: a complete inventory of self-declared unfinished spots in the DPO-adjacent folders (Cycle 5, Task 8)

**Scope and method.** Read-only sweep at HEAD `0f221ac89cff57af4205d342ead51213425c0c45`, audited tree clean (empty `git status --porcelain`) before and after — no edits, no commits in the code repo. I searched eleven folders — `00.Core`, `04.TrainingLoop`, `10.LoRA`, `11.DPO`, `14.ContinuousLearning`, `15.OrchestratorModel`, `16.AutonomousLearning`, `18.CapabilityTraining`, `21.AgentInfrastructure`, `26.TrainableRouter`, `44.DesignConversionDomain` — for a fixed marker set, case-insensitive, across every `.cs` source file, excluding `bin`, `obj`, the `.claire`/`.clone` worktree copies, and `Tests\`. A "marker" here is a word the code uses to flag itself as unfinished: `TODO`/`FIXME`/`HACK` (the three conventional tags a developer leaves in a comment to mean "come back and finish this"), `NotImplementedException` (the exception a method throws to say "this is not written yet"), `NotSupportedException` (the exception that says "this operation is deliberately not allowed here"), `placeholder`, `stub` (a stand-in that fills a slot but does no real work), `not wired`/`wire up` (English for "not yet connected"), `decorative`, `temporary`, and the Russian equivalents `заглушка` (stub), `не подключ` (not connected/wired), `времен` (temporary). Every hit is classified into exactly one of: **(a)** real unfinished code a developer must still write; **(b)** an honest fail-closed scaffold — a deliberate locked door that refuses instead of pretending, whose real completion is the deferred training work; **(c)** a stale/false comment. A fourth pile is none of these — false-positive matches where the word appears but confesses nothing (an exception caught in a `catch` filter, validation code that hunts for placeholder text in data and rejects it, a null-object, a named product mode). I count that pile separately so the a/b/c totals stay honest.

**Headline.** Four of the eleven folders contain ZERO marker hits of any kind: `04.TrainingLoop`, `10.LoRA`, `11.DPO`, and `18.CapabilityTraining`. Notably the preference-training folder itself (`11.DPO`) carries no self-declared unfinished spot. No Russian marker (`заглушка`, `не подключ`, `времен`) matched anywhere in the eleven folders. Every genuine confession is concentrated in a handful of comments plus one training-provenance placeholder in the router; the large majority of raw hits are false positives.

**(1) Full inventory — every hit, folder by folder (file:line + plain description + bucket).**
